# Handover

State of OmniControl for the next agent. Rules: `AGENTS.md`. File map: `ARCHITECTURE.md`. This file: what exists and how it is wired.

## What exists

| Page | What it does |
|---|---|
| Displays | Two collapsible groups. **Hardware**: one collapsed inner group per monitor (and the laptop panel) with what the monitor itself does over DDC/CI: brightness, contrast, red/green/blue gain, saturation, sharpness, speaker volume, colour preset, input source, turn screen off; controls the monitor does not report are greyed out with a tooltip. **Software**: digital brightness, contrast and colour temperature per display (gamma ramp), saturation for all displays, a "Restore defaults" pill on the Software header (everything, incl. warm-light schedule and temperature; presets kept) and one per display group (that display only); the "Count settings" text and the Reset everything card are gone. Every Displays card is half width, two per row (see "Displays compact" below). The header line of Hardware shows "N monitors available" beside the title and the Refresh button (F5) at the right end: the master `pill` button, a header tool (`Tag="grouptool"`, an Up/Down stop like the Mouse layer picker). Master button/field classes and the new `pill glyph`, `TextBox.field`, `TextBox.bar`/`ComboBox.bar`, `ComboBox.tool` are listed in a comment in `Theme.axaml`; unverified on screen. |
| Volume | Two tabs (13 Nov, see "Volume page: Mixer and Wiring tabs"). **Mixer**: **Outputs** (master, programs, combined speakers) and **Microphones** (recording devices, combined microphones). Each row: icon, name, a sentence "→ <devices> · <effects>" (read only: the device words open Wiring at that row), pan (master, programs), level, mute, meter, an FX button, × for a remembered program or a combined device; the effects link ("2 effects", "1 effect, off") and FX open the row's FX window. **Wiring**: Sound mode (one global mode, panel with status and setup), the virtual cable chip and **+ Combine devices** on top; tables Source | Plays on | Cable and Source | Records from | Cable (chips with x and "+ add", or one choice where the mode takes one device); combined device cards (members, cable, Edit, ×). Choices the mode can't do are dimmed with the reason and a Switch link. |
| OSD | Custom pop-up for volume/brightness; options; hide Windows' own OSD. Show OSD + Hide Windows' OSD are half-width cards. |
| Mouse and Trackpad | Group "Pointer settings" (last): Windows' own mouse settings changed live, scheme and single pointers, reset and restore (see "Mouse and Trackpad page: Pointer settings"). Group "Mouse Buttons": remap buttons, wheel and tilt like X-Mouse Button Control; group "Trackpad": three/four-finger swipes, pinch in/out and taps on a precision touchpad, same cards and actions, its own layers (see "Mouse and Trackpad page: trackpad gestures" and "Trackpad layers"); while a gesture has an action, Windows' own gesture is always turned off (no switch; the action's "Also let Windows do its own gesture" keeps it) and given back afterwards (see "Trackpad gesture takeover" and "Trackpad takeover: the shell's own copy"). 10 layers (hold a button for layer N, or a hotkey cycles); no profiles (removed at the owner's request, so no per-program mappings), actions = clicks, scroll, media/volume, window, desktop, edit, browser, custom keys, run a program, or any OmniControl hotkey action. Settings in `mouse.json`. Hotkeys: toggle remapping, next mouse layer, next trackpad layer. |
| Gestures | Mouse-wheel gestures: scroll over the taskbar, top/left/right screen edge, a window title bar or the desktop, or scroll while holding Ctrl+Alt, Ctrl+Shift, Alt or Win. Each is a switch plus an action (volume, brightness, contrast, mic level, window opacity, virtual desktop, next/previous track, zoom, sideways scroll, pointer speed). Middle-click gestures: a middle click over the taskbar, a window title bar or the desktop runs any action a mouse button can have. All start off. A last group "Custom rules" holds rules built as a sentence (see "Custom gesture rules"). Edge strip width is a setting; its "Show margins" switch draws the areas on screen while the page is open. Hotkey: toggle gestures. Settings in `gestures.json`; rules in `rules.json`. |
| Actions and hotkeys (a window, not a page) | The sidebar's bottom entry. The Choose-the-action window in manage mode over every master action: shortcut set, Clear all, Restore defaults; search by name or key; keycaps per entry; several keys per action; Run now; Show on its page. See "Actions and hotkeys window" below. |
| App settings | Theme and accent gallery cards, start with Windows, start minimised (half-width pair), "When I close the window" choice (Ask each time / Minimise to tray / Quit; the footer Close does what the X does), plug-in folders (add a folder, one row per folder with Remove, Scan now), About. |
| Other PCs | Basic only (17 Nov; Windows Remote Desktop is for more). Paired PCs on the same network (or at an address on Tailscale / ZeroTier): browser-style PC tabs (`Controls/BrowserTabStrip`, `docs/mockups/other-pcs-browser-tabs.html`) and under them the chosen PC: header line (name, real name, the one probe tag, Connect chip, Unpair), the Tailscale checklist only while a check fails, then its basic set as groups Screens, Sound (with a one-row Now playing line), Power, Smart devices. Its items are also in this PC's hotkeys, mouse, gestures, automation and search under the section "Other PCs" (group = the PC's name, plain names). See "Other PCs (17 Nov)". |

Also: FX chain window (per program/master/input, Reaper-style), Add effect window (built-ins + VST3 and VST2 scan and file load), rubber-band selection, drag-reorder, tray icon with its flyout and menu (see "Tray flyout"), orange multi-size icon (`Assets/OmniControl.ico`), wrap-around Up/Down navigation.

## How it is wired

```
Program.cs -> App.axaml.cs   builds services, ShellViewModel, MainWindow, tray
ShellViewModel               list of PageViewModels (one line each) + search + footer
App.axaml                    one DataTemplate per VM/card type -> its view
Page VM (Pages/<Name>)       owns cards (SliderItem/ChoiceItem/ToggleItem/GalleryItem/...), talks to a service
Service (App/Audio, Osd, Hotkeys, Services)   state, timers, persistence under %APPDATA%\OmniControl
Core (OmniControl.Core)      Windows only: DDC/CI (dxva2), Core Audio COM, WMI. No UI, no settings.
```

Data flow, volume example: `VolumePageViewModel` (40 ms `Timing.MeterFrame` timer) -> `MixerService.Peaks` / `CoreAudio.DevicePeaks` -> `MeterModel.Feed` -> `LevelMeter` repaints on `Changed`. Slider -> `MixerItem.VolumeChanged` -> `MixerService` -> `AudioSession`/`CoreAudio`.

Keyboard: `RowEditing` (inside a card list), `GroupedCards` (pages with collapsible groups), `MainWindow.axaml.cs` (sidebar). All wrap (`Services/Wrap.cs`). A slider's value is typed by a double-click on the value or Ctrl+Enter on the slider (`Pages/NumberText.cs`; Enter, Tab or a click elsewhere applies, Esc cancels). `HalfPanel` lays out half cards in list order.

Persistence (all in `%APPDATA%\OmniControl`): `settings.json` (AppSettings), `mixer.json` (MixerSettings incl. FX chains, VST3 plug-in states (`FxSlot.State`), the capture test result `CaptureSilence`; input chains keyed `input:<endpointId>`), `hotkeys.json`, `plugins.json`, OSD settings, `mouse.json`, `gestures.json`, `rules.json`, `software-display.json` (original gamma ramps), `pointer-settings-original.json` (the user's Windows pointer settings and pointers before OmniControl's first change; written once, kept), `pointer-swap-trial.json` (only while a button swap waits for Keep), `trackpad-settings-original.json` (each Windows touchpad gesture OmniControl turned off, with the value it had before; an entry lives only until the value is given back), `osd-log.txt`, `mouse-log.txt`, `rules-log.txt`, `hang-log.txt` (diagnostics), `windows-osd-hidden.json` (only while Windows' volume pop-up is moved off screen: its handle, process and place), `engine-journal.json` (only while the engine has silenced or routed a program, or a program closed while silenced, until the next start), `effects-setup-log.txt` (every setup step: OmniControl APO install/remove, Equalizer APO and VB-CABLE installers, failures with their detail). Master effects go to `OmniControl.txt` in the user's Equalizer APO config folder (usually `C:\Program Files\EqualizerAPO\config`) plus one marked `Include: OmniControl.txt` line in its `config.txt`; with the optional OmniControl APO the folder is `C:\ProgramData\OmniControl\APO`.

Adding things: **page** = VM + view + one line in `ShellViewModel` + one DataTemplate; **action** = `HotkeyRegistry.Register` once (a level: `HotkeyAction.ForLevel`; an OSD pop-up: `OsdService.Declare`), see "Master lists" in `ARCHITECTURE.md`; **timing value** = `Timing.cs`; **half card** = `Half = true`.

## FX Mix in Equalizer APO, display feed level, Configurator step, Mixer scan off the UI thread (18 Nov 2026, 0.2.0); compiled only (Release into %TEMP%, 0 errors, no new warnings); the 70 % section text was produced by calling `ApoChainWriter.Section` from a scratch program (nothing written to a config folder); nothing run on the PC (rule 7)

- **Folder Mix and effect Mix in Equalizer APO.** Before: `FxFolders.ApoChainFor` gave a folder's Mix to its effects and the writer left every effect below 100 % out (the folder's effects simply stopped). Now `ApoRow` carries the chain as `FxPart`s (`FxFolders.PartsFor`) and `ApoChainWriter.Section(title, parts, device, channels, blends, levels)` wraps a part or an effect below 100 % in a blend (`SectionText.Blend`): `Copy: OCDRY<n>_<c>=<c> ...` keeps each device channel in a new virtual channel (Equalizer APO adds a channel for a Copy target it does not know and never plays it, CopyFilter.cpp / FilterEngine.cpp at bbfcc3e), the effect lines run on the device's channels only (Copy keeps the channel selection), then `Copy: <c>=<1-m>*OCDRY<n>_<c>+<m>*<c> ...` mixes back. Channels are written by number for the device's own channel count (`ApoEndpoint.Channels`, read from the device's `PKEY_AudioEngine_DeviceFormat` in the registry by `ApoStatus`; 2 when unreadable). Blends nest (a folder's around an effect's own); an IR Loader's Mix is multiplied into its slot's Wet (the built-in engine applies both in turn). The built-in engine was already right (`MixOpenStage` / `MixCloseStage`, `WetStage`: dry + (wet - dry) x m). A 70 % folder on a stereo device writes: `# Device folder <name>: mix 70 %, blended with the sound before it`, `Copy: OCDRY1_1=1 OCDRY1_2=2`, the effects' lines, `Copy: 1=0.3*OCDRY1_1+0.7*1 2=0.3*OCDRY1_2+0.7*2`.
- **Old Equalizer APO.** `ApoState.UpstreamVersion` (EqualizerAPO.dll's file version) and `ApoState.BlendsIn(folder)` (OmniControl APO always; Equalizer APO from `ApoIds.BlendVersion` 1.2, or when its version can't be read); `EffectsRules.ApoBlends` / `ApoNoBlend` / `ApoNoBlendReason`. Below 1.2 the writer leaves such effects out and names them as before, the folder's Mix card is greyed at 100 % with the sentence as its tooltip and on the Method card (`FxDeviceSource.MixBlockedOn`, `FxFolderViewModel.RefreshMix`), and the FX curve leaves them out (`ChainResponse.Of(..., apoBlends, ...)`, `FxCatalog.ApoRuns`, `EffectsEngine.ApoBlends`). This PC has 1.4.2.
- **Display feed level.** `DisplayHold` scaled the loopback by 100 / the slider percent; Windows scales the samples by the master level in dB, an audio taper, so at 60 % the feed stayed far too low. Now `CoreAudio.LoopbackLevel(device)` = 10^(dB/20) of the master level (1 when `QueryHardwareSupport` says the device turns its volume down itself, after the loopback), read on a worker after each level change, and the block is multiplied by its inverse (the old percent rule only when it can't be read). No mono sum or pan law is applied (a stereo plug-in gets the stereo block as is; the balance is not compensated). The device is the folder's own: `EffectsEngine.DisplayDeviceOf` now feeds an effect only when it is in a part that runs on the master's APO device (`FxFolders.ChainFor`), so a folder for another device gets no feed. Embedded and popped-out editors both count (`PluginChainHost.OpenEditor`), and the spectrum uses the same `DisplayHold`. The FX window's "Peak" is the drawn response's peak, not a level, so it is unchanged.
- **Configurator.** `EngineInstaller.SetUpEqualizerApoAsync` is THE step behind every "Install Equalizer APO" (Method menu `InstallApoAction`, the Mixer fix link `MasterApoFix`, the FX device folder's device menu through `FxMixerLinks.InstallApo` / `InstallApoTip`, Easy's `EasySetupSteps`): `ApoStatus.UpstreamConfigurator()` (DeviceSelector.exe in `UpstreamInstallFolder`, which now falls back to Program Files\EqualizerAPO when InstallPath is missing or stale) opens it, otherwise the installer or the download page; `SetUpTip` is each button's tooltip. The Configurator run (also "Choose devices") waits up to `CoreTiming.ConfiguratorWaitMs` on a worker for it to close (`HelperProcess.RunElevated(..., waitMs)`), then `EffectsSupportService.Refresh` reads the devices again. User-facing "Device Selector" wording is now "Configurator".
- **Mixer scan.** `MixerService.Scan()` (`MixerService.Scan.cs`) only asks: a worker reads the devices, the sessions as plain `SessionKey`s and the process paths (`Read`), and the UI thread applies it (`Apply`); one read at a time, asks meanwhile become one more read, an older read that ends late is dropped (`Ticket`). The hardware watch reads on its own worker (`ScanForWatch`). The start-up look stays synchronous (`ScanNow`). Windows' session objects can't be shared across apartments (no proxy/stub is registered for `ISimpleAudioVolume`, `IAudioSessionControl2`, `IAudioMeterInformation`, `IChannelAudioVolume`), so the UI keeps making its own (`Regroup`), but only when a session opened, closed, or started or stopped playing; every other scan does no device or session enumeration on the UI thread.
- **Row icons.** No code change in this pass: the working tree already holds the fix from an earlier pass. The cause: the old `Path.glyph` (Stretch Uniform in a fixed 14 x 14 box, StrokeThickness 2) drew the narrow microphone from the box's left edge and too thick, and the letter was `TextBlock.strong` (SemiBold). `RowIcon` now draws `Controls/IconGlyph` (the library's `mic` at `IconGlyphSize` 16 with the library's 2-on-24 stroke, the sidebar's weight) and a normal-weight letter, each centred both ways inside the 24 px `Border.rowicon` (styles in `Styles/ThemeButtons.axaml`). Not checked on screen.
- **Open / not verified:** the dry copy of a blend is not delayed to match a plug-in's latency (Pro-L's look-ahead makes a short comb at mixes between 0 and 100 %; the built-in engine has the same gap); that Equalizer APO 1.4.2 loads the blend as written (read from its source only); that the feed now reads level with the track on the owner's Audient iD4 (if that device reports hardware volume the feed is no longer scaled at all); the scan on a worker under a real device change.

## OSD duration per app or event (18 Nov 2026), built to `docs/mockups/osd-duration.html`; compiled only (Release into a throwaway copy under %TEMP%, 0 errors, no new warnings); `OsdDurationTests` (4 new) and `RestoreDefaultsTests` pass; nothing launched or clicked
- The OSD page's Customize group has a half card "Duration" beside Opacity (0.5-10 s, one decimal, default 1.5 s = the old fixed `Timing.OsdVisible`, so nothing changes until edited) with the master cogwheel "Duration per app or event…", which opens the "OSD duration" window (`Pages/Osd/OsdDurationWindow`, one at a time through `SingleWindow`): "Everything else" (the same setting as the card), then the exceptions, first match wins (grip drag, the `ChipMenu` "App: ..." / "Event: ..." menu, typeable seconds, remove cross), "+ add exception" (adds the first event without one; the chip then picks), Close. Live, no Apply. Stored in `osd.json` (`OsdSettings.Duration`, `Durations`); edited only through `Osd/OsdDurations`; THE resolver is `Osd/OsdDuration.For` (pure).
- Which program caused a pop-up: Play / pause and Track and artist = the media session's program (`MediaChange.Program`; a Store app's session has no program file, so unknown); Volume and Microphone changed outside OmniControl (keys, another app) = the program in front (`ProgramIdentity.Foreground`). Everything else (brightness, USB, smart devices, OmniControl's own hotkey steps, screenshots) has no known program, so only Event exceptions apply to it.
- Shared pieces extended: `SliderItem.Decimals` (the value counts in tenths, shown and typed as the real number through `SliderField.Decimals`) and `SliderItem.Cog` / `CogTip` (the master `Button.pill.cog` after the value, full and half layouts).
- Mouse first (rule 5): the exception chip's menu is mouse first; the chip, seconds and cross are reachable by Tab. The app list in the chip's menu is the app lists' programs plus the programs running when the window opened (no "Browse…"). Restore defaults resets the duration and keeps the exceptions (like the named places).

## Screen power shows the live state (18 Nov 2026); compiled only, nothing run (rule 7), no screen switched off
- Causes: the state read only `_screenOn` (last DDC read or last command sent from here), so a whole-system "screens off" (SC_MONITORPOWER, invisible to DDC) never changed it, nothing called `StateRegistry.Notify` for it, and standby (VCP 0xD6 = 2, 3) counted as on. Windows' display-state notice was already registered in `HardwareSignals` (GUID_CONSOLE_DISPLAY_STATE and GUID_MONITOR_POWER_ON) but only told "off" and "on" to the wake detector.
- Fix: `HardwareSignals` also tells dimmed (`PowerEventKind.DisplayDimmed`); `HardwareWatches.Screens` (`ScreenLight`) + `ScreensChanged` keep the last word (no second listener); `Pages/Displays/SystemScreens` is what every `HardwareDisplayItem` asks (`ScreenOn` is false while Windows says off; `VcpCode.Power.IsOff`); `ScreensChanged()` re-reads 0xD6 (`IHardwareAccess.ReadRaw`) when screens are on; the page notifies each power state, which the shared state push already sends to paired PCs, and the mirror's group and per-monitor switches update through `PcMirror.Update`. `ScreenPowerSwitch.All(..., knownOff)`: the group switch shows off when Windows (local) or every listed screen (mirror) says off.
- Per monitor: a DDC off/on touches only that monitor; while Windows has all screens off a monitor cannot answer DDC, so "on" sends SC_MONITORPOWER -1 (`PowerCommands.TurnOnDisplays`), which wakes every screen (the others are then read again). Unverified on hardware: whether every monitor/driver sends the display-state notice for SC_MONITORPOWER.

## Route changes on both PCs and one-pill wake control (18 Nov 2026, 0.1.9); compiled only, nothing run (rule 7), no second PC used
- Wake control (`Pages/OtherPcs/WakeSplit`): awake = ONE `Button.pill` (dot, state words, small muted cog inside, a click anywhere opens Set up wake); the split `Wake | cog` (`splitleft`/`splitright`) shows only while `ShowsWake`. No new master style needed.
- Route sync (`OtherPcs/OtherPcsService.RouteSync.cs`, message `route` in `PcProtocol`): picking a route sends it over the current link first, then switches here; the other PC saves the same route (log: "changed on <name>"). No link: `PairedPc.PendingRoute` is kept and sent when a link comes up (`Attach`). A link the other PC opened over Tailscale is now accepted and this PC adopts Tailscale (unless its own change is still pending, or this PC dialled it). The Address route is only followed when an address is stored here. Notes ("Switched on both PCs." / "<PC> will switch when it next connects.") are appended to the Connect chip's tooltip (`PcItem.ConnectTip`). Still true: an address hello (`ts-address`) never changes the route. Open: the tooltip is read when the chip refreshes, not live while open; untested on two real PCs.

## Windows (Photos, Explorer) not coming up while OmniControl runs; hang sweep (18 Nov 2026, 0.2.0); compiled only (Release into %TEMP%, 0 errors, no new warnings; Autoscroll, PowerJournal and RestoreDefaults tests pass), nothing run (rule 7)
- **Cause (from this PC's osd-log.txt):** at every start `WindowsOsd` sent a mute pair to make Windows' pop-up appear, its own `Trigger()` landed after the tick's clock was read (the "-15 ms after a change"), and every candidate already on screen counted as "just opened", so it minimised BOTH Explorer XAML-island windows, one of them a 1920x1200 full-screen Explorer host that is not the volume pop-up. Apps' own windows (Photos is WinUI, ApplicationFrameWindow / CoreWindow for UWP) were never matched by class; the damage was to Explorer's own host windows.
- **Hider rewritten (`Osd/WindowsOsd`, `Core/Engine/WindowsOsdWindows`):** a candidate must be class XamlExplorerHostIslandWindow, untitled, with the bridge and InputSite children, top-level, not WS_EX_APPWINDOW, owned by explorer.exe or ShellExperienceHost.exe (`WindowQuery.IsShellProgram`), and have the pop-up's size and place (`HasFlyoutShape`: at most 40 % x 25 % of its monitor, at least 20 px, centred within 10 %, within 30 % of the bottom or top edge; `CoreTiming.WindowsOsd*`). It is learned only when seen OPENING (a window on screen when hiding starts never counts) 0 to 1000 ms AFTER an outside change (the change is read before the clock, so no negative gap), with the pointer off the taskbar. Then only that ONE handle (and its process id) is touched. No mute pair or any other probing, ever. Hiding moves it off screen (`SetWindowPos` -32000, async, no size, z-order, activation or show-state change); never minimise. Where it was is saved in `windows-osd-hidden.json` (`DataStores.WindowsOsdHidden`) and put back exactly on stop, quit or the next start, only if that handle is still the same pop-up of the same process and still parked. Kill switch: more than `Timing.WindowsOsdMaxWindows` (3) different windows learned in a run stops hiding for the run (logged once). Every step is in osd-log.txt.
- **Left as found (rule 5 of the brief):** windows minimised by EARLIER versions are no longer un-minimised at start (the old "putting back" restored random windows). On a PC that ran an older version, restart Explorer (or sign out) once to clear them.
- **Unverified:** whether Explorer re-places the pop-up itself on each showing (then it flashes for one 60 ms poll before being parked again, as the minimise did) or keeps it parked; whether the pop-up's real size and place pass `HasFlyoutShape` on every scaling and taskbar position (if not it is simply not hidden; the log says "not the pop-up's size or place"); if the volume notice reaches OmniControl after Explorer shows the pop-up, that showing is not hidden (strict 0 ms rule) and the pop-up is let go until the next one.
- **power-journal.json save race:** several listeners save at once (a wake is told by three channels) and all used the one `power-journal.json.tmp`. `PowerJournal.Save` is now one writer at a time (`_saveGate`, taken before `_gate`), and `AppDataStore.SaveText` uses a unique temp name per call (deleted when the save fails), so no two saves of any store share a temp file.
- **Hang sweep, fixed:** (1) `AutoscrollWatcher` (on by default via Block autoscroll) ran a UI Automation lookup into the app under the pointer, Explorer included, through `Task.Run` every 60 ms of movement with an 80 ms give-up, so lookups piled up behind a busy app, and created a new automation object per call without releasing elements. Now one lookup at a time on the watcher's own thread, one automation object per worker (`UiaElements` is an instance, elements and walker released at once), Windows' shell and OmniControl itself are never asked, and a window whose answer took over `CoreTiming.AutoscrollSlowMs` (200) is left alone for `AutoscrollBackoffMs` (5 s). `MiddleClickPolicy.Decide` is synchronous (the timed version and `AutoscrollLookupMs` are gone). (2) `ShellTouchpadSettings` (COM into explorer.exe for the trackpad takeover) runs each call on its own thread, waited at most `CoreTiming.ShellCallTimeoutMs` (1.5 s); while a late call is out no new one is made; a call over 200 ms is logged to the trackpad log, naming it (it is now an instance owned by `TouchpadSettingsStore`). (3) `WinKeyHook` sent its Start-menu-suppressing tap from inside the keyboard hook callback (input sent there waits on the same hook); it is sent from a worker. (4) `MouseHook.Reinstalled`: after Windows dropped and OmniControl reinstalled the mouse hook, `MouseService` lets go of every held input (keys, buttons, held layers, movement as scroll), so a missed release cannot leave Win or a button down; a button still physically held then ends early. (5) `WindowCommands` (minimise, maximise, restore, topmost) and `WindowDrag` use `ShowWindowAsync` / `SWP_ASYNCWINDOWPOS`, so a hung app never stalls the mouse queue. (6) `HardwareSignals.Start` waits at most `CoreTiming.HardwareSignalsStartWaitMs` for its window and for SystemEvents (was unbounded on the UI thread). (7) `ApoApplier` regsvr32 waits at most `ElevatedHelperTimeoutMs`, then kills it.
- **UI watchdog (`Services/UiWatchdog`, started in `App`):** a heartbeat every 500 ms; when the UI thread has not answered for 2 s, every managed thread's stack (from a ClrMD snapshot of this process, package Microsoft.Diagnostics.Runtime 3.1.512801, added for this) goes once per stall to hang-log.txt, at most 5 per run (`Timing.UiHeartbeatEvery`, `UiStuckAfter`, `UiStuckLogsPerRun`, `UiStuckFramesPerThread`). Unverified that the in-process snapshot works on the owner's PC; if refused the stall is still logged, without stacks.
- **Checked and safe:** no plain SendMessage, SHAppBarMessage or Shell_NotifyIcon calls (the tray icon is Avalonia's, created once); `AttachThreadInput` only in `OverlayTopmost`, always detached in `finally`; no SetWinEventHook or WH_SHELL hook; no IVirtualDesktopManager, ITaskbarList or IApplicationActivationManager; no ClipCursor, BlockInput, keybd_event or WM_SETREDRAW; no STA threads created; the single-instance wait has a timeout; audio callbacks only queue; DDC/CI and WMI run on workers; every other `WaitForExit` has a timeout; network code is async with timeouts.
- **Open (reported, not changed):** `HotkeyRegistry.RunHeld` waits up to 150 ms on the UI thread from the mouse hook thread (repeated clicking only); `PointerArea` still sends `WM_NCHITTEST` (50 ms, SMTO_ABORTIFHUNG) from the hook for title-bar gestures; `CompiledRule` program conditions can read a process name on the hook thread on a cache miss; the "leaving" notice to other PCs and the shutdown save run on the power-broadcast thread (bounded at 1.5 s by design, so the notice leaves before sleep); `MixerService.Scan` (Core Audio enumeration) runs on the UI thread every 2 s (audio agent's area); `WindowCommands.AdjustOpacity` sets WS_EX_LAYERED on another program's window synchronously and never takes it off; `ClipboardText` can wait on a hung clipboard owner; `ShellViewModel.Dispose` disposes the mouse hook after the trackpad's restore; the margin overlay asks the active window `WM_NCHITTEST` many times per refresh while "Show margins" is on.

## Taskbar breaking while OmniControl runs, the window opening by itself (18 Nov 2026, 0.1.9; the OSD part is superseded by the section above); reasoned from code and this PC's logs (the bug was seen on the owner's other PC); compiled only (Release into %TEMP%, 0 errors, no new warnings), nothing run (rule 7)
- **Main cause (proven from osd-log.txt):** with "Hide Windows' own OSD" on, `Osd/WindowsOsd` minimised EVERY Explorer window of class XamlExplorerHostIslandWindow with the XAML-island children, every 60 ms. Explorer uses that window for its other pop-ups too, the taskbar's thumbnail previews among them: the log shows dozens of different handles minimised, two at once, and a new one each second while hovering. A minimised thumbnail pop-up never opens again (clicking a grouped taskbar button showed nothing, its windows looked "buried"), and `ShowWindow(SW_MINIMIZE)` also activates the next window, taking the foreground from the window the taskbar had just brought forward. Quitting ran `Restore()` (`SW_RESTORE`), which brought the minimised pop-ups back at once: the "pending thumbnail appears by itself". It came back after a restart as soon as Explorer created a new pop-up.
- **Fix:** the Windows calls are in `Core/Engine/WindowsOsdWindows` (find candidates, `ShowWindowAsync` with SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE / SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE: never activates, never waits on Explorer; the mute pair goes through `InputSender`). `WindowsOsd` takes a candidate for the volume pop-up only when it opens within `Timing.WindowsOsdMatch` (1 s) of an outside volume, brightness, microphone or media change (`WindowsOsd.Trigger`, called by `OsdService`; OmniControl's own changes do not count, as Windows shows no pop-up for them) and the pointer is not on the taskbar or an Explorer pop-up; a taken window that Windows shows again with no such change near is let go. While the pop-up is unknown it is shown once by a mute pair (at most 5 tries, 2 s apart, as before). Turning hiding on puts back any pop-up an earlier run left minimised (Explorer never minimises them itself), without activating it.
- **Limits (best-effort, honest):** a volume key that changes nothing (already at 100) or a pop-up that is only re-filled, never shown again, is not caught; the pop-up then shows that once. If Windows' pop-up is never told apart (5 tries) it is left alone and osd-log.txt says so. Each window minimised or let go is logged with its handle, place and size.
- **Other causes fixed:** (1) `OverlayTopmost.TakeForeground` (screenshot and point-picker overlays) called `AllowSetForegroundWindow(ASFW_ANY)`, letting any program take the foreground; removed. (2) `HardwareSignals` subscribed to .NET's SystemEvents from the UI thread, which is STA, so SystemEvents put its broadcast window on OmniControl's UI thread (every system-wide broadcast then waited for the UI thread); it now subscribes from a short MTA thread, so SystemEvents has its own. (3) The mouse hook asked the window under the pointer `WM_NCHITTEST` (up to 50 ms) for every wheel step and middle press when any area gesture was on; it now asks only when a title-bar gesture is on (`PointerArea.At(..., titleBar)`).
- **Single instance:** `Services/SingleInstance` (named mutex `Local\OmniControl.Running`, event `Local\OmniControl.Open`). `Program.Main`: a start while a copy runs sets the event (the copy opens its window) and ends before anything is read or staged; a start-up entry start (`--background`) ends quietly; a restart (`--after`) waits `Timing.RestartWait` for the copy before it; the "run as administrator" relaunch happens before the claim. A copy run as administrator cannot be asked by one that is not (Windows' object security): that start ends and says so in errors-log.txt. `App` takes the instance in its constructor and listens once the window exists.
- **Diagnostics for the owner to send (errors-log.txt):** "Main window opened by <source>" whenever a hidden or minimised main window is opened (sources: tray double-click, the quick panel's Open OmniControl, the actions Open / Show or hide OmniControl with who ran them (`RunChain.Describe`: a key, button, tray menu or Run now, or the rule chain and whether a person or a trigger started it), a page opened from the tray or an action, another start); "Main window opened, not by any of OmniControl's open requests" if anything else shows it. "Mouse hook: a mouse hook call took N ms (message, the slow handler)" and "Mouse hook: Windows had dropped the mouse hook ... put back", each at most once a minute (`CoreTiming.HookReportGapMs`) with a count of the ones in between.
- **Likely, not proven, for the window opening by itself:** the default key of "Show or hide OmniControl" is Alt+X, a common key inside other programs (registered system-wide, so pressing it in another app opens OmniControl); the new log line will say "a key, mouse button, tray menu or Run now" if so. Two copies running (now impossible). Not changed: the default key (owner's choice).
- **Checked and not a cause:** the keyboard hooks (`WinKeyHook` only classifies and posts; `KeyLearner` is short-lived), the touchpad watcher (raw input, its own thread), the OSD window (`ShowActivated=false`, click-through, parked off screen), the tray flyout and other topmost windows (shown only while in use), the screenshot overlay's keep-on-top timer (stopped on close), the hardware listener's window (own thread). `HotkeyRegistry.RunHeld` still waits up to `Timing.GateAskWait` (150 ms) on the UI thread from the hook thread for "while held" OmniControl actions; now visible in the hook log line if it ever bites.
- **Not verified:** nothing was run; the fix is reasoned from code and this PC's osd-log (many different windows minimised). Unverified on the other PC: that Windows' volume pop-up is still hidden there, that thumbnails and taskbar switching stay normal, and the single-instance hand-off (including the run-as-administrator case).

## Import settings from another PC (18 Nov 2026, 0.1.9); built to `docs/mockups/import-settings-from-pc.html` (incl. the UPDATED grouped list, progress and result); built and packaged only, no second PC was used and none of the owner's settings were imported (rule 7)
- **Where.** `App/SettingsImport/`: `ImportAreas` (the areas, made from `DataStores`: each saved store has `Import` = an `ImportPlace` (group from `ImportGroups`, area name, muted note) or `MachineSpecific` = never sent: Other PCs list, mic-mute key, plug-in paths), `SettingsShare` (this PC as sender: `Offer` = areas with counts, `Pack(area)` = the files, approvals removed, only `ImportAreas` stores so a machine-specific one cannot leave), `SettingsImporter` (receiver), `ImportHooks`, `ImportFromPcService` (opens the one window), `ImportFromPcViewModel` + `ImportItems` + `ImportFromPcWindow`. Transfer: `OtherPcs/OtherPcsService.Import.cs` (messages `import-list`, `import-get`, `import-answer` over the existing encrypted link; one request per area; limited by `Timing.PcImport*`; the sender reports one line when a PC reads its areas). Entry points: the PC tab's right-click row (only while online, thin line above Unpair) and an App settings "Import from another PC" card (`MenuChoiceItem`, the online PCs) next to Backup and restore, through `BackupService.OnlinePcs/ImportFromPc`.
- **Applying (live, no restart).** Automatic backup first (`BackupFile.Write` to `import-backup.omnibackup`, replaced by the next import, removed by a wipe); if it cannot be saved nothing is imported. Per area: only that area's own files are taken, each must be json, the properties a file may not set (`NotImported`) keep this PC's value, a store the sender lacks is left alone. Then per store, in the master list's order: atomic `AppDataStore.SaveText`, read back equal, and the owner's reload (`ImportHooks.Reload`, registered once per store in `Shell/ShellViewModel.Reloads.cs`). A reload reads the file, REPLACES what the owner holds in memory (so what it saves at exit is the imported value: this is the guard against the exit-save race), applies it and tells its pages. A failed area is put back alone (the previous text rewritten, every store reloaded again). Undo import restores the backup the same way. "checked" means: saved, read back equal, and every owner's reload returned true. Not done: reading the file again AFTER the reload and comparing it, because several owners legitimately rewrite their file when they apply (mouse, gestures, rules, mixer through its normal save paths), so such a compare would fail for a good import. There is no staging, no "saved · restart to finish" and no "Restart now" any more (`PendingRestore.StageChosen` and `RestoreMode.Chosen` are removed); an area whose store has no reload hook is refused with one sentence (none is left today).
- **What each owner does on reload.** Hotkeys: `HotkeyRegistry.ReloadFromDisk` (bindings in force and rebinding, no save, no `BindingsSaved`); shortcut sets: `HotkeySets.ReloadFromDisk` through `ActionsWindowService.ReloadShortcutSets`; Mouse, Gestures, Gesture rules: their existing replace paths (`ReplaceSettings`, `Replace`, `ReplaceAll`, which save and apply); OSD: settings copied in, Windows' OSD hide, screens, `SettingsChanged`, `Reloaded` (the OSD page's toggles and sliders show the new values); Mixer: `MixerService.ReloadFromDisk` (new programs added, volume, balance and mute set on the running program, then the effects, routes, combined devices and cables through `AudioDefaults.Apply`, the same step a Volume preset takes; `IsReloading` keeps the Volume presets from capturing the half-way setup); Volume presets, effect presets: lists replaced in place and listeners told; Displays: `DisplaySettingsStore.ReloadFromDisk` (the preset lists keep their identity) + warm-light schedule applied; Smart devices: `DeviceService.ReloadFromDisk` (actions and states unregistered and registered again, `ListChanged`); Automation: `AutomationService.ReloadFromDisk` (rule, custom and script actions registered again, triggers synced, `Reloaded` makes the page list them again); App lists, App actions, Power plan actions, Tray, Icons: replaced and their lists rebuilt; Screenshots: `WindowsKeysSwitch.ReloadFromDisk` (this PC's Windows-keys switch, its backup and recording way are `NotImported`); App settings: `AppSettingsPageViewModel.ReloadFromDisk` (theme, accent, cards, hardware check) + hotkeys switch and Win+A; Recent actions: read from the file at every use; Confirmations: `ConfirmationMemory.Reload`. Mixer's `BusCable`, `RealMasterDevice` and `CaptureSilence` are `NotImported` (this PC's own).
- **Open items.** (1) A page that builds its cards once and has no refresh on show or on its service's events shows the old values until it is reopened or OmniControl restarts, and an edit there would save over the imported value: the OSD and Automation pages were given a refresh; Displays, Mouse, Gestures, Volume and Devices already refresh on show or on their service's events; the Volume page's and Gestures' open cards, the Tray flyout and App settings' plug-in folder rows were not checked one by one. (2) Reloading the mixer cannot move a program that is playing to a device this PC lacks; such a "plays on" is kept and marked not found by the Volume page as before. (3) Smart devices show "N need passwords" (`DeviceService.PasswordsNeededIn`, registered with `OnFact`); it counts the imported devices whose template signs in, using this PC's templates.
- **Differences from the mock-up.** No "Keyboard remapping" area: the app has no such store, so the master list has none. Counts show only for list stores (`Mergeable`; "N entries", not "N gestures"/"N keys"). The App settings chip reads "Choose a PC" (the mock-up shows a PC name) because picking a PC starts the window. The loading spinner is the Other PCs tile mark. New shared pieces: `Border.tickbox.mixed`, `TextBlock.goodtext` / `.badtext`, `Path.glyph.failmark` (`Styles/Menus.axaml`, `Tables.axaml`).
- **Not verified.** The window was never opened (XAML compiled only), nothing was sent between two PCs, every owner's live reload (compiled only, none run: the owner's real settings were not touched), Undo, the Stop button, the window at the narrowest width and in both themes, and "Devices that aren't on this PC are kept and marked not found" (what happens to a mixer device that is missing here is the Volume page's existing behaviour, not checked). The sender reads the saved files, so a setting not yet saved to disk is not sent.

## Wake and log-in rules that need the network (18 Nov) - compiled into a throwaway folder under %TEMP% (deleted, 0 errors, no new warnings); `PowerJournalTests` (16 new) plus `HardwareRetryTests`, `AutomationTests`, `SetToStateTests`, `DevicesTests`, `AutoCheckTests`, `MasterMethodTests`, `DataStoreBackupTests`, `StateTriggerTests` run (118 passed); nothing launched, the PC was never put to sleep, locked or signed out, no device switched
- **The bug:** "Turn Speakers On" (At log in OR when the PC wakes, do Orbit 11: On) did nothing after a wake. Likely cause, from code (no `automation-log.txt` and no such rule exist in this PC's `%APPDATA%\OmniControl`, so the failing run could not be read): (1) the wake was seen only as a 30 s jump between two 5 s looks of a UI-thread timer, which fires within seconds of the resume, before Wi-Fi is back, and Modern Standby can let that timer run in short bursts so no jump is ever seen; (2) a smart-device step was fire-and-forget: `RuleRunner` called the action and moved on, the device's own failure ("not reachable") went only to the status line and the device row, so the rule showed "Ran" and nothing tried again. "Needs Start with Windows" was a real dependency ("At log in" fires only when the start-up entry started OmniControl) but said only where to fix it.
- **Power journal** (`Services/PowerJournal.cs`, the one listener of `HardwareWatches.Power`; no second Windows listener): `Core/Engine/HardwareSignals.cs` now also tells every power and session event with the channel that heard it (`PowerSignal`, `PowerChannel`): hidden window = WM_POWERBROADCAST PBT_APMSUSPEND / RESUMEAUTOMATIC / RESUMESUSPEND, PBT_POWERSETTINGCHANGE for GUID_CONSOLE_DISPLAY_STATE and GUID_MONITOR_POWER_ON (Modern Standby display off/on; `Core/Native/PowerNative.cs`), WM_WTSSESSION_CHANGE lock 7 / unlock 8 / logon 5 / logoff 6, WM_ENDSESSION (shut down or log off); SystemEvents = PowerModeChanged suspend/resume, SessionSwitch lock/unlock/logon/logoff, SessionEnded; third listener = the clock check `Core/Engine/ClockGap.cs` (every `Timing.PowerClockTick` 2 s: `Environment.TickCount64`, which counts sleep and ignores clock changes, against the unbiased interrupt time, which stops in sleep; a sample `Timing.PowerClockGap` 20 s late = "slept" or "stopped (Modern Standby)"). The brief asked for wall clock vs a monotonic tick; TickCount64 is used instead of the wall clock because a time sync moves the wall clock and would fake a wake. Entries go to `power-journal.json` (`DataStores.PowerJournal`, new `StoreKind.Record`: wiped, never exported; last 300 kept; the same kind within `Timing.PowerEventSame` 5 s is one entry listing its channels) and every event and decision is a line in `power-log.txt`.
- **Wake decision** (`Core/Engine/WakeDetector.cs`, pure): evidence = a resume broadcast (Window, SystemEvents), the display back on after at least `Timing.StandbyDisplayOffMin` 30 s off, a clock gap. Confirmed at once by two different channels; with one channel after `Timing.WakeConfirmWindow` 20 s it is confirmed only if it is a resume broadcast after a seen suspend (Windows' own pair), else rejected (a screen time-out, a lone display-on). After a confirmed wake more evidence is a duplicate until a suspend or display-off, or `Timing.WakeQuiet` 2 min. `PowerJournal.Woke` fires once per confirmed wake; `AutomationService` adds the Resume event for the next look (the old 30 s look-gap check and `Timing.AutomationResumeGap` are removed). Pending start/wake events are dropped while rules are paused (not run hours later).
- **Start-up look:** the journal's last entry against Windows' start time (restart while not running; a last Suspend with no restart = woke while not running) and the System event log (`Core/Engine/PowerEventLog.cs`, wevtapi through `Core/Native/EventLogNative.cs`: Kernel-Power 42 (TargetState 5 = hibernate) and 107, Power-Troubleshooter 1 with its wake source) since the last read; logged only, no rule runs for the past. The event log is read again after each confirmed wake (labels a hibernate).
- **At log in once:** `PowerJournal.TakeLogin` (through `IPowerEvents`) fires only for a start by the start-up entry and only once per sign-in (Windows' start time within `Timing.LoginSameBoot` 2 min and the same session id, saved in the journal).
- **Readiness and retries:** a master action that works over the network says so on its entry, `HotkeyAction.Reach` (`Hotkeys/ActionReach.cs`: target name, needs network, `Probe`, `OutcomeSince`; the one edit to `HotkeyAction`). Smart devices set it on every On/Off/Toggle and "via" action (`Devices/DeviceService.Reach.cs`): probe = not busy, then a Scripts method's state read on the UI thread (a simulation-only device is never woken to look; the network check is all); outcome = the command's end recorded by `DeviceRuntime.Commanded` (every On/Off/Toggle run, `DeviceService.RunAsync` wraps `RunCommandAsync`), or, when nothing ran for `Timing.StepOutcomeIdle` 10 s (the run path skipped it as already there, or a block rule stopped it), done if the device is in the wanted state. `RuleRunner` (with a `RunReport`, given only by the automations; gesture rules unchanged) runs such a step through the normal run path, then: when the rule was started by a wake, log-in or start, waits until `Core/Engine/NetworkState.IsUp` (an adapter up with a gateway) and the probe answers (`Rules/ReadyWait.UntilAsync`, `Timing.ReadyBackoff` 2-3-5-8-13-20 s, `Timing.ReadyTimeout` 3 min), then runs it, follows it to its end and tries a failure again (`ReadyWait.RetryAsync`, `Timing.StepTriesMax` 3, `Timing.StepRetryBackoff` 5-15-30 s; a retry first re-probes; a run the user stopped is not retried). Each check and try is a line in `automation-log.txt`.
- **Tags:** while waiting "Waiting for Orbit 11"; after "Ran Wed 01:36 after 2 tries"; on failure "Failed: Orbit 11 not reachable" (red; the tip has the full reason and time) instead of the Ran tag. "Needs Start with Windows" stays (it is a real dependency) and now has one `Button.link` under the tags, "Turn on Start with Windows" (`AutomationService.TurnOnStartup` = `StartupRegistration.Set(true)`, as App settings' switch; also clears a Task Manager "disabled").
- **Unverified:** everything on a real sleep, hibernate, Modern Standby, lock, log-in and shut down (agents may not drive the PC); that the hidden window gets PBT_POWERSETTINGCHANGE and WM_ENDSESSION on this PC; that GetTickCount64 counts sleep and the unbiased time stops in it on this PC (if both run in Modern Standby, the clock check still sees the frozen process); that this PC's System log has 42/107/1 and wevtapi reads it without admin; `NetworkState` with a VPN or Tailscale as the only adapter with a gateway; the outcome wait when a block rule stops a device step (it reports "was not switched" after 10 s); the new link's look at the narrowest window. `power-log.txt` and `automation-log.txt` show what happened after the next real wake.

## Set to state (18 Nov) - compiled into a throwaway folder; `SetToStateTests` (7) pass; nothing launched, no device touched
- **One rule, one place:** `HotkeyAction.StateId` links an action to an on/off `StateEntry`; in `HotkeyRegistry.RunNow` (after the gate) `SkipsOrWaitsForState` reads it through `IStateReader` (`StateRegistry`, set as `HotkeyRegistry.StateReader` in `ShellViewModel`) and `StateTarget.Decide` for family role On/Off: already there = nothing runs, the status line says "X is already on/off"; unknown = `StateRegistry.Refresh` (the entry's `Recheck`) once, then decide; still unknown = runs and says it was sent anyway. Toggle and actions without `StateId` are unchanged.
- **Families covered:** every smart device's On/Off (`devices.<id>.on/off`) and each method's "On/Off via <method>" (all use the device's one power state `devices.<id>.power`).
- **Families added (follow-up):** `Hotkeys/OnOffFamily.cs` makes the On and Off siblings (`<toggle id>.on` / `.off`) of an existing Toggle action, which keeps its id; the owner marks the toggle `Family`/`FamilyRole = Toggle` and registers `Siblings(...)`. Used by: Mute, Microphone mute, Master FX (`VolumePageViewModel.Hotkeys/States`), Keep awake (`KeepAwakeEntries`), Hotkeys (`HotkeysSwitch`), Warm light at night (`WarmLightItem`, `DisplayActions.Once` takes `family`/`familyRole`), App autoscroll block and Pause in full-screen games (`MousePageViewModel`). For the two mutes "On" means muted.
- **Open:** not families (no On/Off action to link): per-screen power (only "Turn screen off" / "Turn off displays" exist, device-scoped; left alone because the Displays hardware work is in progress), mouse and trackpad layers (a choice, not on/off), "Toggle gestures" and "Toggle gesture margins" (no state registered), microphone FX, grayscale and invert (no state), repeated clicking (state exists, action starts a run). Giving those a family is a separate job. A device's last known state can be stale (switched by hand, Simulation cannot read it): Check now or Auto-check refreshes it; a stale "already off" skips On.

## Recording on any PC: chain of ways, watchdog, recording log (18 Nov) - compiled into a throwaway folder under %TEMP% (deleted); recording tests run on this PC (AMD Radeon 860M, one 1920x1200 monitor; temp files only; 111 passed: `RecordingChainTests` (15 new), `RecordingFileTests`, `RecordingAbortTests`, `RecordingCheckTests`, `VideoRecordingTests`, `ScreenshotTests`); the app was not launched, nothing clicked
- **Owner's failing PC (RX 9070 + AMD iGPU, 4K 140 Hz):** Windows Error Reporting showed the UI thread hung about 40 s into a recording whose MP4 stayed at 80 bytes (no frame written). Not reproduced here (this PC's hardware encoder works). What changed so it cannot happen again: (1) nothing of the recorder runs on the UI thread: `ScreenRecorder`'s methods only set flags or start tasks; the sound captures open on the watchdog thread and the bar's sound switches use `SetSystemSoundAsync` / `SetMicrophoneAsync` (before, WASAPI was opened on the UI thread's STA); every recorder thread is explicitly MTA. (2) A watchdog (`ScreenRecorder`, its own thread) runs a chain of ways, each a `RecordingAttempt` on its own thread and part file (`<file>.partN`, moved to the real name only when complete, so a stuck way never leaves a broken MP4 under the real name): `GraphicsCard` (Desktop Duplication + hardware encoder, device manager on the device of the adapter that drives the monitor) -> `OtherEncoder` (GDI copy + hardware encoder: a device on another adapter that has a hardware encoder for the format, per MFTEnum2, else Windows picks) -> `Software` (GDI + Microsoft's H.264 encoder; HEVC falls back to H.264 with a note). A way that does not open within `CoreTiming.RecordOpenWaitMs` (6 s), encodes no frame within `RecordFirstFrameWaitMs` (4 s of recording time, from the Sink Writer's statistics), has one call (frame write, sound write, counts) not returning within `RecordStuckMs` (5 s), or throws, is abandoned and the next starts (the status line says so; the bar's timer restarts with it). A Stop whose Finalize takes over `RecordFinishWaitMs` (10 s) ends as Failed (file lost, said with the log path and Copy details) and the next way is remembered. Disk or folder errors end at once (no other way would help). All ways failing: one sentence, "no way of recording worked on this PC". (3) The way that worked is remembered in `screenshots.json` (`ScreenshotSettings.RecordingRoute`, a `RecordingRoute` = machine key + step; the key is every real adapter's vendor:device:driver version, `GraphicsAdapters.MachineKey`), so the next recording starts there; a new driver or adapter tries the graphics card again. The recorder decides it (`RecordingResult.Route`), `ScreenshotService.RememberRoute` stores it. (4) MP4 is held to `VideoMath.Mp4MaxFrameRate` (60): H.264 level 5.2 holds 4K only to 60 fps; sizes were already even.
- **Async encoders:** the Sink Writer owns the encoder (`MF_READWRITE_ENABLE_HARDWARE_TRANSFORMS`, `MF_SINK_WRITER_D3D_MANAGER`) and drives async hardware MFTs itself; OmniControl never calls an MFT. Here the AMD encoder reports async and records 4K60 from memory. Same-adapter check: `EncoderFacts` reads the chosen encoder back (IMFSinkWriterEx); if it names an adapter LUID other than the screen copy's, the GraphicsCard way is refused. AMD's encoder gives neither its LUID nor its name on the transform ("(name not given)", "adapter LUID not given" in the log), so on AMD a cross-adapter problem is caught by the watchdog (no frame, stuck call), not by that check. At 128x96 the AMD encoder is refused by Windows and the Sink Writer uses the software encoder inside the GraphicsCard way (logged as "software, sync").
- **Recording log (on for every recording):** `Core/Engine/Screenshot/RecordingLog` writes "Recording +s.sss [thread] ..." lines into the app log (`errors-log.txt` = `Guard.LogPath`, %APPDATA%\OmniControl\errors-log.txt): the request (size, fps asked and used, encoder setting, sound), every adapter (name, vendor, device, LUID, driver, monitors), the hardware encoders per adapter, which adapter drives the monitor, the duplication's size and refresh rate, each opening step before and after with its time (Media Foundation start, device manager, Sink Writer create, video stream, video input = where the encoder is chosen, sample pool, sound stream, begin writing), the encoder and its formats, the first frame write, sound write and counts, the first encoded frame, counts every `RecordLogFramesMs` (handed, skipped, received, encoded, written), Stop, Finalize, close, screen copy release, sound stop, move, and any HRESULT. A call that never returns shows as the last "..." line. A failed recording shows "Recording failed" with the log path and Copy details (that recording's lines).
- **For the owner to reproduce:** record an area on the 4K monitor with the hardware encoder on, Stop after about 10 s; whatever happens (a fallback note, the failure dialog), press Copy details in the dialog or send the "Recording +" lines of %APPDATA%\OmniControl\errors-log.txt. A remembered fallback is tried again only after a driver or adapter change; there is no button for it (not asked for).
- **Tests (`RecordingChainTests`; internal `RecordingFaults` / `RecordingWaits`, which the app never makes):** each fault on the GraphicsCard way (fail, hang while opening, hang on the first frame write, no frame encoded) moves on to OtherEncoder and remembers it; both hardware ways failing ends in Software; all failing throws `RecordingFailedException` with details and leaves no file; a hanging Finalize ends as Failed and remembers the next way; a remembered way is used on the same drivers only; OtherEncoder with a forced adapter LUID; 140 fps capped to 60; a 3840x2160 writer at 60 fps in hardware and software (30 frames, playable; the hardware one reports async).
- **Not verified:** the owner's RX 9070 / iGPU PC, 4K duplication at 140 Hz, a real cross-adapter encoder, a real driver hang (only simulated ones), the HEVC fallback, the failure dialog on screen, why the UI thread hung there (the fix removes every recorder call from it). A truly hung Windows call keeps its thread and part file until Windows lets go (the file is deleted then). **Open:** an area larger than the software encoder's level (over 4096x2304, e.g. spanning two 4K monitors with GDI) has no further fallback (it fails with the sentence; scaling down is not built); GIF is not used as a last resort for an MP4 (it would change the file type unasked).

## Recording hang investigation, save dialog, JPG default (18 Nov) - tests run on the PC (recording a 128x96 corner and the full 1920x1200 screen into %TEMP%, nothing else changed); the app was not launched, nothing clicked
- **Tests (new `tests/OmniControl.Tests/RecordingFileTests.cs`, all pass):** the real `ScreenRecorder` for 2.5 s: MP4 hardware (graphics-card path, Desktop Duplication) and software (GDI + Sink Writer), each with and without system sound, at 30 fps and the owner's 60 fps with the pointer drawn, plus GIF; Stop must finish within `Timing.RecordingStopWait`; the MP4 must have ftyp, moov and mdat and Media Foundation's Source Reader must read its duration (2.0-3.3 s) and at least 20 video frames; the MP4 writer alone in hardware and software (exactly 60 frames, 2.0 s). Ran green together with `RecordingAbortTests`, `VideoRecordingTests`, `ScreenshotTests` (121 tests).
- **Measured (throwaway diagnostics, deleted):** Stop to finished file 41-154 ms in every case: 128x96 and 1920x1200, 2.5 s and 16 s, hardware and software, from an STA thread with a pumping synchronisation context like the UI thread, toggling system sound during the recording. The graphics-card writer alone at 4K and 1080p (fake texture source) finalised in under 10 ms. The two older MP4s in Videos\OmniControl (16 Nov) are complete (ftyp, moov, mdat) and Media Foundation decodes them.
- **Root cause: not reproduced.** No Stop hang could be made in Core on this PC with the owner's settings. Real defects found and fixed: (1) Windows refuses Desktop Duplication with E_ACCESSDENIED on the secure desktop (it happened during the test run while the PC was locked); .NET throws that as `UnauthorizedAccessException`, which `DesktopDuplication` did not catch, so a graphics-card recording failed to start, or failed mid-way on a UAC prompt or Ctrl+Alt+Del (`AcquireNextFrame` gives the same code). Now it is "lost": GDI copies into the texture until it can duplicate again. (2) The recording's clock started before the encoder opened (0.5-1.8 s for the hardware encoder here), so every hardware MP4 began with a gap of that length (4.27 s file for 2.5 s recorded); the clock now starts once the file and screen copy are open (16.58 s for 16.5 s). (3) `Completion` waited for the screen copy and the WASAPI captures to be released after the file was finished; it is now set as soon as the file is closed, so a slow release can never hold Stop. (4) The unopenable MP4 the owner saw fits a file left open by a stuck recorder: `Abort` cannot delete a file the Sink Writer still holds, and an unfinished MP4 has no moov box. Not verified on the owner's session: what held his Stop; if it happens again, `errors-log.txt` has no recording lines today, so the next step is a log line per stop step (loop end, Finish, Dispose) to see which one blocks.
- **Stop that hangs:** the old auto-abort after `Timing.RecordingStopWait` (which deleted the video) is gone: past that time the footer says it once and the X (Esc twice, the emergency key) asks with "Force close" (`SaveKinds.ForceCloseQuestion`), so the person always has a way out and a slow save is not thrown away unasked.
- **Save dialog (`docs/mockups/recording-save-and-cancel.html`):** `Screenshot/SaveFileWindow` + `SaveFileModel`, one dialog for both kinds (`SaveKind`, `SaveKinds`), run by `SavePlaces.ChooseAsync`. The folder shown and "Always save here" are the action's own settings (`ScreenshotOptions.Folder` / `VideoFolder`), and the choice is one new `ActionOption` per kind on the same actions, "Ask where to save" (`AskFolder` / `AskVideoFolder`, on by default) beside each "Save folder"; no App settings switch. A recording is written to the action's video folder as before and moved after Save (`File.Move` off the UI thread; if that fails it stays where it was and the footer says so); Discard (asked first) deletes it. Esc or the window's X counts as Discard (asks). Mouse first: the dialog has Enter = Save, Esc, Tab between the fields; no keyboard walk of its own.
- **Differences from the mock-up:** the default name is the action's "File name" pattern (default "Screenshot {date} {time}", so "Screenshot 2026-11-18 01-12-33.mp4"), not "Recording yyyy-MM-dd HH-mm": a second naming rule would duplicate that setting. The Force close question keeps the mock-up's "Keep recording" as its no-button although the recording has stopped by then. The folder line's tooltip is set on the OSD pop-up, but the pop-up is click-through on screen, so it cannot be hovered there.
- **OSD:** `OsdMessage.Detail` = a third smaller muted line, `TextTrimming.PrefixCharacterEllipsis` (start and end of the path stay), drawn by `OsdView.BuildMessage`; the screenshot and video notes put the folder there.
- **JPG default:** `ImageFormats.DefaultFormat` (JPG) and `DefaultQuality` 80 are the named defaults; `ScreenshotOptions.Format` and the Format option's `Default` (what Restore defaults puts back) read them; existing saved settings keep their format. Copy follows the action's format: DIB always (every program pastes it), plus JPG at the action's quality in the registered "JFIF" clipboard format when JPG is chosen, else PNG as before. What copy cannot carry as JPG: programs that read only PNG from the clipboard (some browsers and web editors) get the bitmap instead, so the pasted picture is lossless there; a .jpg file is put on the clipboard only with "Copy as: Image and file path" after a save; WebP and BMP are copied as PNG.
- **Open:** `ScreenshotService` / `VideoRecording` constructors grew by the confirm function; the WASAPI captures still use NAudio's obsolete `WasapiCapture` (works; the replacement is `WasapiRecorderBuilder`). Not checked on screen: the save dialog at both themes, its placement over the overlay, the confirm's red Discard (`Button.usebtn.danger`, now applied to any `DialogMessage.Danger`), the OSD third line.

## Recording stop and kill switch (18 Nov) - compiled into a throwaway folder; `MarkRenderTests`, `VideoRecordingTests`, `RecordingAbortTests` pass; the real overlay and bar were not clicked
- Cause (from code, not reproduced): `Record area` called `VideoRecording.StartAsync` from `PictureSurface`'s WM_LBUTTONUP, inside the native window that the close destroys; the continuation after the recorder started could land off the UI thread and the bar/frame then failed to open or run. Now `CaptureOverlayViewModel.Record` posts, `StartAsync` returns to the UI thread, and a bar that fails to open cancels the recording.
- `ScreenRecorder.Abort()` completes `Completion` at once (`RecordingEnd.Cancelled`, no file); `VideoRecording.Cancel()` closes bar and frame at once; `Stop()` past `Timing.RecordingStopWait` no longer aborts (see "Recording hang investigation" above: Force close). Bar: X pill, Esc twice (`Timing.RecordingEscTwice`). Action `screenshot.stoprecording` (NeverBlock, Ctrl+Alt+F12): stop; again while ending = cancel. A global double-Esc was not built (Esc cannot be a hotkey).
- Highlighter overlap: `ImageBitmaps.DrawLayered` is the one drawing path (screen, `MarkPainter`, export). Markers separated by a pen or box stroke in the order are layered separately.

## Hardware watches (18 Nov) - compiled into a throwaway folder under %TEMP% (0 errors); `RestoreDefaultsTests` and `SuggestKeyTests` run (22 passed); nothing launched, no Remote Desktop session, plug or sleep tried on the PC
- The bug: after a Remote Desktop session a monitor stayed undetected until Displays > Hardware > Refresh. Cause: the only trigger was `Window.Screens.Changed` -> a cheap `Rescan`, which compares Windows' display list, and that list is the same after Remote Desktop while the DDC/CI handles are dead.
- ONE master list, `Services/HardwareWatches.cs`: a `HardwareWatch` = id, name, `HardwareSignal` flags (which Windows announcements can affect it; the one place that says so), `Refresh` (worker thread, returns when done) and an optional `LooksStale`. Signals come from `Core/Engine/HardwareSignals.cs`, two listeners: a hidden top-level window on its own message-loop thread (never HWND_MESSAGE, which gets no broadcasts; registered with `WTSRegisterSessionNotification` NOTIFY_FOR_THIS_SESSION: WM_DEVICECHANGE, WM_DISPLAYCHANGE, WM_WTSSESSION_CHANGE for console connect 1, remote connect 3, remote disconnect 4, logon 5 and unlock 8, WM_POWERBROADCAST resume) and .NET's `SystemEvents` (SessionSwitch with the same reasons, DisplaySettingsChanged, PowerModeChanged resume; its own window and thread; package `Microsoft.Win32.SystemEvents` in Core); the same kind from both within `CoreTiming.HardwareSignalDuplicateMs` (1 s) passes once (`Core/Engine/HardwareSignalFilter.cs`) and from the sound devices' own notice (`CoreAudio.SessionWatcher.DevicesChanged`, the mixer passes it in with `Signal(Audio)`). A watch WITH `LooksStale` gets the retry schedule (`Services/HardwareRetry.cs`, `Timing.HardwareRetrySchedule` 2, 5, 10, 20 s counted from the last signal of a burst; a new signal starts it again): the first try always refreshes, a later try first asks `LooksStale` (a Refresh pressed meanwhile ends it), and the schedule stops once `LooksStale` is false after a refresh. A watch without `LooksStale` gets one refresh per burst (`ThrottledRun`: `Timing.HardwareDebounce` 2 s, `HardwareRefreshGap` 4 s). Every signal (with its source: which message or SystemEvents reason) and every try's result is one line in `hardware-log.txt` (`HardwareWatches.LogFile`). The monitors' watch (`displays.hardware`) refreshes with `ReloadNow`, the Refresh button's own work, and looks stale when fewer DDC/CI monitors answer than were found before with the same desktop monitors attached, or none while Windows lists one (`Pages/Displays/MonitorExpectation.cs`). Bug fixed 18 Nov: after Remote Desktop the monitors stayed undetected until Refresh; the window and its registration were sound, the cause was one refresh about 2 s after the burst (at most a second at the 4 s gap) while DDC/CI often does not answer for 2-10 s after a console reconnect, and the periodic check is Off by default, so nothing looked again. Not verified on a real Remote Desktop return (agents may not drive the PC); `hardware-log.txt` shows what happened if it recurs. Every refresh runs on a worker inside `Guard`.
- **Second round (18 Nov, monitors still missing after RDP/unlock/wake/start until Refresh)** - compiled against a clean HEAD export under %TEMP% with only these files overlaid (0 errors; the live tree did not compile only because of the Other PCs wake job's unfinished files); `HardwareRetryTests` 9 passed; nothing launched, no PC state touched. Causes found and fixed:
  1. The retry's check ran before the monitors were read: `ReloadNow` returned as soon as handles were opened (the cards probe on their own task), so `LooksStale` only compared handle count with the display list of the SAME refresh, always equal, and the log said "try 1 of 4 found it; stopping" (seen in hardware-log.txt at an unlock) while DDC/CI still did not answer; the probe then kept "no answer"/a partial answer on the monitor until Refresh. Fix: `ReloadNow` now ends with `ProbeMonitors` (parallel `IMonitor.Probe`, the answer is kept so the cards' read is free) and the check reads the answers.
  2. The check compared against `_engine.Displays` taken in that refresh (a stale snapshot), so a display Windows attached later never looked missing. Fix: `IMonitorEngine.DisplaysChanged()` (cheap, Windows' list now vs. held) is part of the check (`Rescan` uses it too).
  3. `MonitorExpectation` only knew what this run had seen, so at start (and after each rebuild of the app) one monitor found = "right". Fix: a stricter `HardwareWatch.LooksUnsettled` (new optional field, used by the retry schedule only): a desktop display without a handle, or a monitor not answering at all, keeps the schedule going; plus `MonitorExpectation.AnswersLess` (a monitor answering fewer codes than its best this run) in the normal check.
  4. No automatic look at start: the engine read once in its constructor. Fix: `HardwareWatches.Start` gives every watch with a check its schedule (trigger "start", each try only when the check says wrong). Schedule now 2, 5, 10, 20, 40 s.
  5. Missing signals: a monitor arriving/leaving (now `RegisterDeviceNotification` for GUID_DEVINTERFACE_MONITOR on the hidden window, `Core/Native/DeviceNotifyNative.cs`, raised as Display), the screens coming back on (power event DisplayOn -> new `HardwareSignal.DisplayPower`, check-first) and a clock gap (-> Resume); the last two are mapped in `HardwareWatches.OnPower` from the power events the same listeners already make, so `HardwareSignals`' power code is unchanged.
  6. `MonitorEnumerator` asked for exactly one physical monitor per display: a display with two (mirrored) was refused and dropped. Now `GetNumberOfPhysicalMonitorsFromHMONITOR` first (falls back to one), the extras are released.
  7. `QueryDisplayConfig` answering "buffer too small" while displays attach left names and device paths empty for that look; now asked up to 3 times (`DisplayConfigNative.Query`).
  8. `RefreshNow` caught only Win32/WMI errors from the laptop-panel read; any other (COM, access) skipped showing the monitors at all. Now the panel read is guarded alone and the monitors are always shown. The Refresh button's task is guarded too.
  - Periodic check: `AppSettings.HardwareCheck` default is now 1 minute (the owner's settings.json had no value, so it applies to him); intervals moved to `Timing.HardwareCheck*`. It asks only `LooksStale` (Windows' display list vs held, handle count vs best, codes vs best; probes are kept, so no DDC traffic) and refreshes at most `Timing.HardwareStaleRefreshMax` (3) times in a row while still stale (a monitor switched to another input), then waits for an announcement or Refresh (one log line says so).
  - Logging: every automatic refresh is one line `Name (trigger): result. Found: ...` (trigger = start, after Session/Display/Resume/DisplayPower, periodic check, its own notice, an action's device was missing); `HardwareWatch.Describe` gives the "Found" part (monitors: "3 displays, 3 DDC/CI monitors: PHL 27M1F5800 19 codes, ...").
  - Unverified: all of it on the owner's PC (no RDP, unlock, sleep or plug was done); that GUID_DEVINTERFACE_MONITOR notices arrive on this PC; the extra refreshes (up to 5 after each unlock while a monitor with DDC/CI off never answers, each rebuilding the Hardware cards) being acceptable; whether 40 s is long enough after a slow Remote Desktop return (the periodic check then catches a monitor that answered before).
- Registered (by their owners): `displays.hardware` (full `MonitorEngine.RefreshMonitors` + probe of every monitor = the Refresh button's `ReloadNow`; stale and unsettled as above), `displays.software` (`RescanNow`), `audio.devices` (the mixer's scan, both playback and recording; ONE entry because one scan reads both lists), `pointing.devices` (`TrackpadService.Refresh`; stale when the touchpad's presence differs), `devices.smart` (`DeviceService.CheckAllNowAsync`: reads every device that has an auto-check, due or not).
- Signal to watch: Display and DisplayPower -> displays.hardware, displays.software; Session and Resume -> every watch; Device -> audio.devices, pointing.devices; Audio -> audio.devices. (Device is NOT given to the monitors: it fires for every USB plug and a DDC/CI reopen on each would be heavy; a monitor plug also raises Display.)
- On demand: `HotkeyAction.HardwareWatch` names the watch whose list an action's device picks come from (set by the Displays page on its scoped actions); `HotkeyRegistry.RunNow` checks `HasMissingPick`, calls `HardwareWatches.RefreshThen` once and runs the action again; a watch is not refreshed for an action more than once per `Timing.HardwareOnDemandGap` (10 s).
- Periodic: App settings > Start and close > "Check hardware every" (Off, 1, 5, 15 minutes; `AppSettings.HardwareCheck`, `Services/HardwareChecks.cs`; part of Restore defaults). **Default 1 minute since the second round** (it reads no DDC/CI, see above). It only refreshes watches whose `LooksStale` says so; a watch without one (mixer, smart devices) is left to signals.
- Removed: `Window.Screens.Changed` in `WindowLifecycle`, `ShellViewModel.NotifyDisplaysChanged`, `PageViewModel.OnDisplaysChanged`, `DisplaysPageViewModel.OnDisplaysChanged`, `Timing.DisplayChangeSettle`; the trackpad's own `DevicesChanged` now only asks its watch (`Request`) instead of posting `Refresh` itself. The mixer's 2 s timer, the session watcher's own re-attach and `MasterWatcher` stay: they are part of how those services work, not hardware-refresh listeners.
- Skipped: Other PCs (it has its own `Timing.PcLinkCheck` loop and I left `OtherPcs/` alone, files there are being edited by another job); mouse hook (no device list; its watchdog exists); actions of the Volume page have no device-pick scope except the combined microphones, which were not given a `HardwareWatch` (they would use `audio.devices`; one line each on the three `ComboDevices` actions); the OSD's USB watcher (`UsbDeviceWatcher`, WMI) is a separate pop-up feature and was left as it is.
- Unverified: the whole feature on a real Remote Desktop round trip; that the hidden window receives WM_DISPLAYCHANGE and WM_WTSSESSION_CHANGE on this PC; the 2 s settle being long enough for the display driver after a Remote Desktop return (if not, raise `HardwareDebounce`); the card's placement (it sits in Start and close because no group fits better; the owner may move it).

## Narrow card fit (17 Nov) - built to `docs/mockups/narrow-card-fit.html`; compiled into a throwaway folder (0 errors); not run, nothing launched or clicked
New `Controls/LeadFitPanel` (chip gives way first down to `ChipMinWidth`, then the slider field down to `SliderFieldMinWidth`; both in `App.axaml`) used by `Pages/SliderRow`'s plain layout; `ChoiceChip` puts the full value in the chip's tooltip. Only user of `SliderItem.Lead` is the Other PCs mirror (`PcMirror`). Open: `SliderFieldMinWidth` (116) is the slider's least width + value 48 + gap 8 typed once as a number; the companion-switch and half-width layouts and the Volume/Easy `SliderField` users have no chip and are unchanged; narrow window, both themes and search results not looked at (rule 7).

## Screenshot overlay speed, third pass: a native picture surface (18 Nov) - compiled only (Core, the app and the test project into a throwaway folder under %TEMP%, 0 errors); `MarkRenderTests`, `ScreenshotTests`, `BlankScreenshotTests`, `SelectionFrameTests` run (80 passed, 19 new, the old picture-cache test removed); nothing captured, drawn or shown on the PC

- **Root cause (read from Avalonia 11.3.6's own code: `ServerCompositionTarget.Render`, `WinUiCompositedWindow`, `D3D11AngleWin32PlatformGraphics`):** the defaults here are ANGLE on Direct3D 11 with WinUI composition. Its render target never keeps the last frame (`RetainsPreviousFrameContents` is false on every GPU target; only the software framebuffer sets it), so on EVERY frame with any change Avalonia draws the changed area into its own full-window layer, then clears the whole window surface, copies the whole layer into it and hands Windows a whole new surface to compose. At 3840 x 2160 that is about 33 MB cleared, 66 MB copied and the full monitor composed again per frame: 100+ MB of graphics memory traffic per frame, about 14 GB/s at 140 Hz, however small the change. 1080p at 60 Hz is a quarter of the pixels at under half the rate (about 1/9 of the work), which is why the laptop felt fine. The owner's readings (graphics card 3D in bursts up to 27 %, CPU nearly idle) fit: the work is on the card and comes in short bursts, too short for the card to clock up. Frame pacing is not a 60 Hz timer: the render timer ticks on the compositor's commits (`RequestCommitAsync`, 1 s watchdog), so it follows the display. Avalonia draws on DXGI adapter 0 (no `GraphicsAdapterSelectionCallback` set); on a PC with an iGPU and a dGPU, if Windows' graphics preference makes the iGPU adapter 0 for OmniControl, every frame is also copied across to the card that drives the monitor (the "Copy" engine activity); not confirmed on the owner's PC. The second pass's `GpuPictureCache` (the picture uploaded once) was right but could not touch this per-frame cost.
- **The fix (`Core/Engine/Screenshot/PictureSurface.cs`, `SelectionFrame.cs`, `App/Screenshot/CaptureCanvas.cs`, `MarkPainter.cs`):** the overlay's picture is now a plain Win32 child window in the overlay (Avalonia `NativeControlHost`), painted as Lightshot paints: two copies of the picture in DIB sections, made once when the overlay opens (dimmed with `ScreenImage.Wash`, and bright), and on every change only the changed rectangles are copied with GDI `BitBlt` (dim outside the selection, bright inside), then the frame and handles are filled. A selection change repaints only the strips that turn bright or dim and both frames (`SelectionFrame.Changed`; a 3 px move of a 2000 x 1500 selection repaints well under a tenth of it). Windows composes only that area, on whichever card drives the monitor, so it no longer matters which adapter Avalonia uses. Avalonia's own window under it does not change during a drag, so it draws no frames. The drawings are painted into the bright copy by `MarkPainter`: it compares the drawings with those painted last and repaints only where one came, went or changed (`Mark.Covers`: a box's four sides, a line in 128 px pieces, a free-hand line per 64 points; `Mark.Version` / `CoversChangedSince`: only the newest points of a line being drawn), each part drawn by `ImageBitmaps.Render`, the same drawing as the export (Skia on the CPU). The drawing in progress is still its settled parts plus the growing rest, faded as one group by an opacity mask. All of it runs at most once per frame (`OncePerFrame`) and is painted at once (`UpdateWindow`).
- **Bars as pop-ups:** a native child window covers anything Avalonia draws under it, so the size label, the toolbar, the action bar and the text box are now `Popup`s (`Popup.floating` in `Styles/Bars.axaml`: placed at a point of the window by their offsets, never slid or flipped, never light-dismissed, no window shadow) placed by `CaptureOverlay.PlaceBars` as before; moving one only moves its small window. The colour and QR pop-downs open from them as before. Keys still go to the overlay window (the picture window never takes the keyboard); the text box takes them while open.
- **Removed:** `Screenshot/GpuPictureCache.cs` (nothing draws the picture on the graphics card any more), its test and `CaptureSession`'s cache sum; `Mark.SettledCount`; the canvas's Avalonia layers. **Moved:** the window-class P/Invokes from the touchpad's `RawInputNative` to the shared `Core/Native/WindowNative.cs`; `GdiFlush` from `VideoNative` to `ScreenshotNative`; the hand-written DIB headers to `ScreenshotNative.CreateTopDownBitmap` (screen capture, recording frame grabber, pointer painter, the surface).
- **Looks, kept and changed:** the dim, the bright selection and the drawings are pixel for pixel what the export draws. The frame was a 1 screen-unit line drawn half over each side of the edge with soft edges; it is now a crisp line of round(scale) physical pixels astride the edge (at 100 % one pixel just outside the selection), the handles crisp squares of round(8 x scale) pixels. The pointer's shapes are Windows' own (as Avalonia used). Mouse first as before; keyboard unchanged.
- **Colour pop-down fix (owner's report):** clicking a colour closed the pop-down but did not change the colour. A `Button` raises `Click` before it runs its `Command` (Avalonia 11.3.6 `Button.OnClick`), and `OnSwatchPicked` closed the flyout in `Click`; a closed flyout leaves the window's logical tree, so the swatch lost its data context and its `Pick` command was gone by the time the button looked it up. The flyout is now closed after the click (`Dispatcher.UIThread.Post`). Test `PickedColour_IsUsedForNewDrawingsOnly` (view-model and painter: the next drawing uses the colour, the earlier one keeps its own); the click order itself is not unit-tested.
- **Costs to know:** each monitor now holds the picture three times (the Avalonia bitmap for the export, QR and blur, plus the two copies): about 100 MB per 4K monitor, released when the overlay closes.
