  pipe's rate and block size (`PipeFormat.SampleRate`, `CoreTiming.PipeMaxBlockFrames`), mains on, start process. Processing:
  interleaved in/out copied to channel buffers allocated once in native memory (no allocation per block), processReplacing or the old
  accumulating process with cleared outputs, mono plug-ins as on the VST3 path (`PluginStage` mixes L+R in, copies one output to both),
  extra inputs (side-chain) get silence, outputs past two are dropped; all under the same per-plug-in lock as VST3, which UI-thread
  changes also take. Latency: `initialDelay`, read live, so it reaches the row's delay and the FX header like a VST3's. State: the
  program chunk (effGetChunk index 1, the bytes Equalizer APO applies) for plug-ins with chunks, else every parameter's name and value;
  marked so `Vst2Settings.Read` knows it. Parameters: by index, name (effGetParamName), the plug-in's display text with its label, 0..1,
  continuous; the default (double-click reset) is the value right after loading; typing uses percent of the range (VST2 has no units of
  its own). Editor: effEditOpen into the host child window, its size from effEditGetRect (asked again once shown; 400 x 300 if it never
  says), effEditIdle every `Timing.PluginEditorIdle` (33 ms) from `PluginEditorArea`'s timer, a size the plug-in asks for
  (audioMasterSizeWindow, any thread) taken in that idle call and raised as `Resized`; not resizable by the host; no host scale: a Scale
  other than 100 % is refused as for VST3 editors that ignore it (drop-down greyed, Parameters offered). One editor per plug-in at a
  time. Host callback: allocation-free, never throws into native code, finds its instance through `AEffect.user` (no static table);
  answers version 2400, current id, time info (rate and sample position only), sample rate, block size, process level (real time on the
  audio thread), vendor/product "OmniControl", canDo sizeWindow, language English, ioChanged/updateDisplay; everything else 0. Unload:
  mains off, `user` cleared, effClose, buffers freed; the DLL itself is never unloaded once its code ran (TabForge's rule); at exit under
  `ExitGuard` like VST3.
- **Scanner and folders.** `PluginScanner` finds every type by its file ending and header only (`MaxDepth` 4 = the one depth limit);
  a .dll counts only if it exports VSTPluginMain or main and is 64-bit. `PluginFolders.All()` = the VST3 folders, Program Files\VstPlugins,
  Program Files\Steinberg\VSTPlugins, Common Files\VST2, Common Files\Steinberg\VST2, the registry's HKLM\SOFTWARE\VST\VSTPluginsPath,
  then the user's folders; every root is scanned for both formats. plugins.json stores the tag ("VST2"). `PluginLibrary` is ONE object
  made by `ShellViewModel` and shared by every FX window and App settings (before, each FX window read its own copy); it reads
  plugins.json when first needed; `FoundText` is the one result sentence ("Found 20 plug-ins: 15 VST3, 5 VST2.").
- **App settings > plug-in folders.** `AppSettings.PluginFolders`; cards after "When I close the window": "Plug-in folders" (master
  `FileItem`, now with `FileKind.OfFolder`, which opens the folder picker), one "Scanned folder" row per folder (master `ButtonItem`:
  the full path, Remove), "Plug-in scan" (Scan now; the card's line shows the progress and then the result: `ButtonItem.Description`
  can change now). All searchable; hotkey "Scan for plug-ins" (`app.plugins.scan`). The page gets the scan as two callbacks, so Pages
  still never reference `App/Fx`. The Add effect window's status line ends with "Use App settings > Plug-in folders for other places."
- **Equalizer APO.** An enabled VST2 slot at 100 % wet on a Fast mode APO row is one line
  `VSTPlugin: Library "<full path>" ChunkData "<base64>"` or `... "<name>" <value> ...` (names quoted, inner quotes doubled, invariant
  decimals); no saved settings = the line with the library only (the plug-in's defaults). Left out with its reason in the row's
  "saved only, not run here" list: file missing, or no VSTPluginMain (Equalizer APO calls only that). Wet below 100 % is left out as for
  every effect. VST3 is unchanged (saved only there). The row status says "plug-ins run inside Windows with the settings saved here,
  their delay not known" (instead of "no extra delay") and "default settings until its window is opened: <names>"; the FX window's
  per-effect note says "in Equalizer APO with the settings saved here", its tooltip the rest (no window there, changes reach Windows when
  the plug-in's window closes, a plug-in that misbehaves there can interrupt Windows audio). A plug-in's saved settings are written
  into the line whenever the plug-in host saves states (`StatesSaved` -> `EffectsEngine.Reconcile`; the files change only when the text
  does). The FX header's Latency reads "not known" on such a row (`ChainCurve.RunsPlugins`); the curve lists the plug-in as not drawn.
- **Wording corrected:** AGENTS.md "Honest reporting", README, ARCHITECTURE.md, NOTICE.txt (TabForge: what was taken, MIT/GPL; the
  VeSTige line), Fast mode's details text, the Add effect texts; the old "VST2 plug-ins (.dll) can't run in OmniControl" message is gone.

**Differences from the plan.** `Vst2State.cs` became `Vst2Settings.cs` (named after its type). The Reaper host-API probe was not
ported (ReaPlugs ask for it; without it they get 0; not verified that they then run). Tail size (effGetTailSize) is not used:
OmniControl plays a live stream and never stops a chain to let a tail ring out, so there is nothing to use it for.

**Not verified (nothing was run).** No VST2 plug-in was loaded, processed, opened or saved; no VSTPlugin line was read by Equalizer
APO; no screen was seen. In particular: (1) the AEffect layout and callback against real plug-ins (it is TabForge's, which has run
real plug-ins); (2) whether effEditOpen into Avalonia's NativeControlHost child works for every VST2 editor (DPI-unaware editors will
look small on a scaled display: VST2 has no host scale); (3) the program-chunk choice: a plug-in whose program chunk lacks part of its
state would lose that part (OmniControl and Equalizer APO alike; TabForge saves the bank chunk); (4) that Equalizer APO accepts the
base64 and the quoted names as written (read from its source only); (5) the App settings cards at the narrowest window and in both
themes, and focus after Remove (the removed card had the keyboard); (6) the folder picker on a network path.

**Risks of in-process VST2 hosting (said plainly).** A VST2 plug-in runs native code inside OmniControl: a crash or hang in it takes
OmniControl down (the engine journal restores the sound at the next start), and nothing can catch an access violation in its code.
Its DLL stays loaded until exit. The host finds itself through `AEffect.user`: a plug-in that writes that host field (against the
interface) would crash OmniControl at its next callback. In Equalizer APO the same plug-in runs inside Windows' audio engine
(audiodg.exe, no window, the audio service's account: plug-ins that need the user's files, a licence check or an iLok may not work
there); Equalizer APO catches a crash while processing and then passes the sound unprocessed, but a hang there stalls Windows audio.

**Open items.** (1) Out-of-process hosting (one helper per plug-in) would contain crashes for both formats; not built. (2) VST2 bank
chunk vs program chunk: if a plug-in is found that saves only part of itself in its program chunk, store the bank chunk for
OmniControl's own engine and keep the program chunk for Equalizer APO. (3) No VST2 programs (factory presets) picker. (4) Equalizer
APO runs one VST2 instance per channel pair; a mono plug-in runs once per channel there. (5) Changes made in a VST2's window on an APO
row reach Equalizer APO only when the window closes (each write makes Equalizer APO reload the plug-in, so it is not done live).
(6) MIT entries in NOTICE.txt (TabForge, NAudio, Avalonia...) give the copyright line but not the MIT permission text; shipping the
texts (a licences folder) is still to do. (7) The scan reads the export table of every .dll under the folders; a very large user folder
makes the scan slow (off the UI thread; it can be stopped in the Add effect window, not on App settings).

**Owner test steps.**
1. App settings: "Plug-in folders" > Choose… > pick a folder with a 64-bit VST2 .dll; a "Scanned folder" row appears with the path.
   Scan now: the line counts, then "Found N plug-ins: … VST3, … VST2." Remove the row, Scan now again: its plug-ins leave the list.
2. Full control, a program's FX window > Add: the VST2 shows with "VST2"; add it. Its own window shows inside the FX window; move a
   knob, double-click its row: it pops out with the knob as set; Put back. Parameters: the list of its settings; move one, the plug-in follows.
3. Play sound through the row: the effect is heard; the row's delay includes the plug-in's latency if it reports one.
4. Close OmniControl and start it again: the VST2's settings are as left.
5. Fast mode, the master's FX window: add the VST2, open its window, change something, close the FX window. In
   `C:\Program Files\EqualizerAPO\config\OmniControl.txt` the master's section has `VSTPlugin: Library "...dll" ChunkData "..."` (or
   names and values); the sound changes (Equalizer APO runs it). The row says "plug-ins run inside Windows with the settings saved
   here, their delay not known"; the FX header Latency "not known". Wet 90 %: the line goes, the row lists it as not run here.
6. Load VST file… with a 32-bit VST2 or a non-plug-in DLL: one sentence says why; a VST2 instrument: "This is an instrument, not an effect."

## FX window tidy, keyboard instructions off screen (15 Nov) - plan, written before coding (as-built notes in the next section)

Owner-approved layout (with a visual) and the new design principles 12 (no keyboard instructions on screen) and 13 (fit more,
clutter less).

**FX window, top to bottom.** Title bar and menu (FX, Options) as before; Options gains "Plays on" (a program's output device, see
below) and "Keyboard shortcuts…". Then ONE toolbar row (`Border.toolrow`, a new master in `Styles/Bars.axaml`: about 30 px high,
11 px type, 22 px controls) holding, left to right: the Effects switch with its on/off words; the selected effect's preset drop-down
(equalisers only), Wet label + compact slider + value, On check box, Parameters pill, Scale drop-down, one Pop out / Put back pill
(these come up from under and above the editor, same bindings, tooltips and remembered settings; hidden while nothing is selected);
a "…" pill (only when folded); a flexible gap; then two tiny stacked lines "Peak …" / "Latency …" (10 px, muted label + value, the
caution Peak kept) and the response thumbnail (now 120 x 24) with its ±N label, hover graph and pin unchanged. Below it the two panes:
the effects list (narrower: 170 px) with small Add / Remove pills at its foot, and the editor area with ALL the remaining height
(nothing under it). At the very bottom ONE thin status strip (`Border.statusstrip`, 10 px muted, one line, trimmed, tooltip = full
text): Fast mode's recording caution (caution colour, only when it applies), the engine status line, and the selected effect's
"Runs in …" note at the right end only if it fits.

**Folding (narrowest FX window 560 px).** `Fx/FoldingRow` is extended instead of adding a second piece: children may carry
`FoldingRow.FoldLevel` (1, 2 …: folded in that order while the row is too wide), `FoldingRow.ShowsWhenFolded` (N: shown only while
level N is folded: the "…" pill) and `FoldingRow.Fill` (takes the width left over: the gap in the toolbar, the engine text in the status
strip, which then trims). Level 1 = Peak/Latency (they stay in the hover graph); level 2 = Parameters, Scale, Pop out, which then live
in the "…" pill's `MenuFlyout` (mouse first, the master menu look; the same items as the FX menu). The old `Reserve` and the
`besideoutput` rule go (no Output picker in the header any more).

**Output picker.** The approved row has no room for a program's Output drop-down, so it moves to Options > "Plays on" (radio items:
Master (the default device) and each device; several devices shows as on the Volume page). Decision the owner can overrule.

**Removed.** The FX window's keyboard footer strip and its strings; the effect count that sat in it (the list numbers the rows);
the Output picker in the header; CPU everywhere: the header and panel number, `FxAnalysisViewModel.CpuText/CpuTip` and smoothing,
`Dsp/CpuMeter.cs`, `ChainProcessor`'s block timing, `EngineRun.Cpu`, `RowLoad.Cpu` / `CpuSample`; the "Add... Ctrl+N" / "Remove Del"
key captions on the list buttons (the tooltips name the keys).

**Keyboard instructions app-wide (principle 12).** Every long hint paragraph at the bottom of a page or window goes: Volume (both
tabs), Mouse and Trackpad, Gestures, Displays, OSD, Shortcuts, App settings, the Parametric and graphic equaliser editors, the rule
editor, the master picker (both pages). Facts that are not about keys move to tooltips: a new `CardGroup.Tip` shown on the shared
group header (one place, every group can carry its facts), a `Tip` on `ToggleItem` / `GalleryItem` (OSD's "best-effort", the accent
note), the equaliser graphs' mouse gestures on the graphs, Test's "where the pointer is". Short captions that carry state stay
(App settings' start-up note, the Combine window's "Tick at least two devices", the Shortcuts page's one-line caption, the OSD page's
intro; the wording pass that follows this job may trim them).

**Where the shortcuts live.** Each page owns its text: `PageViewModel.KeyHelp` (virtual); the keys every card list shares are one
constant (`PageViewModel.CardKeys`), the shell's own keys one constant in `ShellViewModel`; shared windows keep theirs beside their
model (`PickerModel.KeyHelp`, `RuleEditorViewModel.KeyHelp`) and the pages that open them add them. The main window's footer gets a
"Keyboard shortcuts" pill (and F1) that opens the existing small `MessageWindow` with the open page's text, the card keys and the
shell's. The FX window's Options > "Keyboard shortcuts…" opens the same window (owned by the FX window) with the chain's keys and the
equaliser editors' keys (`EqPanelItem.KeyHelp`, `GraphicEqItem.KeyHelp`). All key handling and tooltips stay. The search index never
held the hint texts (search lists editors only), so it does not change.

**Light density pass (principle 13).** Only cheap, clearly better changes in the files touched; further ideas go to open items.

## Full control owns the default playback device (15 Nov) - compiled only (throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors); nothing run, no audio device or Windows setting changed; VB-CABLE is not installed on this PC

Owner: "Full control mode should use the virtual drivers; toggling it doesn't change the default playback to the virtual driver."

**As built**
- Planning (`RoutePlanner`, pure): in Full control the master bus is **always** planned (it takes the first free cable after the remembered
  ones), not only with master effects or several devices. `PlanMaster` plays the bus on `MasterPlaysOn` (now any non-empty list in
  Full control, so one combined device works) or else on the real device from `MasterDevice.Real` (`App/Audio/MasterDevice.cs`, the one
  rule: Windows' default when it is a real device, else `MixerSettings.RealMasterDevice` when plugged in, else the first real playback
  device; a virtual cable is never real). `RoutePlan.Bus` (`BusPlan`: cable + that choice) tells the engine what to do.
- `Audio/Engine/BusDefault.cs` (called by `EffectsEngine.SyncDefault` after every plan and whenever the bus run's status changes): while
  the bus run is **running**, it remembers the real device (`RealMasterDevice`), sets `MixerSettings.BusCable` (persisted, so a crash
  is repaired at the next start), and makes the cable the default (`MixerService.TrySetDefaultDevice`, console + multimedia). When the bus
  stops (Fast mode, cable removed, run failed, exit via `EffectsEngine.Dispose` -> `Release`) the default goes back to the remembered
  device, **only if the cable is still the default** (a device the user chose meanwhile is not undone). A bus run that is only
  restarting on the same cable keeps the cable as the default. Unplugged real device: the first plugged-in real device is used and one
  sentence says so (the remembered device is kept, so it comes back when plugged in). A refusal by Windows is reported once and not
  retried until something differs.
- A device picked in Windows' Sound settings while Full control runs becomes the new real device (the cable is made the default again).
- One helper for "the master's device": `MixerService.MasterDeviceId` (real device while the bus owns the default, else null =
  Windows' default) and `DefaultDeviceId` (replaces the Volume page's private copy). Master volume, mute, balance
  (`CoreAudio.*Master(..., deviceId)`), the hotkeys and wheel steps, the master meter (`Peaks`) and the OSD pop-up for outside changes
  (`MasterWatcher.Device` / `Retarget`, `OsdService.WatchVolumeOf`) all use it. **Choice: master volume changes the real device's
  level**, not the cable's, so it does not depend on whether loopback capture sees the cable's volume. `MixerService.MasterDeviceChanged`
  refreshes the master row.
- Wiring tab: in Full control `ChooseMasterTarget` changes `RealMasterDevice` (`MakeMasterDevice`) while the cable stays the default;
  with no cable (bus can't run) or in Fast mode it makes the device the default as before.
- `EffectsEngine.BuildChain` now returns an empty chain while a row's effects are switched off (the bus always runs now, and a chain
  with effects off must not be applied; this also fixes a program on several devices with effects switched off). The bus status says
  "Playing through the master bus" when nothing is processed.
- `SoundModes.Full.Details` no longer says system sounds bypass OmniControl.

**Owner test steps** (needs VB-CABLE)
1. Fast mode: note Sound settings' default playback device (say Speakers). Switch to Full control. Within about a second Sound settings
   should show "CABLE Input" as the default and sound should still play from Speakers (system sounds too).
2. Switch back to Fast: the default must be Speakers again. Exit while in Full control: after exit the default must be Speakers.
3. In Full control change the master's device on the Wiring tab: the default must stay CABLE Input, the sound moves to the new device,
   the volume keys change the new device's level. Then change the default in Windows' Sound settings: OmniControl makes the cable the
   default again and plays on the device you picked.
4. In Full control set the default to something else in Windows, then switch to Fast: the default must stay as you set it.
5. Unplug the real device in Full control: one sentence names the device now used; switching to Fast puts the default on that one.
6. Kill OmniControl in Full control (Task Manager), start it in Fast mode: the default is put back at start-up.

**Skipped / not verified**
- Nothing was run: not the default change, the restore, the crash repair, the OSD watcher retarget or the volume on the real device.
- Fast-mode helpers that read "the default playback device" (the probe's `DefaultDeviceId`, `ApoStatus`, the OmniControl APO install in
  `EngineInstaller`) are not redirected: they see the cable while Full control runs. They matter only in Fast mode, where the default is
  the real device again after about a second.
- With only combined devices on the master, the volume acts on `RealMasterDevice`, which is then not one of the master's devices.
- Only one VB-CABLE: the bus now takes it, so processed programs, combined devices and processed microphones without a remembered cable
  report "Needs another virtual cable" (before, the bus only took one when needed).
- A refusal by Windows (undocumented IPolicyConfig) leaves the real device as the default with the bus idle; only programs sent to the
  cable by per-program routing pass through it. The status line for that case is the reported sentence only.

### Full control: master bus watchdog (15 Nov)

- `BusRun.Tick` compares the cable's own meter (`MixerService.DevicePeak`) with the bus's capture peak. Sound at the cable and none in the recording for `Timing.BusDeafLimit` (3 s) fails the run ("the master bus got no sound, so the default went back to <device>"); `EffectsEngine.OnRunChanged` then stops it and `BusDefault` restores the real default through the normal path. The failure stands (no flapping) until the routing changes or the sound mode / PC facts change (`OnModeChanged`).
- Not detected: a bus that records fine but plays into a muted or wrong real device (a muted master reads silent, so it would false-trip); a silent cable never trips (nobody playing proves nothing).
- Start race: the default can not wait for "first audio" (with nothing playing there is none, and system sounds would never reach the bus), so it is still taken once the run is up and the watchdog covers the rest.
- Unverified suspects for the original silence: the cable endpoint's own volume or mute (if loopback is post-volume it is silent), and a loopback format refusal. Nothing was tested on screen.

## Parametric EQ editor redesign (design C, docs/mockups/eq-design-c.html)
- Band list left (`Button.bandrow` rows in `BandRowsPanel`, min 18 px, master scroll bar, "+ Add band" under it), graph middle, selected band's vertical gain slider right (`Slider.vband`, +24 top to -24 bottom, double-click resets to the band's default, step half a dB, Shift/Page 2 dB), Type/Frequency/Bandwidth/On panel under (`EqBandRow` with `ShowGain=False`; it keeps its Gain row in the plain per-band cards).
- Graph: double-click a circle toggles the band (no move); bypassed = dashed hollow circle; circles 9 px, 6 px above 10 bands (selected +3 and an accent ring); numbers hidden inside small circles. Band colours = `BrushBand1..6`, cycled by band place (`EqBandColors`).
- Keyboard in the list (`EqPanelItem.ListKey`, tunnelled in `EqPanelRow.axaml.cs`): Up/Down wrap, Space/Enter toggle, Delete removes. Mouse-first design choice for the list; the overall card keys (Left/Right) still pick a band.
- Removed: the "Tabs" switch and `AppSettings.EqShowTabs` (old settings.json entries are ignored), `Button.scope.band`, `EqBandItem.TabText`.
- Differences from the mock-up: Type/Frequency/Bandwidth keep the existing slider rows of `EqBandRow` (not boxed fields); Remove band, Reset defaults, Grid and Phase stay in a wrapping row under the panel (no "..." menu); the accent bar is inside the row (not at its outer edge); the gain readout sits under the slider but the typed-number box was not added for it (the band's typed gain is not available in this view); band colours are my own light/dark variants of the placeholders.
- Unverified (nothing run): whether the list Up/Down keys beat the card's own Up/Down routing, the viewport-height binding in `BandRowsPanel`, row look at 18 px, narrowest FX window width, light theme colours.

## Compressor, Limiter and IR Loader editors from TabForge (graphs, meters, picture)

Brief: reproduce TabForge's compressor, limiter and IR Loader windows (MIT, same owner; read-only clone at commit 3774ab6) as exactly as OmniControl's card list allows, with live graphs wired to the real processing. Not touched: the three equaliser editors.

**What was built**
- **The sound (program engine).** Compressor and Limiter now run in OmniControl's own engine (`FxEngines.Program`; before they were saved only): `Audio/Engine/Dsp/CompressorStage` (stereo-linked peak detector, soft knee from `Core/Models/CompressorCurve`, attack/release in the gain-reduction domain, makeup or auto makeup, meters) and `LimiterStage` (1.5 ms look-ahead true-peak limiter with `TruePeakDetector`, `SlidingMinimum`, fixed or automatic release; its look-ahead is its latency, `IDspStage.LatencyFrames`), ported from TabForge's `CompressorEffect` / `LimiterEffect`. They do not run in Equalizer APO (master and microphones in Fast mode): the row's status line, the effect's "Runs in OmniControl's engine only" note and the "saved only, not run here" text say so.
- **Stored data stays compatible.** Compressor keeps `Threshold, Ratio, Attack, Release, Makeup, Knee` in that order and gains `Auto makeup` (index 6); Limiter keeps `Ceiling, Release, Input gain` and gains `Auto release` (index 3). Older chains load: `FxCatalog.Complete` pads missing values with the defaults (the stage builder and the editor both call it). The `Makeup` setting's label is now "Makeup gain" (TabForge's); it is stored by index, nothing else used the label. `FxParam.IsSwitch` = an on/off setting (a half-width `ToggleItem`, stored 0/1). `Audio/DynamicsParams.cs` holds the indexes and limits once.
- **Live graphs.** `Fx/DynamicsItem` (the card's model) polls `EffectsEngine.ReadingOf(key, slot)` every `Timing.MeterFrame` (25/s; TabForge about 30/s) only while its `DynamicsRow` is on screen. The reading comes from the stage that processes the sound (`IDynamicsReadout`, written once per block with `Volatile`, read through `DspChain.ReadoutOf` and `EngineRun.ReadingOf`). Compressor card: `TransferGraph` (the transfer curve from the slot's own settings through `CompressorCurve`, the 1:1 line, the threshold line, a dot at the live input level) beside two `DynamicsMeter` bars (Gain reduction, Input level). Limiter card: the same two bars (the second is Output level) and the look-ahead line. When nothing runs in OmniControl's engine (effects off, Equalizer APO, the effect switched off, a fan-out run) the bars are empty, the dot is left out, the numbers read "-" and the card says "No live reading: this effect is not running in OmniControl's engine now (see the status line)." The curve is a picture of the settings, so it is always drawn.
- **Presets.** The compressor has TabForge's Bass / Vocal / Guitar factory presets in the toolbar's preset box (`FxKind.Presets`, `FxSlotViewModel.Dynamics.cs`); "Custom" shows while the settings match none. The toolbar tooltip now says "Preset of the selected effect".
- **IR Loader.** `IrFileItem` / `IrFileRow` / `IrGraph`: the file row (path trimmed at the start, "Browse..." starting in the loaded file's folder, drag a file onto the card), TabForge's picture (waveform above, frequency response below: 20 Hz to 20 kHz, 0 dB down to -54 dB, 100 Hz / 1 kHz / 10 kHz marks, the drop hint while empty, a missing file named) and a facts line (length, sample rate, mono or stereo). The picture is worked out once per file on a worker (`Audio/Ir/IrWaveFile`, `IrPictures`, `Fft`, from TabForge) and is a picture of the file, not of any processing. Mix and Gain stay the ordinary slider cards.
- Credit: NOTICE.txt (TabForge entry) lists every adapted piece. No new brush, style or master class: the drawings use `BrushBackground/Divider/Muted/Accent/AccentSoft/On/Caution/Danger`; new sizes `DynamicsGraphSize` and `IrGraphHeight` in `App.axaml`.

**Where it differs from TabForge, and why**
- Layout: TabForge puts the controls (sliders, switches, preset box) at the right of the curve in one window and the meters under them. Here the graph card (curve left, meters right) comes first and the settings are the ordinary slider cards below it, because OmniControl's editor is a list of cards driven by the shared keyboard model. The limiter has no graph in TabForge either (meters and a latency line only).
- Attack's smallest value is 1 ms (TabForge 0.1 ms) and the Ceiling moves in whole dB (TabForge decimals): the shared `SliderItem` is whole-number. Open item: a decimal slider card.
- No Bypass switch: the effect's own On switch (toolbar) does that; switching off removes the stage at once (TabForge fades for 5 to 10 ms), as every OmniControl effect does. The makeup / input-gain glide is the engine's linear `Glide` (30 ms), the same every other stage uses (TabForge: a one-pole).
- The stage's own slow meter fall (300 / 500 ms) is kept; TabForge's extra per-update bar fall limit in `MeterBar` is not repeated.
- The limiter's "reported to the engine so other tracks stay in time" wording is not used: OmniControl does not compensate any effect's delay, it adds it to the row's Latency (the FX window's Latency tooltip calls it "plug-ins": that number now includes the limiter's 1.5 ms).
- IR Loader: TabForge's Separate Left/Right files, Auto level, Trim silence, A/B and the latency / CPU / auto-gain readout are NOT built. OmniControl's IR runs in Equalizer APO as a plain `Convolution:` line (file and Preamp gain), which has none of them, and OmniControl's own engine does not run the IR at all; settings that do nothing would break the honest-reporting rule. The file types follow what Equalizer APO reads (wav, flac, aif, aiff); only a WAV can be drawn (others say "The picture is drawn for WAV files only."). TabForge's recent-files list is not kept: the picker starts in the chosen file's folder only.
- Colours: TabForge's `Success / Warning / Danger / AccentBrush / WindowBackground / BorderSoftBrush / TextMuted` map to `BrushOn / BrushCaution / BrushDanger / BrushAccent / BrushBackground / BrushDivider / BrushMuted`.

**Open items**
- The IR Loader in OmniControl's own engine (a partitioned convolver; TabForge's `PartitionedConvolver` and `IrPrep` are the model) and with it the L/R, Auto level, Trim and A/B settings and a real latency readout.
- Delay, Noise gate and Reverb editors (TabForge has `DelayEditor`, `GateEditor`, `ReverbEditor`; none of the three runs here yet), same card pattern as `DynamicsItem`.
- A decimal slider card (attack below 1 ms, ceiling steps).

**Owner test steps** (Full control mode, or a program in Fast mode, so OmniControl's own engine runs the row)
1. Add a Compressor to a program that plays music. The curve shows threshold -18 dB, ratio 4; the dot moves with the loudness and the Gain reduction bar grows when the sound passes the threshold line. Change Threshold: the yellow line and the curve move at once and the sound changes.
2. Choose Preset > Vocal: the sliders and the Auto makeup switch change at once; moving any slider shows "Custom".
3. Add a Limiter and raise Input gain: Gain reduction rises, Output level stays at or under the Ceiling; the look-ahead line shows 1.5 ms (72 samples). The row's Latency grows by about 1 ms.
4. Master in Fast mode: both effects show "No live reading..." and the status strip says they are saved only / not run there.
5. IR Loader: Browse a .wav, or drop one on the card: the waveform and response appear; a missing or non-WAV file shows its sentence.

**Skipped / not verified** (compile only; nothing was run, heard or looked at)
- On screen: the card layouts at the narrowest FX window, both themes, the meters' colours, the dot, the half-width switch when it is the only half card, drag and drop of a file (Avalonia 11.3 `e.Data.GetFiles()`), the picker's start folder.
- The DSP was not heard and has no unit test: the ports are TabForge's code mapped onto the interleaved block (the compressor links any channel count, the limiter handles stereo only, like the pipe); the limiter's state hand-over on an edit (one `LimiterState` carried to the new stage) and the compressor's envelope carry-over were reasoned, not run.
- Whether `EffectsEngine.ReadingOf` finds the same `FxSlot` objects the chain was built from in every run kind (it does for the program, master bus and microphone runs by reading; a pasted slot is a new object and a new stage).

## Volume keys in Full control (cable mirror) - compiled only; nothing run on screen, VB-CABLE state of this PC unknown
- Cause: in Full control the bus cable is Windows' default, so the keyboard volume keys and the Windows flyout changed the cable's endpoint, not the speakers.
- As built: `Audio/Engine/CableVolumeMirror.cs`, started and stopped by `BusDefault` (Take starts it once the cable is the default, Give stops it, `Dispose` at exit). Two `CoreAudio.MasterWatcher`s (cable and real device; the real id comes from the plan). A change on one side is written to the other (level and mute) on a worker. The cable first adopts the real device's level at start. Choice: the cable is kept AT the real device's level (not reset to 100 %), so one key press raises the real device by exactly one Windows step. Echoes of its own writes are told by value within `Timing.MirrorEchoGrace`, so no loop. Stop leaves the cable at the real level and unmuted; the real device is never touched on stop. A real device that is unplugged: reads/writes fail quietly, adopted again when the plan's real device changes.
- OSD: unchanged; the OSD's own watcher follows `MasterDeviceId` (the real device), which the mirror writes, so an outside volume change shows its pop-up. Not verified on screen.
- Owner test: VB-CABLE, Full control on; press keyboard volume up/down/mute, check the speakers, the Volume page master and the OSD; hold a key and tap quickly; switch to Fast and press the keys again.
- Not verified: everything at run time; the COM calls from a worker thread; a cable whose driver does not allow endpoint volume changes would simply not mirror.

## Equaliser presets: trace, kept settings and TabForge's preset manager

Brief: (a) "the 32 band EQ presets don't work?", (b) switching presets should keep things saved, (c) port TabForge's preset manager with a "Manage presets..." row at the end of the pull-down.

**(a) What was found.** The path was read end to end (ComboBox -> `ChoiceItem` -> `editor.Load` -> `Export` -> `_slot.Bands` -> `_save` -> `MixerService.SaveFx` -> `FxChanged` -> engine / APO writer; `GraphicEqItem.Load`, `GraphicBandItem` Gain/GainPos rounding at +-12 and +-24, `GraphicBands.Shaped` rounding to 0.5 dB, `EqLayout` matching). By reading, a picked preset does reach the sliders, the curve, the saved file and the engine; I found no break there, and the Parametric rebuild (`EqPanelItem.Load`) did not disturb it. What WAS wrong and is fixed: (1) a pick destroyed the hand-tuned curve for good; (2) the box could receive a null selection when its list was swapped (switching effects), and `FromPreset(null)` then threw inside the binding; the shared `PresetPicker` ignores it and keeps one list object while its entries do not change; (3) the toolbar box was 96 px wide, so "Treble boost" and any starred name were cut (now 120). Probable reason the owner saw "nothing": the shapes are only +-5 to 6 dB (the Parametric's old 10-band shapes, kept so they sound as before), rounded to 0.5 dB, on sliders that reach +-12: a visible but modest move. Not changed (they are the shared `Eq.PresetShapes`); stronger shapes are an open item for the owner to judge. Nothing was run, so whether a real failure remains on screen is unverified.

**(b) Kept settings.** One `Fx/PresetPicker` serves the Parametric, 16 and 32 bands and the compressor. The shown name is always worked out by matching the saved settings (built-in first, then the user's own, starred), so it is right after reopening the window and after a restart. When a preset (or Flat, Reset defaults) replaces a hand-tuned sound, that sound is stored on the effect (`FxSlot.Custom`, new optional field; old files load) and "Custom" in the list puts it back; with nothing stored, "Custom" does nothing and the box shows what the settings really are.

**(c) Preset manager.** The pull-down is: built-in presets, the user's own (marked with a star), Custom, "Manage presets...". The last row opens `Fx/Presets/PresetManagerWindow` (modal over the FX window) and the box never stays on it. Own presets are stored in presets.json (`Audio/UserPresetStore`, through `AppDataStore`), one list for every effect kind; a preset of one kind is offered only to that kind (16 and 32 bands differ in count; TabForge also keeps presets per kind). Built-ins stay in the catalog (`PresetCatalog` reads `FxKind.Eq` / `FxKind.Presets`); the manager and the pull-down read them, no copy. The manager has: kind list with counts, presets of the chosen kind (built-ins greyed with a "built-in" tag), Save current, Rename (button, F2, double-click, in place; a taken name asks "Replace it?"), Duplicate (built-ins too), Move up / Move down (Alt+Up/Down, as a block), Delete (at once, Undo for `Timing.PresetUndo`), Import... / Export... (a .json file per preset), search across every kind with Show in list (Enter), Ctrl+F, Ctrl+A, Esc. Mouse-first dialog (rule 5 exception); its buttons and keys also work from the keyboard. UPDATE 14 Nov: this window is gone; "Manage presets..." opens the shared preset manager at the effect's card (see "Unified preset manager").

**Where it differs from TabForge, and why**
- Modal dialog over the FX window; TabForge has one non-modal window for the whole program. Chosen so it is tied to the effect it was opened from (Save current needs that effect).
- TabForge saves from a separate "Save preset..." button beside each pull-down; here "Save current" is in the manager's toolbar (the owner asked for only one extra pull-down row).
- Kind tree: TabForge has groups with children (per plug-in); here a flat list of the four effects that have presets.
- No drag grip to reorder, no pencil and bin on hover in the pull-down itself (rename, delete and reorder are in the manager), no "Built-in" / "Yours" group headers in the pull-down (the star marks own presets), no " *" edited marker (an edit simply shows Custom or the matching preset), no remembered-folder dialogs, no per-row error line (one message line at the foot).
- The Undo is a line at the foot of the window, not a floating toast.
- Button tooltips are fixed words (TabForge changes them with the reason a button is disabled).
- Preset files are JSON of one `UserPreset`; TabForge's formats are its own.
- Limiter has no presets of any kind yet, so it has no pull-down (open item: a user-presets-only pull-down for it).
- Equaliser presets keep the bands only, not the Overall gain card.

**Owner test steps.** Open an FX window with a 32-band equaliser: pick Bass boost (sliders and curve move; the sound changes), drag a slider (box shows Custom), pick Vocal, pick Custom (your curve returns), close and reopen the window and restart the app (names right). Open the list: last row "Manage presets..." opens the manager and the box shows the real name again. Save current as "Mine", see it starred in the list; rename, duplicate, move, delete + Undo, export and import a file; check the same on the 16-band, the Parametric (presets of one are not listed for another) and the Compressor. Try the narrowest window and both themes.

**Not verified:** everything on screen (nothing was run); the ComboBox reset after the Manage row (done through a posted refresh); keyboard focus into the in-place name box; the file pickers; whether a pick still looks "broken" if the cause is the weak shapes.


## Full control: per-program effects (owner: "the per application EQ doesn't work, the master fx work fine") - compiled only (throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors); nothing run, heard or seen

**Trace and evidence.** The owner's `mixer.json`: sound mode "full"; Spotify `FxOn` true with one Parametric EQ (band 2 at 1 kHz, +24 dB, enabled: clearly audible if it ran), Helium `FxOn` true with an empty chain (nothing to run, so it follows the master), Claude effects off, master `MasterFxOn` true with an empty chain, `MasterPlaysOn` empty, no combined devices, the microphone's effects off, `BusCable` = "CABLE Input". `engine-journal.json` (written with it) held only Helium as routed (to the bus), so no Spotify cable run was active at that moment. The two "virtual cables" are VB-CABLE's "CABLE Input" and "CABLE In 16ch": the second is a second input of the SAME cable (no recording side of its own; both come out of "CABLE Output"). Plan for this file (`RoutePlanner.Make`): the bus takes "CABLE Input" (first by name) and plays on the Realtek speaker; Spotify, while it has a stream, gets "CABLE In 16ch" (Run kind `Cable`, output = the bus cable, so the master chain applies after its own); Helium and Claude are bus routes. The chain path (FX window -> `SaveFx`/`SetFxOn` -> mixer.json -> `ChainBuilder` -> `EqStage`) is the same code for the master and a program (key only differs) and the saved bands are right, so the EQ settings reach the engine.
- **Root cause found in code: nothing checked that Windows really moved the program.** `CableRoute` sets the program's per-app device (`HoldRouting`, Windows' undocumented per-app policy) and then said "Effects running" as soon as its recording of the cable started. Many programs (Chromium/Electron/CEF apps such as Spotify, Helium, Claude among them) keep their open stream where it was until they reopen it, so the program kept playing into Windows' default device, which in Full control is the bus cable: heard through the master bus, without its own chain, while the row claimed its effects ran. The master works because a default-device change is something every program follows. Not provable here (needs the owner's ears), but it matches "master works, per-app not" exactly.
- **Other defects on the same path:** (1) when the bus was given up by its watchdog, program runs kept playing into its cable, which nothing plays any more (silence) until a routing change; (2) a program whose own chosen device was a free cable could be given that same cable for its run (records and plays one cable: a loop); (3) a processed or combined microphone could be given a cable apps cannot record from (here "CABLE In 16ch", whose "Output" is CABLE's, so apps would hear the whole bus mixed with the microphone).

**As built**
- `CableRoute.Tick`: while the program is still heard on a device other than its cable (`MixerService.PeakOutside`, the program's own session meters, threshold `ProgramEngine.Audible`) for `Timing.CableMoveLimit` (3 s), the row shows (waiting, not running) "Windows has not moved the program to its cable yet, so it plays without effects: pause and play it, or restart it"; it goes back to "Effects running" by itself once only the cable carries it.
- `RoutePlanner`: `PlanInput.BusGivenUp` (the engine passes its failed master): no cable for the bus, no master run, programs that follow the master play on Windows' default (the real device again); `EffectsEngine.OnRunChanged` plans again right after the bus fails. `CableBox` keeps the cable records: `TakeAny(fits)`; a program's run never takes a cable among its own outputs; microphones and combined microphones only take `CableBox.Recordable` cables (with a recording side). Fast mode is untouched (only the Full-control branch changed).

**Depends on the cable count.** With the owner's VB-CABLE (2 inputs, 1 output): the bus + ONE processed program. A second program with effects says "Needs another virtual cable. Playing without effects." (cables go out in settings order). A processed microphone in Full control now says it needs another cable instead of using "CABLE In 16ch". Several processed programs need VB-CABLE A+B (or another cable driver).

**Owner test steps** (Full control, Wiring tab "Ready · 2 virtual cables")
1. Start Spotify and play music. Mixer tab: Spotify's effects link opens its FX window; the Effects switch is on, the equaliser has a +24 dB band. Within a few seconds the row says "Effects running · about N ms delay" and the boost is heard; Windows' Sound settings > Volume mixer shows Spotify on "CABLE In 16ch".
2. If instead the row says "Windows has not moved the program to its cable yet ...": pause and play Spotify (or close and reopen it). The row must change to "Effects running" and the EQ must be heard. Tell me which programs needed this.
3. Switch the Effects switch off and on: off = plain sound through the master, on = EQ again.
4. Give Helium an equaliser too while Spotify plays: Helium's row says "Needs another virtual cable. Playing without effects." (only one spare input).
5. Stop all sound and check the master row still says "Playing through the master bus".

**Open items**
- If pausing and playing does not move a program, a stronger move is needed (for example capturing that program like Fast mode does, which on this PC silences the capture when the program is muted). Decide after test step 2.
- "CABLE In 16ch" is counted as a virtual cable ("2 virtual cables") although it shares "CABLE Output" with "CABLE Input"; whether Windows plays both inputs at once and how its 16-channel loopback folds down to stereo is unverified. If test step 1 is silent or odd, the next fix is to count it only for recording-free runs or not at all.
- The Wiring cable count wording does not say that a cable without its own recording side serves only programs, the bus and combined speakers.

**Not verified:** nothing was run or heard; the session meter read on another device as the "not moved" sign; the 3 s limit against how long a move takes; the re-plan after a given-up bus.

## Full control: per-program effects, second pass with evidence (14 Nov) - headless tests 15/15 pass; Release compile 0 warnings, 0 errors; one owner-approved live experiment on a throwaway process only; no program moved, nothing played; the running app was not stopped

Owner after the first pass: "per app Fx still doesn't work in full control mode, only master Fx work", then the footer: "Helium: Effects stopped: Windows refused to send the program to the cable (Windows refused the change (0x80070057).). Playing without effects."

**Evidence (read only).** A throwaway probe on the running build's own `OmniControl.Core.dll` (scratchpad, deleted):
- Endpoints: playback `CABLE Input` {10207fab} (Windows' default, so the bus runs), `CABLE In 16ch` {1e2139d3}, `Speaker (Realtek)` {35486a40}; recording `CABLE Output` {cbe87cfe}, Stereo Mix, Microphone Array. All mix formats 48 kHz, 2 ch, float (the 16ch input too). `VirtualCables.Find` gives `CABLE` (play CABLE Input, record CABLE Output) first and `CABLE In 16ch` (no recording side) second, so the bus takes CABLE Input and ONE spare input is left for programs.
- Sessions: Spotify pid 1748 (its main process) and Helium pid 2340 on CABLE Input, both inactive (state 0, peak 0), Helium also inactive on Speaker. Helium's pid 2340 is `chrome.exe --type=utility --utility-sub-type=audio.mojom.AudioService --service-sandbox-type=audio`: a browser plays from a sandboxed audio-service process and keeps that session open while silent. OmniControl itself has an active silent session on CABLE Input (most likely the bus's loopback recording of that endpoint; not proven).
- `engine-journal.json` absent: no program was held by any run (Helium's run had failed and been undone).
- Per-app policy, read side only (`GetPersistedDefaultAudioEndpoint`, slot 26 of the same factory): S_OK for pids 1748, 2340, OmniControl and the probe; **0x80070057 for a process id that is not running** (999999). No program has a per-app device stored now. Windows' per-endpoint app store (`HKCU\...\Audio\PolicyConfig\PropertyStore`) has entries for Spotify and Helium on CABLE Input (`.../00010001`) and none for any program on CABLE In 16ch (`.../00010002`): no program has ever played on the 16ch input.
- Windows build 10.0.26300. The interface id `ab3d4648-...` and slot 25 are what Windows 11 uses (the factory activates with it); the device string is the RENDER endpoint's interface path (`\\?\SWD#MMDEVAPI#{0.0.0...}.{id}#{e6327cad-...}`), never the recording side.

**Headless tests** (`tests/OmniControl.Tests`, new): the planner on the owner's settings and endpoints, a sine through the exact chain a run builds, the process side of routing.
- Before the fix, `RoutePlanner.Make` on the owner's state (Helium and Spotify with open streams): `run master: Bus CABLE Input -> Speaker`, `run Helium: Cable CABLE In 16ch -> CABLE Input`, `block Spotify: "Needs another virtual cable. Playing without effects."`.
- After: with Spotify heard and Helium silent, `run Spotify: Cable CABLE In 16ch -> CABLE Input`, `block Helium: "Needs another virtual cable..."`; with Helium failed, Spotify gets the cable even while both play; the program holding the cable keeps it while both play; a pause does not restart the run; a given-up bus -> Spotify plays on Speaker by id; Fast mode unchanged (Capture runs, master to the APO, no cable). The owner's real `%APPDATA%\OmniControl\mixer.json` (read only) gives the same Spotify run.
- DSP: Spotify's saved chain through `ChainBuilder` + `ChainProcessor(level: null, gateOpen: true)` (as `CableRoute` and `BusRun` build it), -40 dBFS sine, Goertzel: **+24.00 dB at 1059 Hz, +23.88 dB at 1 kHz** (the editor's curve: +23.88); the master chain matches its curve at 100 Hz, 1 kHz, 2.5 kHz (+13.91, +0.35, -12.00); an empty or switched-off chain: 0.00 dB. The program chain is not the problem.

**Root causes.**
1. *Proven (planner, reproduced):* one spare cable went to the first program in settings order with any open stream. Helium comes first and a browser always keeps a silent stream open, so it took CABLE In 16ch and Spotify, the one playing, was told it needs another cable.
2. *Proven (code + the owner's footer):* a failed program run kept its cable in every new plan (the planner was not told about failures), so after Helium's run failed Spotify stayed blocked until the routing changed.
3. *Proven:* Helium's run failed because Windows answered 0x80070057 to the per-app call. *Suspected, not proven* (which argument Windows rejects): (a) Helium's sandboxed audio-service process for the set call (the read call accepts it), (b) CABLE In 16ch as a per-app target (no program has ever played there), (c) a process that had ended (proven to give exactly this code on the read side). The fix covers (a) and (c); the experiment below ruled out (b) and found (d): Windows refuses to move a process with no open stream, which a silent browser is.
4. *Found by the tests:* with a given-up bus a processed program played on "Windows' default", which is still the cable if giving the default back failed (a loop).
5. The status read "(Windows refused the change (0x80070057).)." (doubled stops) and was cut in the footer.
6. `ProcessTree.IsRunning` was unused and judged by opening the process (an ended process still opens while a handle to it exists).

**As built**
- `RoutePlanner`: `PlanInput` now has `Sounding` (sessions heard now, `MixerService.Running.IsPlaying`), `Holding` (programs with a cable run now) and `Failed` (rows whose run failed; the master there = `BusGivenUp`). Programs get spare cables in `InCableOrder` (heard and holding, heard, holding, the rest; settings order inside). A failed program gets no cable, no bus route and keeps its failure status. With no bus, a processed program that follows the master plays on `MasterDevice.Real` by id; none plugged in -> "No speakers are plugged in to play on".
- `EffectsEngine`: passes those sets; any failed run plans again at once (not only the bus).
- Core `CoreAudio.TrySetProgramDevice` (the one routing call; `TrySetAppDevice` is private now): skips processes that ended (`ProcessTree.IsRunning` = exit code), and when the process that plays is refused asks the topmost process of the same .exe (Windows keeps the choice per .exe). Used by `HoldRouting`, `ReleaseRouting`, `KeepRouted`, own devices and `EngineJournal`. Reasons have no final stop; callers end the sentence.
- Status: `EngineStatus.Detail` (Windows' answer) is shown only in the full line (`LineFor`: the row status tooltip and the FX window strip). `CableRoute` fails with "Effects stopped: Windows would not move it to its cable. Playing without effects." and detail "Windows refused the change, code 0x80070057"; the footer shows the short sentence.

**Live experiment (owner-approved, own process only; done).** A throwaway console program (same factory, slot 25/26 and device-path form as `CoreAudio.Routing.cs`) set the per-app device of ITS OWN process only, read it back, and cleared it; nothing else was touched and nothing played. Its output:
```
read back (no audio-service connection yet): console 0x80070057 | multimedia 0x80070057
read-only session enumeration: 5 sessions listed
read back:                       console 0x00000000 '' | multimedia 0x00000000 ''
set CABLE In 16ch:               console 0x80070057, multimedia 0x80070057   read back 0x00000000 ''
set CABLE Input:                 console 0x80070057, multimedia 0x80070057   read back 0x00000000 ''
clear (null):                    console 0x00000000, multimedia 0x00000000   read back 0x00000000 ''
registry PropertyStore before -> after: no difference
```
Afterwards: no entry for the throwaway .exe in `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Internet Explorer\LowRegistry\Audio\PolicyConfig\PropertyStore` (25 keys, none naming it); the program was deleted. Without COM initialised first, every call answered 0x80070057 too (first two runs).
- **Suspect 3b is ruled out as stated:** CABLE In 16ch is refused exactly like CABLE Input, so the 16ch input is not singled out and stays a usable cable for a program run (its loopback carrying a program's sound is still unverified).
- **What the refusal depends on (proven for this test process):** Windows answers 0x80070057 to "move process X to device D" when X has no audio stream of its own, for every device, while clearing is accepted. A process unknown to the audio service is refused even for reading. Together with Helium's audio process holding only a closed (inactive) session while silent, the most likely reading of the owner's failure is that Helium's run started while Helium was silent. *Not proven* for a process WITH an open stream (that would need the test process to open a stream, outside the approved scope), and the topmost-process fallback could not be tried without touching Helium: it needs the owner's test (steps below).
- **Change from it:** a program's cable run now starts only while the program is heard (`PlanInput.Sounding`, a started stream) and is kept while it pauses (`Holding`); an open but silent stream waits ("Starts when the program plays") instead of being moved and failing. Tests: 15/15 pass (two added: a silent program is not moved even with a free cable; two heard programs, the second needs another cable).

**Owner test steps** (Full control; Wiring tab top bar says Full control is ready)
1. Leave Helium open and silent. Play music in Spotify. Volume > Mixer: within about 2 s Spotify's row says "Effects running · about N ms delay" and the +24 dB band is heard (a loud nasal tone around 1 kHz); Helium's row says "Starts when the program plays". Wiring tab: Spotify's Cable column shows CABLE In 16ch.
2. If Spotify's row says "Windows has not moved the program to its cable yet ...": pause and play Spotify once; it must change to "Effects running".
3. If Spotify's row says "Effects stopped: Windows would not move it to its cable. Playing without effects.": hover it and send me the end of the tooltip ("Windows refused the change, code 0x..."). Then Windows refuses even a program that is playing, and the capture-and-replay option below is the way.
4. Pause Spotify and play a video in Helium: Spotify keeps the cable (Helium says "Needs another virtual cable. Playing without effects."). Close Spotify while the video plays: Helium gets CABLE In 16ch and its own -24 dB cut around 700 Hz must be heard (this is the test of the top-process fallback for a browser), or its row gives the same tooltip as in step 3.
5. Fast mode: switch to Fast and play Spotify: behaves as before (recorded and replayed).

**Open items**
- If Windows still refuses a playing program (step 3 or 4), there are two ways, the owner decides: (1) install a second independent cable pair (VB-CABLE A+B, each with its own Input and Output) - same mechanism, still a per-app move; or (2) process programs in Full control the way Fast mode does (record the program by process loopback, silence its session, play the processed copy into the bus cable): no extra cable and no per-app move at all, works for any number of programs, but depends on the capture staying alive while the original is silenced (Fast mode's Test).
- Programs without effects that follow the master are still sent to the bus cable by a per-app move even while silent (`BusRun.SetRouted`, unchanged); if Windows refuses that, the footer says "Couldn't send ... to the master's cable" once per program. Harmless (the default device already is the bus cable) but noisy; gate it on sound too if it shows up.
- Pause and play of a program that loses or gets a cable moves it again; Chromium programs may play dry until their next stream (the "not moved" status says so).
- Whether the topmost-process fallback is what Windows accepts for a browser was not seen.

**Not verified:** nothing was played, heard, moved or seen; the per-app SET call was made only on the throwaway test process (never on a program); whether Windows accepts it for a program that is playing; the top-process fallback for Helium; the loopback of CABLE In 16ch carrying a program; the "not moved" detection; the OmniControl session on CABLE Input being the bus's loopback.
## Thick sliders in the FX editors (2026-11-14, mock-up `docs/mockups/eq-thick-sliders-a.html`)

Graphic EQ band sliders unchanged. EQ gain column widened to 56 px, readout no longer ellipsised.
Differences from the mock-up (nothing tested on screen): signed sliders (`.centered`) fill with the accent between 0 and the knob through `Controls/SliderCenterFill` (an overlay inserted under the template's track: grey base plus fill, the buttons' own track made transparent; colours are the theme brushes `BrushHoverStrong`/`BrushAccent`, size `SliderThickTrack`; origin = value 0 within Minimum/Maximum, either orientation; fill ends are rounded at the zero point too; `IsDirectionReversed` is not handled; unverified on screen: that the overlay lines up with the Fluent track in both orientations and the knob travel maths); the knob ring uses `BrushBackground`, so it differs slightly from the card colour on a hovered or selected card. Unverified: knob clipping at narrowest window and Scale 0.7, light theme, ring appearance.

### Design change (same day): the parametric EQ's vertical slider is the OVERALL gain
`EqPanelItem.OverallGain` (the effect's own "Overall gain" `SliderItem`, set in `FxSlotViewModel.BuildEq`) drives the vertical slider beside the graph (whole dB, its range, reset and live curve redraw as before); its readout is the full-width `ValueText`, no ellipsis. The Parametric editor no longer lists the separate "Overall gain" card (the graphic EQs keep theirs; the Parameters view still shows it). The selected band's Gain is a horizontal thick row again under Frequency and Bandwidth in `EqBandRow` (`ShowGain` removed; greyed without a tooltip for types without gain). Removed the unused `EqBandItem.GainSmallChange/GainLargeChange`. The vertical slider has no number-typing box (SliderItem value only). Unverified on screen.

- **Picker "Back" button (14 Nov):** the settings page of the master picker has a `Button.pill` "Back" at the bottom left (`PickerModel.CanGoBack`, shown only when the page was opened from the list, hidden when the window opened straight on the settings). It is `CloseOptions`: nothing is chosen, the window stays, search text, list selection and category are untouched. Settings changed on the page were already applied live (principle 2) and stay applied. Like "Use this ..." it is mouse-only (not a Tab stop, the picker keeps one focus holder); keyboard: Esc (as before) or Alt+Left. Not seen on screen.

## Combining microphones, option A + S1-S6 (14 Nov) - headless tests 28/28 pass; Release compile 0 warnings, 0 errors (throwaway build, deleted); nothing run, heard, recorded or seen; the running app was not stopped

Design: `docs/design-combine-microphones.md` (option A, shared fixes S1-S6, owner's decisions: combined microphones allowed in Fast, the master bus always wins the cable in Full control, Stereo Mix allowed with a caution tag, no monitoring yet, member level 0-200 %). Mock-up: `docs/mockups/combine-microphones-a.html`.

**As built, per work item**
1. *Pair grouping (S2).* `VirtualCable.PairId` (Core `VirtualCables.Find`): an input of a driver with exactly one recording side ("CABLE In 16ch (VB-Audio Virtual Cable)") belongs to that side's pair; `VirtualCables.Pairs` lists the pairs. `CableBox` (planner): a microphone's run (`TakeRecordable`, `TakeRemembered(..., recording: true)`) takes a cable only while its whole pair is free and then holds every input of the pair (listed in `CablesInUse`, so no program or menu gets "CABLE In 16ch" while a microphone records "CABLE Output"). Superseded the same day (see "third pass, live evidence" below): every consumer takes the whole pair, because "CABLE In 16ch" was measured unusable while "CABLE Input" is in use. "CABLE In 16ch" is still never given to a microphone (no recording side of its own).
2. *Planner (S1, S3) and gate split.* Fast mode now gives recordable cables to combined microphones (remembered first) and plans them with `PlanMic`; combined speakers stay "Needs Full control mode: combined speakers run through a virtual cable". Full control takes the master bus's cable FIRST, then remembered cables (fault 1 gone: a remembered microphone cable never takes the bus's place). A microphone blocked while the bus holds the only recordable pair gets the status "Needs another virtual cable" with the detail `SoundModes.BusHoldsCable` ("The master bus uses CABLE. In Fast mode it is free."). `SoundFeature.CombineDevices` is split into `CombineSpeakers` (Full only) and `CombineMicrophones` (open in both). Programs, the bus and Fast mode's APO rows are planned exactly as before (the 15 earlier tests pass unchanged).
3. *Drift reader (S4).* `Core/Engine/Audio/DriftReader.cs` = the old `ExtraOutput` logic in one place (resampler nudged by the ring level, trim past `PipeMaxQueueMs`, prefill). `ExtraOutput` uses it (same target, same behaviour); every capture of an `AudioPipe` is now read through one too, target `CoreTiming.PipeCaptureDriftTargetMs` = the old prefill (20 ms), so no delay is added. This applies to every capture run (Fast mode's programs, Full control's cables and bus, microphones): before, a capture that drifted trimmed (a jump) or ran dry (a gap); now it is resampled by at most 0.5 %. The resampler is NAudio's `WdlResampler` in the mode extra devices already used (linear interpolation with a light low-pass): whether that low-pass is heard on captured programs is unverified.
4. *Member levels (S5).* `CombinedDevice.MemberLevel` (percent by endpoint id, absent = 100) and `MemberMuted` beside `Members`, so old mixer.json files load unchanged (test 8). Limits `Percent.BoostMax` = 200, `ClampBoost`, `ToGain`. `MixerService.SetMemberLevel` (saves, `ProgramChanged`), `ToggleMember`, `EditCombined` drops settings of removed members. `AudioPipe.SetSourceGain` (a float per source read by the playback thread); `MicRun` pushes the gains when its pipe has started and on every change (no restart).
5. *Wiring.* Microphones table = recording devices AND combined microphones (`SyncWiring`); the "Combined devices" group is now "Combined speakers" (stored key kept) and holds speakers only. Header: "+ Combine microphones" (`VolumeGroup.Tool`, `Button.pill.cardaction.main`, `Tag="grouptool"` so Up/Down reaches it), the count beside the name. Real microphone rows: "This microphone" + dashed `Button.blockadd.small` "Combine with..."; its menu (`FlyoutPresenter.listmenu` of `Button.menurow`) lists every other microphone (picking one opens the window with both ticked) and "Add to <combined microphone>" for each one it is not in (added at once). Combined microphone rows: icon + name + `Border.tag` "Combined" (`VolumeRow.SourceTag`, shown by `SourceName`), one `Button.chipx` per member (not plugged in: "(not plugged in)") and "+ add" (menu: recording devices not in it, then "Rename..." which opens the window for its name and members). Cable column: the cable name (tooltip "Apps record it from CABLE Output"), or the caution tag with `Button.link.small` "Switch to Fast" (only in Full control when the bus holds the pair; tooltip = the reason) and the step pill (More cables / Install VB-CABLE, `.main`). The × removes it and asks "Remove <name>? Apps using CABLE Output hear nothing."
6. *Window.* `CombineStart` (kind fixed by the opener: the kind chips are hidden; devices already ticked; suggested name "Combined microphone", numbered when taken, selected so typing replaces it). One-line hint per kind ("Mixes them into one microphone. Apps record it from CABLE Output." / "Plays the same sound on all of them."), a tick note only while too few are ticked ("Tick at least one more microphone"). The cable line is `CombineCableLine`, asked again when the kind changes or after Switch to Fast: "<cable> is ready for it." / "The master bus uses CABLE. In Fast mode it is free." + Switch to Fast + More cables / "Needs VB-CABLE: it waits until a cable is free." + Install VB-CABLE. The top bar "+ Combine devices" and the search card are no longer dimmed in Fast mode: the window opens on Microphones there and its Speakers chip is dimmed with the reason.
7. *Member pop-down.* `MemberLevelMenu` + `MemberLevelView`: clicking a member chip's words opens a listmenu with its name (`TextBlock.menutext`), `Slider.compact` 0-200 % (double-click resets to 100 via `SliderReset`; wheel via `ValueWheel`; typing via `NumberText`) and `Button.pill` "Mute" (`.custom` while muted); live. Pressing on the chip's × takes the member away (the last one stays). The pop-down hangs on a Panel around the chip, not on the chip, so `Button.chipx` styles do not reach into it.
8. *Wording and Stereo Mix.* `SoundModes`: `AppsRecordFrom` (running status, cable tooltip, window hint), `BusHoldsCable`, `NoMicrophonePluggedIn`, `RecordsPlaybackTag` / `RecordsPlaybackTip`. Core `LoopbackInputs` = the one list of "records what the PC plays" names (Stereo Mix and its translations, What U Hear, Wave Out Mix): the caution tag in both menus and the member chip's tooltip.
9. *Hotkeys and search.* `volume.combomic.mute`, `volume.combomic.up`, `volume.combomic.down` (level id `combomic`, so the wheel gestures offer it) with a device source over combined microphones (all or a pick; OSD = the Microphone element with the combined microphone's name). Search: the "Combine devices" entry has "mics mix stereo mix" added; the Wiring rows' keywords "combine combined mix mics"; the cards' heading is "Combined speakers".

**Differences from the mock-up (and why)**
- Cable cell words: the mock-up has "Ready: apps record CABLE Output" / "Needs a free virtual cable" / "Full control: the master bus uses the cable". Built with the design document's S6 wording and the existing shared constants: the cable's name as the tag with "Apps record it from CABLE Output" as tooltip, and "Needs another virtual cable" (one word for one idea across every row) with "The master bus uses CABLE. In Fast mode it is free." as tooltip.
- The "Switch to Fast" link and "More cables" pill sit under the caution tag in the Cable column (the mock-up shows them as links inside the tag text); "Switch to Fast" shows only in Full control while the bus holds the pair.
- The combined row has no accent edge: Wiring rows all use the master `ListBox.rows.compact` look; the "Combined" tag marks it (no per-row style, AGENTS.md master designs).
- The pill is `Button.pill.cardaction.main` (the brief's master), close to the mock-up's accent outline.
- Extra beyond the mock-up: "Rename..." at the end of a combined microphone's "+ add" menu (the design asks for rename via the window; rows have no Edit pill).

**Skipped / open items**
- The Stereo Mix caution tag is not shown in the Combine devices window's ticked list (`Controls/OptionList` has no tag slot; adding one is a change to a shared control): the menus and chip tooltip carry it.
- Member chips are mouse only (`Button.chipx` is not focusable, as on Outputs): a member's level and mute can't be reached from the keyboard (AGENTS.md rule 5 exception, pop-downs); the combined microphone's own level has hotkeys. Owner may overrule.
- A muted or changed member does not show its level on the chip (not asked for).
- In Fast mode a program whose "plays on" is CABLE Input while a combined microphone also plays into it would mix into the microphone; the menus no longer offer a cable a microphone holds, but an existing choice is not undone.
- The row's mode line for a combined microphone in Fast mode uses the mode's "Program" method words ("by recording"), which is what happens (recorded and replayed into the cable) but does not name the cable.
- Monitoring ("hear the combined microphone") left for later as decided.
- Drift test: an extra device holds 0.5 % either way; a capture (20 ms target) holds 0.5 % fast and 0.2 % slow (a real crystal drifts ~0.01 %); 0.5 % slow would need a larger target, i.e. more delay on every capture.

**Not verified (nothing can be heard or seen here):** that two microphones really mix and reach CABLE Output; the AMD array's and Realtek's real drift and that resampling every capture is inaudible (the low-pass of the resampler on programs in Fast mode); Windows' conversion of a mono or 16 kHz headset; the pop-down, chips, header pill and Cable column layout at 720 px and in both themes; Switch to Fast from inside the window; the hotkeys' OSD.

**Owner test steps**
1. Volume > Wiring in Fast mode: the Microphones header shows "Microphones 2 devices" and "+ Combine microphones" at the right. Each microphone row says "This microphone" and a dashed "Combine with...".
2. On "Microphone Array" click "Combine with..." > "Stereo Mix" (yellow tag "Records the PC's sound"). The window opens titled "Combine microphones", both ticked, name "Combined microphone" selected; the hint names CABLE Output and the cable line says "CABLE is ready for it." Create.
3. The new row "Combined microphone [Combined]" shows two chips and "+ add"; the Cable column shows CABLE. Mixer > Microphones row says "Apps record it from CABLE Output · about N ms delay".
4. In Discord (or Windows' Sound > Recording, level meter) pick "CABLE Output (VB-Audio Virtual Cable)"; speak and play a video: both are heard. Click the "Stereo Mix" chip: the pop-down's Mute takes the video out; the slider changes its level live; double-click resets to 100 %.
5. Click the × part of a chip: the member goes (the last one cannot). "+ add" brings it back. "+ add" > "Rename..." opens the window.
6. Switch to Full control: the row's Cable column says "Needs another virtual cable" (tooltip: the master bus uses CABLE), with "Switch to Fast" and "More cables". Switch to Fast: it runs again.
7. Shortcuts page: "Toggle combined microphone mute" and "Combined microphone level up/down" exist with an "All combined microphones" chip.
8. Leave a call running 30 minutes and listen for clicks; check both themes and the narrowest window.

## Combined speakers move into Outputs (14 Nov) - headless tests 28/28 pass; Release compile 0 warnings, 0 errors (throwaway build, deleted); nothing run or seen; the running app was not stopped

Owner's request: the separate "Combined speakers" group goes; Outputs gets what Microphones got. Done through the same code path with the kind as the only difference.

Real speaker rows and their "Plays on" cell are unchanged.

## Full control: per-program effects, third pass with live evidence (14 Nov) - tests 31/31 pass; Release compile 0 warnings, 0 errors; owner-approved live tests on a throwaway test process only (deleted); no program moved, the default device, volumes and the running app untouched

Owner after the second pass: "per app FX still does not work in Full control"; footer: "Couldn't send Helium to the master's cable: Windows refused the change, code 0x80070057." The owner approved bounded live tests on a throwaway process: its own quiet 1 kHz tone (peak 0.05, at most about 10 s, on the default device = the bus), moving ITS OWN process, recording the cable with the engine's capture settings, the engine's chain on that recording, then clearing.

**State read at the time (read only).** Default playback = CABLE Input. Sessions: Spotify pid 1748 and Helium's audio-service pid 2340 (root 5280) on CABLE Input, both inactive; OmniControl pid 16244 on CABLE Input and Speaker. No per-app device stored for any program. `engine-journal.json` = Helium routed (by the bus). Real mixer.json: Full control, Spotify FxOn with an enabled equaliser, Helium FxOn with an EMPTY chain (so it follows the master: a bus route), a combined microphone remembering CABLE Input. Plan for that state: bus on CABLE Input -> Speaker, Spotify "Starts when the program plays" (silent), Helium a bus route.

**Live results (raw)**
```
own process (testhost.exe), default = CABLE Input; recordings with the engine's settings (float 48 kHz 2 ch, 20 ms)
recording CABLE In 16ch (loopback), any format:  AudioDeviceInUseException 0x8889000A "already in exclusive use"
recording CABLE Input (loopback) / CABLE Output: OK (float, pcm16 and mix format)
A  tone on the default device: own session on 'CABLE Input' state=1 peak 0.050; 1 kHz at -26.0 dBFS in CABLE Input loopback and CABLE Output
-- with the old path (...991e976a79d4):
MOVE own PLAYING process to CABLE Input:   False 'Windows refused the change, code 0x80070057'; read back ''
MOVE own PLAYING process to CABLE In 16ch: False 'Windows refused the change, code 0x80070057'; read back ''
-- with the render interface class (...991e976a79d2):
MOVE own PLAYING process to CABLE Input:   True ''; read back '\\?\SWD#MMDEVAPI#{0.0.0.00000000}.{10207fab-...}#{e6327cad-dcec-4949-ae8a-991e976a79d2}'
MOVE own PLAYING process to CABLE In 16ch: True ''; read back '\\?\SWD#MMDEVAPI#{0.0.0.00000000}.{1e2139d3-...}#{e6327cad-...-991e976a79d2}'
B  the stream opened before the move stays on 'CABLE Input' (state=1, peak 0.050): an open stream is not moved
C  a new stream opened on the default device after the move: AudioDeviceInUseException (0x8889000A): Windows sent it to CABLE In 16ch, which could not be opened
ENGINE CHAIN (ChainBuilder + ChainProcessor, Parametric +24 dB at 1 kHz) on the real CABLE Output recording: dry -26.0 dBFS, processed -2.0 dBFS, gain +24.0 dB
CLEAR own setting: True; read back ''
```
WAVs of an earlier run (dry -30.7 dBFS, processed -6.7 dBFS, +24.0 dB) were written to the session scratchpad only. Afterwards the per-app store held one entry for the test host's own .exe on "CABLE In 16ch"; it was removed (only that one); nothing else names a test program.

**Root causes (proven)**
1. **Wrong device path in the per-app call:** `CoreAudio.Routing.cs` built the endpoint path with `{e6327cad-dcec-4949-ae8a-991e976a79d4}`; DEVINTERFACE_AUDIO_RENDER is `...79d2` (also what EarTrumpet uses). Windows answered 0x80070057 to every move of every program to every device (proven above with a playing process) and accepted only "clear" (no path). So OmniControl never moved any program: program runs failed ("Windows would not move it to its cable") and every bus route failed (the footer line). The master worked because it changes Windows' default device, a different call.
2. **"CABLE In 16ch" is unusable while "CABLE Input" is in use:** recording it by loopback and playing into it both fail with 0x8889000A, in every format. The planner gave it to program runs; after fix 1 a program moved there would have lost its sound. Why the driver refuses (one shared pin for both inputs) is suspected, not proven.
3. **Two writers of a program's device (proven by reading the code; regression test added):** when a program that followed the master gets effects, `Reconcile` starts its `CableRoute` (holds it on its cable) and only then calls `BusRun.SetRouted`, whose `ReleaseRouting` removed the run's hold, moved the program back to the default (the bus's cable) and forgot the run's journal entry.
4. The second pass's reading "Windows refuses a process with no stream" was a consequence of cause 1 (every set failed). The sounding gate stays (a silent program needs no cable).

**As built**
- `CoreAudio.RenderInterfacePath` (render interface class `...79d2`, defined once), used by `TrySetAppDevice`.
- `RoutePlanner.CableBox`: a cable pair is ONE cable for every consumer (taking any input holds the whole pair). With one VB-CABLE the bus holds it and a processed program says "Needs another virtual cable. Playing without effects." `TakeRemembered` lost its `recording` flag.
- `App/Audio/RoutingHolds.cs` (new, pure): the device a program is held on is the holder's claim; `MixerService.ReleaseRouting(key, cable)` lets go only while the program is still held on the caller's cable and says whether it did; `BusRun` and `CableRoute` forget their journal entry only then.
- Tests: `ProgramRoutingTests` (path regression, late bus release), `RoutePlannerTests` (one-pair truth for the owner's PC; scenarios on the same PC with a second pair "CABLE-A", `OwnerPc.*TwoPairs`; no run ever on "CABLE In 16ch"), `CombinedMicrophoneTests` one assertion updated (Spotify no longer gets 16ch). 31/31.

**What this means for the owner (Full control, one VB-CABLE):** the master works; per-program effects cannot work through cables on this PC, because the only usable cable is the bus's. The program's row now says so. Two ways to get them; the owner decides:
1. Install a second independent cable pair (VB-CABLE A+B: "CABLE-A Input/Output"). The planner then gives it to the program that plays. The program must still reopen its stream after the move (an open stream is not moved: proven above), which the "not moved" status reports.
2. Capture and replay in Full control: record the program by process loopback (as Fast mode does), silence its session, play the processed copy into the bus's cable. No extra cable and no per-app move; any number of programs; depends on the recording surviving the silencing (Fast mode's Test).

**Behaviour change to know:** bus routes now really move programs that follow the master (Helium, Claude) to CABLE Input per app while Full control runs (before, every move failed). They are moved back (cleared) when released; the journal repairs a crash.

**Owner test steps** (after the next build, Full control)
1. Play Spotify: its row says "Needs another virtual cable. Playing without effects."; the master's effects still work; the footer no longer shows "Couldn't send Helium to the master's cable".
2. Windows Settings > System > Sound > Volume mixer: while Full control runs, Helium and Claude show CABLE Input as their output; switch to Fast mode: they show "Default" again.
3. With a second cable pair installed: play Spotify; within a few seconds its row says "Effects running · about N ms delay" (pause and play once if it says Windows has not moved it yet) and the +24 dB is heard.

**Not verified:** a real program (Spotify, Helium) being moved with the fixed path, and whether Chromium-based programs reopen their stream after it (an explicit-device stream does not move: proven); the topmost-process fallback for Helium; any program run end to end on a second cable pair (none on this PC); why VB-CABLE refuses the 16ch input.

## Full control: Capture and replay for programs, and the per-program route choice (14 Nov) - tests 43/43 pass; Release compile 0 warnings, 0 errors (throwaway build, deleted); live tests on throwaway test processes only (deleted); no real program, volume, default device or the running app touched

Owner's choice: per-app effects in Full control with NO extra cable, by recording the program (as Fast mode does), making its original inaudible, and playing the processed copy into the master bus. Owner's addition: the user picks per program how its effects run (Automatic, Virtual cable, Recording).
