**The hard part: silencing the original without silencing the recording.** Candidates, each with its evidence:
- (a) *Send the original to an endpoint nobody hears.* PROVEN impossible on this PC: the render endpoints are CABLE Input (Active, the bus records it), CABLE In 16ch (Active, but unusable while CABLE Input is in use: a stream sent there fails with 0x8889000A, measured in the third pass, so the program has no sound to record), Speaker (Realtek) (Active, heard), Headphone (Realtek) (Unplugged), three "Digital Output (AMD)" (NotPresent). Muting a real endpoint would mute the bus's own output on it too. Not built.
- (b)/(c) *Mute or volume 0 with process loopback (include or exclude tree).* PROVEN to silence the recording (owner's earlier Test with PowerShell, and here: mute and volume 0 gave exact digital silence in the recording). Exclude-tree mode records everything BUT the program, so it cannot record the program; not tried.
- (d) **Turn the original down instead of off, and make the recording up ("Quiet"). PROVEN to work.** Process loopback is taken after the session's volume, linearly (a scalar of 1e-4 = exactly -80.00 dB); float keeps the turned-down recording exact, so a gain of 1e4 brings it back; the original stays in the mix 80 dB below the processed copy.

Raw output, throwaway program playing its own 1 kHz sine (peak 0.05 = -26.02 dBFS) on Windows' default (CABLE Input, the bus's cable); a second throwaway process (not a child) records it by process loopback through the running build's own `OmniControl.Core.AudioPipe` (`PipeSource.Program`, source gain 1/v, a safety clamp at 0.06 that was never hit) and plays the result into CABLE Input; that second process also records CABLE Input by loopback (what the bus hears). The session volume was set FROM THE OTHER PROCESS, as OmniControl does.
```
replay: set the test process's session volume to 0.0001 from this process
replay: gain 10000 (1/v, v = 0.0001); safety clamp hits 0; AudioPipe.DelayMs min 59 max 70 mean 65
dry v=1            : process capture -26.02 dBFS | endpoint -26.02 dBFS (SNR 84.5)      (x3)
v=0.0001 no replay : process capture -106.02 dBFS (SNR 147.5) | endpoint (the leak) -104.99 dBFS
v=0.0001 + replay  : process capture -106.02 dBFS | endpoint -26.03 dBFS (SNR 38.3), peak 0.0500
set_by_other_process_readback=0.0001
stream restart     : restart_kept=0.0001   (x3: the program closed its stream and opened a new one; Windows kept the volume)
after restarts     : endpoint -26.04 dBFS (SNR 45.1)
onset at the endpoint after Play(): dry 131.9, 132.6, 126.9 ms | replayed 218.1, 222.5, 218.4 ms
added delay (mean replayed - mean dry): 89.2 ms
```
A repeat with the volume set by the program itself gave the same levels and 85.9 ms. The session-volume law at other values (the recording scaled once): 0.75 -> -2.50 dB, 0.3 -> -10.46 dB, 0.05 -> -26.02 dB, 0.02 -> -33.98 dB, 0.01 -> -40.00 dB (measured inside one process, where its own recording was scaled a second time by the same session: -31.02, -46.94, -78.06, -93.98, -106.02 dBFS = exactly twice those; a mute and volume 0 gave digital silence; recording SNR 145-153 dB at every level). The session meter (`IAudioMeterInformation`) read 0.050 at every volume and under mute: it is taken BEFORE the session volume, so `IsPlaying` / `PeakOutside` are unaffected.
- **Mistake in the first end-to-end run (told honestly):** its make-up assumed the doubled in-process law, so the replay was 40 dB too loud and was held by the probe's safety clamp: a clipped 1 kHz tone at about -22 dBFS played into the bus (so on the speaker) for about 10 s. Nothing else was affected.
- Measured limits: the original stays in the mix at -80 dB under the processed copy (inaudible: -105 dBFS for a -26 dBFS program); the recorded path adds about 86-89 ms (the pipe estimates 59-70 ms); in Full control the bus adds its own delay after that, as for every program. The replay of a pure tone came back with a residual of -38 to -45 dB (SNR), against 84.5 dB for the dry path: the capture's drift resampler (`DriftReader`, NAudio's WDL resampler in linear mode, used by every capture run since the combine-microphones job) is the suspect (SUSPECTED, not measured alone); it applies to Fast mode, cable runs and the bus as well. Open item below.
- Clean-up: the per-app store (`HKCU\...\Audio\PolicyConfig\PropertyStore`) had one entry per probe .exe (Windows remembers a program's volume); the three were removed, nothing else touched. The running OmniControl recorded every probe as a program row in `mixer.json` (OcCaptureProbe, OcE2EProbe, OcProgProbe, OcProgProbe2, OcReplayProbe, plus testhost from the third pass): harmless, not edited while the app runs; remove them from the Volume page (or tell me to remove them from the file while the app is stopped).

**As built**
- Core: `AudioSession.Scalar` / `SetScalar` (the volume finer than a percent); `AudioPipe.SetSourceGain(index, gain, rawCeiling)`: a block of a source whose recorded samples go above the ceiling is left out (`AudioPipe.MixInto`), so a make-up of +80 dB can never amplify full-level sound (the session not turned down yet, a program or the user raising it in Windows' mixer). Gains go through 0 so the playback thread never pairs a large gain with no ceiling.
- `SilenceMethod.Quiet` + `QuietLevel` (`MixerService.EngineHolds.cs`, the one rule: `Scalar` 1e-4, `Makeup` 1e4, `RawCeiling` 4e-4, `MakeupFor`, `CeilingFor`, `KeepsSound`). `KeepSilenced` holds the scalar (new streams too). `Unsilence` returns false when the program closed while silenced; the run then keeps its journal entry (Windows keeps the closed program's volume; the next start, or its next stream through the mixer's saved values, puts it back).
- `ProgramEngine` (both modes): make-up and ceiling set the moment the original is silenced and again on every new pipe; the check uses `KeepsSound` (for Quiet: sound AND turned down; a recording that stays loud fails with "the recording did not follow the program's volume (press Test to try the other ways)"); a run that started before its program had a stream now starts listening when one opens (it never did before). Running words "Effects running by recording" (`SoundModes.RunningByRecording`); cable runs say "Effects running through a cable".
- `CaptureTestResult`: `quiet` added; the failure is now stored as `none`; untested and the old `failed` (written before the quiet way existed) use Quiet. `CaptureTest` tries mute, volume 0, then quiet (silent ways first) and stores the first that works; its message names the way. The row's Test link shows in Full control too, unless the program is set to Virtual cable.
- **The route choice (data and planner, no picker yet):** `AppAudioEntry.FxRoute` ("" = Automatic, "cable", "capture"; old files load as Automatic, test), `SoundModes.ProgramRoutes` (`RouteAutomatic` "Automatic" / `RouteCable` "Virtual cable" / `RouteCapture` "Recording", each with a one-line description and a tooltip, `RouteOf`), `MixerService.FxRouteOf(key)` / `SetFxRoute(key, route)` (saves, plans again, clears failures). `RoutePlanner.PlanFullProgram` states THE RULE once: Virtual cable = a whole free pair or "Needs another virtual cable. Playing without effects."; Recording = always `RunKind.Capture`, never a cable; Automatic = a free pair first, else Capture; a recording in progress keeps recording while it plays or pauses (`PlanInput.Capturing`). A Capture run plays on the program's own devices, else into the bus's cable (the master chain applies after), else (bus given up) the real speakers by id. It sets no device, so `RoutingHolds` has no second writer; the original stays on its own device (`Direct`), 80 dB down.
- Wording: Full control's program method "through a cable or by recording"; its details, tag and status tooltip no longer say a program needs a cable; Fast mode says it silences (not mutes) the original; "Recording stops when the program is turned down here" when no way works.
- Tests (`tests/OmniControl.Tests`, 43): planner on the owner's one-pair PC: Automatic -> Spotify recorded into CABLE Input (no cable), Helium silent waits; Virtual cable -> "Needs another virtual cable"; Recording never takes a pair even when one is free; with a second pair Automatic prefers the cable; a recording keeps recording when a pair frees, and takes the cable after its run ended; play / pause (kept) / close (waits) / play again (same run, the bus never restarts); bus given up -> Automatic takes the freed pair, Recording plays on the Speaker; recording impossible -> the row says why; Helium without effects stays a bus route. `QuietSilenceTests`: stored results, the check, make-up exact to 1e-8, full-level sound never made up. Updated for the new default: four planner tests and two combined-microphone tests (Spotify is now recorded instead of blocked).

**For the UI job (not built here):** bind a picker of `SoundModes.ProgramRoutes` (Name as the choice, Description as its line, Tip as tooltip) to `MixerService.FxRouteOf(key)` / `SetFxRoute(key, route)`, through the program row `Pages/Volume/MixerItem` (a new property, e.g. `FxRoute`, kept in step like `CanTest` in `VolumePageViewModel.Routing.cs`) and/or the FX window's Options menu beside "Plays on" (`FxWindowViewModel`, radio items like `OutputChoice`). Show it only in Full control (in Fast every program records). A global default is not needed: Automatic is the default and needs no cable on this PC.

**Owner test steps** (after the next build; Full control, VB-CABLE as now)
1. Play music in Spotify (its equaliser has +24 dB at 1 kHz). Within about 2 s Spotify's row says "Effects running by recording · about N ms delay" and the boost is heard. Hover the status: "Full control mode · effects through a cable or by recording · Effects running by recording ...".
2. Windows Settings > System > Sound > Volume mixer: Spotify shows volume 0 (it is held at 0.01 %, -80 dB) and its output is "Default" (CABLE Input). OmniControl's own Spotify slider keeps your level and acts on the processed sound.
3. Switch Spotify's effects off: the row clears, Windows' mixer shows Spotify at your volume again, plain sound through the master. On again: step 1 again.
4. Pause Spotify for 10 s and play again: the effects continue without a restart. Close Spotify while playing and reopen it: its row says "Starts when the program plays", then running again; Windows' mixer must never leave Spotify at 0 while OmniControl's effects for it are off.
5. Press Test on Spotify's row while it plays: it must say "It works on this PC: the recording kept the sound after turning the original down to near silence. Extra delay about N ms ...".
6. Helium with no effects plays through the master as before. Give Helium an equaliser too: both run by recording at once (no cable needed).
7. Fast mode: Spotify's effects now run there too ("Effects running by recording"); before, the recording went silent.
8. Listen for an echo or flanging (the original 80 dB down under the copy should be inaudible) and for a dull or grainy top end on a pure tone (the resampler open item below).

**Open items**
- The UI for the route choice (queued as its own job).
- The drift resampler's residual on a replayed pure tone (-38 to -45 dB): measure `DriftReader` alone with a tone and, if it is the cause, use a better interpolation (it affects every capture run in both modes).
- If a program or the user sets the program's own session volume while it is recorded, its blocks are left out (silence on the processed side, the original at that volume) until the mixer's next pass (`Timing.MixerRefresh`, 2 s) turns it down again. Which programs set their own session volume is unknown (SUSPECTED rare: Spotify's own volume slider is not seen to move Windows' session in the owner's earlier sessions, not checked).
- Windows' Volume mixer shows a recorded program at 0: the owner should know it is on purpose.
- A program that closes while recorded keeps a journal entry until the next start (`engine-journal.json` then stays on disk meanwhile).

**Not verified with real programs:** Spotify, Helium or any Chromium program recorded and turned down by OmniControl (the levels and law were proven only on the test processes; the same session control is used); the ProgramEngine and CaptureTest code paths end to end (the pieces were: the session scalar, the pipe, the make-up and the ceiling); the 2 s window after a program changes its own volume; a program with several processes (several roots, each made up the same way).

## Infinite layout loop crash (14 Nov 2026, open until the owner confirms)
Event log: `InvalidOperationException: Infinite layout loop detected` (Avalonia 11.3.6, `MediaContext.FireInvokeOnRenderCallbacks`) after resizing the window back and forth. Cause SUSPECTED, not reproduced (Avalonia.Headless is not in the offline NuGet cache, so no layout test could be written). Only feedback from a size into a size found in the code: `BandRowsPanel` takes its row height from the ScrollViewer's `Viewport.Height` binding; the rows added up to the viewport plus floating-point noise, so the bar could toggle and the viewport value jitter, each change starting another pass. Fix: the viewport value is floored to whole pixels and row heights are floored (`Fx/BandRowsPanel.cs`), so extent never exceeds viewport when the rows fit. Read and judged stable, no change: GroupHeader, WiringTable/WiringRowView (Auto shared columns are measured at infinite width; chip wrapping only grows height as width shrinks), HalfPanel, FoldingRow. Not analysed to the end: `PluginEditorArea` (a plug-in's `Resized` calls `Grow` which resizes the window; only with a plug-in editor open). If the crash returns, note which page/window was open.

## Program effects method control (14 Nov 2026)
A program row in the Mixer shows "Method: Automatic v" (small link style) in its sentence, after the engine status and Test, only in Full control and only when the program has effects (Fast mode always records, so it is hidden there). It opens an `optionmenu` pop-down of cards from `SoundModes.ProgramRoutes` (name, description, tooltip = Tip, current one `.selected`); a pick calls `MixerService.SetFxRoute` at once and the engine replans. It sits in the Mixer row (not the FX window) because the status saying which method runs is already on that row. The "no free cable" wording is the planner's own status, not duplicated. Search keywords added to the row entry. Pop-down is mouse-first; keyboard: Enter on the link opens, Up/Down/Enter/Esc via `OptionPanelKeys`. Not verified on screen (no UI was driven): wrapping at the narrowest width and the focus walk through the row's links.

## Cable availability after removing a combined microphone (14 Nov 2026)

Owner report: after removing the combined microphone "to free a cable", something still said the cable is not available. Findings: every consumer re-plans on `MixerService.Changed()` (RoutingChanged, then Updated on every scan) and every row re-reads `EngineStatus` on each `Merge`, so no engine/row state was found stale. In Full control the master bus takes the only pair FIRST, so a blocked combined microphone never held it and removing it frees nothing; the other rows' "Needs another virtual cable" (tooltip: the bus holds the pair, "Switch to Fast") is the true answer. One real stale source was fixed: the Combine devices window's `FreeCables` also counted every combined device's remembered `Cable` as taken, even one the plan did not give it; it now reads only `EffectsEngine.CablesInUse`. Tests in `CombinedMicrophoneTests` cover the planner side; the window line itself and the engine's live wiring could not be tested headlessly (needs the app).


## Trackpad taps (14 Nov) - as built; compiled and unit-tested with synthetic frames only; no real touch tried

Six new entries in `TrackpadGestures.All` (ids `tap.one/two/three`, `doubletap.one/two/three`; new `MouseButton` values appended at the end, so stored keys and `RuleEngine`'s button-sized arrays are unaffected; no release, `HasRelease` unchanged). Cards on the Trackpad group, rule triggers (category Trackpad), search and conflict checks come from the master list with no other edit; the group's tip says one-finger taps are also clicks and that a double tap replaces the second tap.
- **Recogniser:** a tap = all fingers lift within `CoreTiming.TrackpadTapMaxMs` (250) of the first touching, fingers landed within `TrackpadTapLandMs` (80) of each other, centroid (and for two fingers the finger distance) moved no more than `GestureRecogniser.TapMoveDistance`, and no swipe or pinch was recognised in the touch. Count = the most fingers at once (1-3; four-finger taps are ignored). A second tap with the same count within `TrackpadDoubleTapMs` (400) gives the double tap instead of the second tap, so the first tap's action still runs at the first tap (a tap cannot be delayed to wait for a second one); with a mapping on both, the pair runs the tap action then the double-tap action. Taps skip the swipe cooldown (it would block the second tap); the action runner's own launch limiter still applies.
- **Windows tag:** `WindowsTouchpad` also reads `TapsEnabled`, `TwoFingerTapEnabled` (needs taps on; since the takeover, read on its own: they are separate TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS bits) and `ThreeFingerTapEnabled` (missing value = on). Names assumed from the registry's usual keys; not checked on a PC with the setting changed.
- **Open:** the thresholds are guesses until tried on the owner's touchpad; a touchpad that does not send a frame with no fingers when lifting would never finish a tap (the 250 ms gap rule then resets it).
- **Owner test steps:** Mouse and Trackpad > Trackpad: give "Two-finger tap" an action (e.g. Mute). Tap lightly with two fingers: the card lights and the action runs; Windows' right-click menu also appears unless Windows' tap is off (Windows touchpad settings: Taps > two-finger tap off; one-finger tap = left click, three-finger tap = search by default). Press and hold, drag, scroll or swipe must not light a tap card. Tap twice quickly: "double tap" lights. Swipes and pinch unchanged. The "Windows also does this" tag shows on a tap card only while that Windows tap setting is on.

## Trackpad gesture takeover (14 Nov) - as built; compiled and unit-tested with a fake settings store only; nothing written to this PC's settings

> Superseded in part (14 Nov, later; "Trackpad takeover: the shell's own copy" at the end of this file): the page switch "Turn off Windows' own gestures while OmniControl uses them", its setting and hotkey are gone (the takeover is always on for a remapped gesture); a registry write is now followed by telling the shell, not by a WM_SETTINGCHANGE broadcast.

**Owner report.** Three-finger tap mapped to "Switch window (Alt+Tab)", tagged "Windows also does this": Windows "keeps taking over, it reverts to search".

**Cause (proven from code and the registry, read only).** OmniControl only copies the touchpad's reports (Raw Input); it never wrote a touchpad setting (no write call anywhere before this change). On this PC `ThreeFingerTapEnabled` is missing under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\PrecisionTouchPad`, which is Windows' default, "Open search". So every three-finger tap runs both: OmniControl's Alt+Tab and Windows' search, and search opening on top makes the switcher look as if it "reverted". Nothing re-enables the setting: no touchpad policy (`HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\Microsoft\Windows`: no touchpad entry), the ELAN software (`ETDCtrl`, `ETDService`, driver ELAN 22.2.55.4, devices "ELAN PrecisionTouchpad Filter Driver" and "ELAN HID Class Filter Driver") has no gesture values in `HKCU\Software\Elantech`. The same holds for every other gesture: three/four-finger swipes (task view, desktops), pinch to zoom, two-finger tap (right click), one-finger tap (click).

**Evidence (read only on this PC, Windows 11 Home 10.0.26300).**
- `reg query HKCU\...\PrecisionTouchPad`: `TapsEnabled`, `TwoFingerTapEnabled`, `ZoomEnabled`, `PanEnabled`, `TapAndDrag`, `RightClickZoneEnabled`, `FeedbackEnabled`, `LeaveOnWithMouse` = 0xffffffff (on); `ScrollDirection` 0, `AAPThreshold` 2, `CursorSpeed` 10, `EnableEdgy` 1; no `ThreeFingerSlideEnabled`, `FourFingerSlideEnabled`, `ThreeFingerTapEnabled`, `FourFingerTapEnabled` (Windows' defaults in force); `Status\Enabled` = 1.
- `SystemParametersInfo(SPI_GETTOUCHPADPARAMETERS 0xAE, 44, TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS_V1)` returned true: `01 00 00 00 | 05 00 00 00 | 00 00 00 00 | 1D 00 00 00 | BF 01 00 00 | 02 00 00 00 | 0A 00 00 00 | 32 00 00 00 | 32 00 00 00 | 00 00 00 00 | 00 00 00 00` = version 1, 5 contacts, no legacy touchpad, status 0x1D (present, external mouse present, enabled, active), user settings 0x1BF (allowActiveWhenMousePresent, feedback, tap, tapAndDrag, twoFingerTap, rightClickZone, pan, zoom; bits 0-9 in WinUser.h order), sensitivity 2, cursor speed 10, feedback 50, click force 50. Version 2 (48 bytes) also answers. Layout from the local SDK `WinUser.h` 10.0.26100 (`TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS_V1`); Microsoft's touchpad tuning guide documents SPI_SETTOUCHPADPARAMETERS with `SPIF_UPDATEINIFILE | SPIF_SENDCHANGE` from build 26027.
- Three/four-finger settings are NOT in TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS. A read-only scan of `System32` found the names `ThreeFingerTapEnabled`, `ThreeFingerSlideEnabled`, `FourFinger*`, `ThreeFingerUp/Down/Left/Right(KeyParams)`, `CustomThreeFingerTap` only in `twinui.dll` (the shell's `touchpadgestureprovider` / `touchpadactions`), `SettingsHandlers_nt.dll` and `SettingsEnvironment.Desktop.dll`; `twinui.dll` imports `RegNotifyChangeKeyValue` and WNF functions. So the shell reads these registry values itself and very probably watches the key. Values (Settings app choices, matching geekrewind/elevenforum guides): tap 0 Nothing, 1 Open search (default), 2 notification centre, 3 play/pause, 4 middle button (plus a custom shortcut); swipes 0 Nothing, 1 switch apps and show desktop (default), 2 switch desktops, 3 audio and volume (plus custom).

**What was built (one mechanism, from the master lists).**
- `Core/Engine/Trackpad/WindowsGestureSettings.All`: one entry per `WindowsGesture` (each `TrackpadGestures` entry's `AlsoWindows` points to it): name, what Windows does, registry value, route (registry for swipes and three-finger tap; TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS bit 2/4/8 for tap to click / two-finger tap / pinch to zoom), the "Nothing" value, Windows' default.
- `ITouchpadSettingsStore` + real `TouchpadSettingsStore` (registry value write or delete + a WM_SETTINGCHANGE "Software\...\PrecisionTouchPad" broadcast, 200 ms per window, hung ones skipped; or SPI_GET then SPI_SETTOUCHPADPARAMETERS with save and announce). `SpiNative` holds the one SystemParametersInfo declaration (new `Bytes` overload, codes 0xAE/0xAF).
- `GestureTakeover` (pure logic over the store): for each Windows setting an action uses and that is on, the original raw value is saved first (`trackpad-settings-original.json`, one entry per setting, an existing entry is never replaced, so a crashed run's original survives), then set to "Nothing". Given back when no longer wanted, but only if it is still at "Nothing" (a newer choice of the user's stays); the entry is removed only after the value is back (a refused give-back keeps it and says so). `Check` turns it off again if something turned it back on, at most 3 times a minute (`CoreTiming.TrackpadTakeoverReapply*`), then stops and says so. A refused write leaves nothing changed and reports one sentence. Each state change goes to `mouse-log.txt`.
- `TrackpadService` owns it: wanted = the Windows settings of every gesture with an action on any layer (`MouseSettings.HasAction`), and none while "Remap mouse and trackpad", "OmniControl trackpad gestures" or the new switch is off. It follows `MouseService.Applied` (new event, raised by `Apply`) and the page's `Refresh`; the work runs one job at a time off the UI thread; while something is off a `Timing.TrackpadTakeoverCheck` (3 s) timer calls `Check`; `Dispose` (exit) waits for the queue (`Timing.MouseQueueDrain`) and gives everything back; at start a crashed run's leftovers that are no longer wanted are given back (still wanted ones are kept with their first original).
- Page: new half switch "Turn off Windows' own gestures while OmniControl uses them" (`MouseSettings.TakeOverWindowsGestures`, default on) beside "OmniControl trackpad gestures"; "Windows touchpad settings" moved to the end of the group so the gesture cards keep their pairs. New hotkey "Toggle turning off Windows' own touchpad gestures" (`mouse.trackpad.takeover.toggle`). (Superseded: the first-time question below was removed.) The first time a takeover would change a Windows setting (and only while the page is on screen) one message asks: "OmniControl can turn off Windows' own three-finger tap while your action is set, and turns it back on when you remove the action. Turn it off now?" [Turn off / Not now]; the answer is kept (`MouseSettings.TakeoverAsked`); Not now (or closing the message) turns the switch off and the old tag stays. Setting the switch by hand counts as the answer. `EngineMessage.Decline` names the second button (the message window used "Cancel" before; unchanged elsewhere).
- (Superseded: the green tag was removed, see "Trackpad takeover kept by events".) Card tags: green "Windows' own turned off" (tooltip: which Windows setting was set to Nothing and when it comes back), or "Windows also does this" (tooltip: what Windows does, the failure sentence if a change was refused, and the switch to use when it is off). `MouseButtonItem.WindowsState` / `WindowsDetail` replace `WindowsAlso` and the fixed detail text.
- Changed on the side: `WindowsTouchpad` reads through the store and the master list (two-finger tap no longer counts as off while one-finger taps are off: they are separate TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS bits, and Microsoft documents them as separate settings).

**Tests.** `tests/OmniControl.Tests/GestureTakeoverTests.cs`, 14 tests with a fake store and fake backup: only wanted gestures are written, a setting already off is left alone, the original is saved before the write and a missing value is deleted again on give-back, give-back on removal, `RestoreAll` (exit and switches), crash recovery at the next start (given back, or kept with the first original), re-apply then give-up after the limit, `Check` writes nothing when nothing changed, a value the user changed meanwhile stays, a refused write is reported and leaves no entry, a refused give-back keeps the entry, nothing wanted never writes or saves, `WouldTurnOff` for the question, every gesture has one Windows setting. Whole suite: 70 pass (56 before). Some test builds were refused by Smart App Control (`FileLoadException` 0x800711C7 on the fresh `OmniControl.Core.dll`, every test failing at load, see the Smart App Control section); a rebuild with `-p:Deterministic=false` got an allowed file and all 70 passed.

**Not verified (nothing was written to this PC's settings).** That the shell (`twinui.dll`) picks up a registry change to `ThreeFingerTapEnabled` / `*SlideEnabled` at once (very likely: it imports `RegNotifyChangeKeyValue`; the broadcast is a second nudge; the guides say a sign-out may be needed after a .reg edit); that deleting the missing-value again is picked up live as the default; SPI_SETTOUCHPADPARAMETERS writes (documented, not tried); the switch, tags, question and their wording on screen, at the narrowest window and in both themes.

**Open items.**
- One Windows setting covers several gestures: mapping only "three fingers up" turns off all of Windows' three-finger swipes (Windows' "Custom" per-direction values could keep the others; not built). Mapping a one-finger tap or double tap turns off tap to click entirely.
- Custom rules with a Trackpad trigger do not count as "an action uses it" yet (only mappings do); their gesture still runs Windows' own action too.
- (Superseded: no limit now, at once on the registry change; see "Trackpad takeover kept by events".) If the user turns Windows' gesture back on in Settings while OmniControl keeps it off, OmniControl turned it off again within 3 s, up to 3 times a minute, then stopped and said so.
- At sign-out or shutdown without a normal exit the setting stays at "Nothing" until OmniControl next starts (then given back if no longer wanted).
- If the live pick-up of a registry value fails on the owner's PC, the next step is to look at what `SettingsHandlers_nt.dll` does after writing (a WNF state change is likely).

**Similar root causes elsewhere (Windows also acts on the same input).**
- Mouse buttons, wheel and tilt cards: handled today by the low-level hook swallowing the input (the "Also send the button's normal click" option lets it through on purpose). Back/Forward with no mapping over the desktop or OmniControl's windows: swallowed (12 Nov fix). Nothing to improve.
- Gestures page wheel areas (taskbar, edges, title bars, desktop, modifiers): an active gesture swallows the scroll (`GestureService`), so Windows does not also scroll. Nothing to improve.
- Pinch: Windows' pinch reaches programs as their own zoom or a Ctrl+wheel; now covered by the takeover (pinch to zoom off while a pinch card has an action), which also stops that synthetic Ctrl+wheel reaching the Wheel cards (the earlier open item).
- Keyboard shortcuts: `RegisterHotKey` refuses a combination Windows or another program owns, and the Shortcuts page is told (`HotkeyService.Failed`); Windows' reserved Win+ combinations cannot be taken over from user mode without a keyboard hook. Can be improved by saying "Windows keeps this one" for the known reserved Win+ keys; not built.
- Volume keys: not intercepted; Windows' own volume flyout is hidden by the OSD page's "Hide Windows' OSD". Nothing to change.

**Owner test steps.**
1. Build and launch. Mouse and Trackpad > Trackpad: the first row is "OmniControl trackpad gestures" | "Turn off Windows' own gestures while OmniControl uses them" (on); "Windows touchpad settings" is the last card.
2. With "Three-finger tap" set to an action (as now): the message "Turn off Windows' own gesture?" opens once when the page is shown. Choose Turn off: the card's tag becomes the green "Windows' own turned off". Open Windows touchpad settings > Three-finger gestures > Taps: it shows "Nothing".
3. Tap with three fingers anywhere: only OmniControl's action runs (no search).
4. Set the card back to Default: the tag goes; Windows' Settings shows "Open search" again (reopen the Settings page). Give it the action again: turned off again without a question.
5. In Windows' Settings set the tap back to "Open search" while the action is set: within 3 s it is back at "Nothing" (do it 4 times in a minute: the fourth stays, and the card says why).
6. Turn either switch off, or exit OmniControl from the tray: Windows' Settings shows the tap as before. Start again: it is turned off again.
7. Repeat with a three-finger swipe, a four-finger swipe, pinch in, and a two-finger tap: Windows' swipes / pinch to zoom / tap with two fingers to right-click go to off while mapped and come back when not.
8. Answer "Not now" on a fresh profile (delete `TakeoverAsked` from `mouse.json`): the switch turns off and the old "Windows also does this" tag stays.
If something goes wrong: `%APPDATA%\OmniControl\trackpad-settings-original.json` lists what is still to be given back, `mouse-log.txt` has a line per change; Windows' Settings > Bluetooth & devices > Touchpad sets everything back by hand.

**Change (14 Nov, later): takeover follows the action's setting; the question is gone.** Owner's rule: a gesture left at Default is never touched; once it has an action Windows' own gesture is turned off, unless that action's settings tick "Also let Windows do its own gesture".
- One setting, not two: `MouseOptions.KeepOriginal` (key `original`, stored as `|original=1`) is also the gesture option. Its wording comes from the input kind in one place (`MouseOptions.OriginalFor`, chosen with `MouseButtons.IsTrackpadGesture`): buttons read "Also send the button's normal click", trackpad gestures "Also let Windows do its own gesture". `MouseActionCatalog.Choices(..., gesture: true)` builds the gesture list (the page keeps a third list next to pressed and fired). Offered on every action except Default and Do nothing (and "Switch to layer N", which has no settings at all); a gesture also gets it on Next/Previous/Reset layer.
- `MouseSettings.ReplacesWindows(button)` (replaces `HasAction`): some layer has a non-Default action without the setting. `TrackpadService.Wanted()` uses it, so ticking or unticking the setting (any picker change calls `MouseService.Apply` -> `Applied`) gives Windows' value back or takes it over again at once. Old `mouse.json` files have no setting, so they mean "take over".
- Gestures arrive from Raw Input (`MouseHook.Raise`); the hook's return value is ignored for them, so `MouseService.Handle` neither swallows nor double-fires them; for gestures the setting only matters to the takeover.
- Removed: the first-time question, `MouseSettings.TakeoverAsked` (an old file's value is ignored), `TrackpadService.AwaitingConsent`/`AnswerTakeover`, `GestureTakeover.WouldTurnOff`, `EngineMessage.Decline`. The page switch stays (default on) with a tooltip.
- (Superseded: see "Trackpad takeover kept by events".) Tags (only once the gesture has an action): green "Windows' own turned off" when OmniControl turned it off; neutral "Windows also does this" when Windows' own is on (tooltip says when that is the action's own setting); none at Default. Two gestures sharing one Windows setting (three-finger up/down...): one replacing it turns it off for both, and the other's tooltip says so.
- Tests: 88 pass (`GestureOptionTests`: Default untouched, remapped, option on, other layer without it, old file, wording per input; `GestureTakeoverTests`: following the wanted set on, off and on again). Compile check only; nothing on this PC's settings touched, app not stopped or relaunched.
- Not verified on screen: the option's label in a gesture's picker settings page, the tags at the narrowest window and in both themes, the live give-back when the option is ticked.
- Owner test steps (replace step 2 and 8 above): (a) Three-finger tap at an action, option off: no question; tag "Windows' own turned off"; Windows' Settings shows Nothing. (b) Open the card's cogwheel, tick "Also let Windows do its own gesture": Windows' Settings shows "Open search" again, tag becomes "Windows also does this"; untick: Nothing again. (c) Back to Default: no tag, Windows as before.

## Trackpad layers; the trackpad switch removed (14 Nov; compiled and unit-tested only, nothing seen on screen)

Owner's request: remove the "OmniControl trackpad gestures" switch, and give the Trackpad group's title line a layer ("preset") drop-down and cogwheel exactly like the Mouse Buttons one, with the trackpad's own layers.

- **Switch removed.** The card "OmniControl trackpad gestures", `MouseSettings.TrackpadEnabled` (an old file's value is ignored), `TrackpadService.IsEnabled/SetEnabled` and the hotkey "Toggle trackpad gestures" (`mouse.trackpad.toggle`; a binding saved for it in `hotkeys.json` stays in the file and is simply never used, a mouse or gesture mapped to "omni:mouse.trackpad.toggle" reports that the action is missing) are gone. Gesture mappings follow "Remap mouse and trackpad" (as they already did in `MouseService`); the takeover follows that switch and its own. The touchpad watcher now always runs (like the mouse hook), so rules with a Trackpad trigger keep working while remapping is off (they follow the Gestures page's switch, like every rule). The takeover switch and "Windows touchpad settings" now share the group's first row, so the ten gesture cards keep their pairs below. Earlier notes that mention the trackpad switch (the "trackpad gestures" plan and test step 8, takeover test steps 1 and 6) are superseded by this.
- **Two families of layers** (`App/Mouse/InputFamily`: Mouse = buttons, wheel, tilt; Trackpad = gestures; `FamilyOf(button)` is the one place that decides). Each has its own names, "only list used layers" choice, layer in force (`LayerState`, inside `MouseService`; `LayerOf(family)`, `SetLayer/StepLayer(family, ...)`, `LayerChanged(family, layer)`), manage window (the one `LayerManagerModel`/`LayerManagerWindow`, given the family; title "Manage mouse layers" / "Manage trackpad layers") and drop-down (`Pages/Mouse/LayerPicker`, used twice; each edits only its own group's cards and doubles as the live indicator). The footer says "Mouse layer: ..." (was "Active layer: ..."); the trackpad's is shown by its own drop-down. UPDATE 14 Nov: the layer manager window is gone; each cogwheel opens the shared preset manager at the Mouse Buttons / Trackpad card (see "Unified preset manager").
- **Storage scheme (mouse.json), backward compatible.** Bindings stay in the one `Bindings` dictionary keyed `layer.Button`; the number is a layer of the input's own family, so `2.Middle` is mouse layer 2 and `2.ThreeFingerUp` trackpad layer 2. New: `TrackpadLayerNames` (null only in older files) and `TrackpadOnlyUsedLayers` (default on). `MouseSettings.Upgrade` (run by `Load`, once: it runs only while `TrackpadLayerNames` is null) keeps every gesture mapping on its layer number (so nothing mapped is lost; layer N's gestures become trackpad layer N's), copies the mouse name of each layer that held a gesture mapping to the trackpad's, and rewrites a gesture's layer action to the trackpad one (`layer.next` -> `trackpad.layer.next`, options kept). Profiles from very old files are still folded in first.
- **Layer actions** (ids in `MouseActionCatalog`: `layer.next|previous|reset|set.N|hold.N` = mouse, unchanged; `trackpad.layer.*` = trackpad). Mouse cards: the mouse layer actions as before, plus "Hold for trackpad layer N" (category "Trackpad layers"; the only way to hold a trackpad layer, since a gesture has no release). Gesture cards: "Next / Previous trackpad layer", "Back to trackpad layer 1", "Switch to trackpad layer N" (no hold). Behaviour change: a mouse button's "Next layer" no longer moves the trackpad, and a gesture's layer action moves only the trackpad. New hotkey "Next trackpad layer" (`mouse.trackpad.layer.next`), so it is also an OmniControl action in every picker and on Shortcuts; "Next mouse layer" moves the mouse only.
- **Rules:** a rule with a Trackpad trigger checks the trackpad's layer in force, any other the mouse's (`RuleTrigger.Family`, used by `CompiledRule`, `RuleConflicts` and the editor's Layer names). A rule saved earlier with a trackpad trigger on layer N now means trackpad layer N.
- **Takeover** unchanged in rule: `ReplacesWindows` already looks at every layer of the gesture's key, which are now the trackpad's layers.
- **Search:** the two drop-downs are search entries ("Mouse layer" under Mouse Buttons, "Trackpad layer" under Trackpad); the trackpad cards also match "layer" and "preset".
- **Tests** (`LayerFamilyTests`, 9): an old mouse.json loads with every mapping, the gesture layer actions rewritten and the names copied; the upgrade runs once; a new file; layer sets count, name, list and clear only their own inputs; the takeover looks at every trackpad layer; the layer in force is kept per family (chosen, held from a mouse button, released, stepped with wrap); each card lists its own family's layer actions; ids read back; trackpad triggers use the trackpad's layers. Whole suite: 97 pass.
- **Not verified (nothing seen on screen):** the trackpad header's drop-down and cogwheel at the narrowest window and in both themes (same markup and classes as the Mouse Buttons header, so they should match); keyboard Up/Down reaching the new drop-down (`Tag="grouptool"`, as on Mouse Buttons); the manage window's bound title.
- **Owner test steps.** (1) Mouse and Trackpad: the Trackpad header reads "Trackpad ... N settings [Layer 1 v] [cog]", just like Mouse Buttons; the first row is "Turn off Windows' own gestures..." | "Windows touchpad settings"; no "OmniControl trackpad gestures" card. (2) Gesture mappings made before are still there on the same layer numbers (trackpad drop-down). (3) Pick trackpad layer 2: only the gesture cards change; the mouse drop-down stays. Pick mouse layer 2: only the button cards change. (4) Cog on Trackpad: "Manage trackpad layers"; rename layer 2, it shows in the trackpad drop-down only; Clear removes only gesture mappings. (5) Give a gesture "Next trackpad layer": swiping it moves the trackpad drop-down live, the mouse one stays. Give a side button "Hold for trackpad layer 2": while held, the trackpad drop-down shows layer 2. (6) Turn "Remap mouse and trackpad" off: gestures do nothing of OmniControl's and Windows' own gestures come back. (7) Shortcuts: "Next trackpad layer" is listed; "Toggle trackpad gestures" is gone. (8) A custom rule with a Trackpad trigger: its Layer drop-down lists the trackpad's layer names.

## Mixer: "Show closed" and searchable "Add program" (14 Nov, built to `docs/mockups/mixer-closed-programs-a-c.html`; unverified on screen)

- **What:** the Outputs header (Mixer tab) has "Show closed" (a small switch, `AppSettings.MixerShowClosed`, default on, hotkey "Show or hide closed programs") and the "Add program..." pill, which opens a `FlyoutPresenter.listmenu` pop-down: search box, programs "recently used" then "installed" (each alphabetical, a muted source word), and "Browse for a program (.exe)..." last. The old "Not playing yet? Add program" list row and `AddProgramRow` are gone. The summary ("Master + N programs") now sits beside the name, counts the listed programs and is trimmed first.
- **Listing rule** (`ProgramListing.IsListed`): a program is a row when it is open, or closed with "Show closed" on and worth remembering (`AppAudioEntry.KeepsRow`: added by hand, or it has effects, a route, a non-default level, balance or mute). Hidden programs keep their `MixerItem` (effects, status, engine carry on) and return at once when opened; the main search still finds them (`AllRows`). The Wiring tab lists the Mixer's rows, so a hidden program is not on Wiring either.
- **Menu source:** `ProgramDiscovery` (off the UI thread, cached `Timing.InstalledProgramsCache`) over `Core/Engine/InstalledPrograms` (Start menu .lnk targets of the user and all users, `App Paths` keys). Setup tools, Windows' own programs and `IgnoredPrograms` are left out. Choosing an entry calls the old `MixerService.AddProgram`, which now sets `AppAudioEntry.Added` so the row stays until its x is pressed.
- **Behaviour you may not expect:** closed programs with nothing set (defaults, never added by hand) are no longer rows in either state (the mock-up's rule); they appear in the menu as "recently used". Entries added by hand before this change have no `Added` flag, so those with default settings disappear from the list (they are offered again in the menu). Adding a closed program while "Show closed" is off switches it on, so the new row can be seen.
- **Not done:** installed Store (UWP) apps (no cheap reliable .exe path); a Down-arrow walk inside the menu (Tab walks the rows, Enter in the search box chooses the first match, Esc closes; the pop-down is mouse first as AGENTS.md rule 5 allows).
- **Owner test steps:** (1) Mixer tab: the Outputs header shows "Show closed", a switch and "Add program..."; at the narrowest window the summary is trimmed first. (2) Switch off: closed programs vanish, open ones stay, the count drops; switch on: they return dimmed with "Not open". Restart: the switch keeps its state. (3) Give a closed program an effect via its FX window, switch off, open the program: its row is back with the effect. (4) Search the sidebar for a hidden program's name: it is found. (5) "Add program...": the pop-down lists programs; type to filter; click one: a row appears ("Not open"), it stays after a restart until x. (6) Type nonsense: "No match. Browse for a program..." and the Browse row still works. (7) Both themes; Esc closes the pop-down; Tab/Enter from the keyboard.

## Trackpad takeover kept by events; the "turned off" tag removed (14 Nov; compiled and unit-tested with a fake store only; nothing written to this PC's settings, nothing tapped)

**Owner's requests.** (1) Remove the green "Windows' own turned off" tag and its tooltip: turning Windows' own gesture off is the expected remap behaviour. (2) Three-finger tap mapped to "Switch window (Alt+Tab)": pressed a few times, Windows "still sometimes slips" and opens search although its setting is Nothing. Find the root cause and make sure no gesture can slip.

**Tags now.** A gesture card (with an action) shows a tag only when it says something not obvious: neutral "Windows also does this" when the action's settings tick "Also let Windows do its own gesture" AND Windows' own gesture really still runs (another gesture sharing the setting may have turned it off); a caution "Windows' own still on" when turning it off failed (a refused or not-kept write), tooltip = the reason. Nothing else is tagged, including a mapped gesture while the takeover switch is off (owner's rule). Removed: `WindowsTag.TurnedOff`, `MouseButtonItem.ShowsWindowsOff` / `WindowsOffText`, the green tag in `MouseButtonRow.axaml` and its tooltip text; added `WindowsTag.Problem`, `ShowsWindowsProblem`, `WindowsProblemText` (master `Border.tag.caution`).

**Investigation (read only on this PC, 14 Nov 14:20-14:40).**
- (a) PROVEN applied and correctly encoded. `HKCU\...\PrecisionTouchPad\ThreeFingerTapEnabled` = 0 (REG_DWORD); `trackpad-settings-original.json` = `{"ThreeFingerTap":null}` (the value was missing = Windows' default, search); the running OmniControl (started 14:09:28) logged `Windows' three-finger tap: None -> TurnedOff` at 14:09:34. Only ONE value selects the tap: a scan of every System32 binary finds `ThreeFingerTapEnabled` read only by `twinui.dll` (the shell) and written by `SettingsHandlers_nt.dll` (Settings); the only other names are `CustomThreeFingerTap` / `CustomThreeFingerTapKeyParams`, used only for the "Custom shortcut" choice (and `ThreeFingerTapCount` in `win32kbase.sys`, a counter). No hidden setting struct: SPI_GETTOUCHPADPARAMETERS answers versions 1 and 2 only (no three-finger fields; version 3 refused, error 87). 0 = Nothing is the Settings app's encoding (0 Nothing, 1 Open search (default), 2 notification centre, 3 play/pause, 4 middle button).
- (b) NOT the cause in this run, as far as the evidence reaches: no "turned back on" line was logged by the 3 s check since 14:09:34 (it logs every time it finds the value on), no Modern Standby/resume between 14:09 and 14:40 (System log: standby 13:08-13:21 only, before this run), no touchpad reconnect logged. Not excludable: a flip that something put back to 0 within 3 s (nothing but OmniControl writes 0, and before this change OmniControl's own re-write on a page refresh or mapping change was silent in the log). The 3-a-minute cap could leave Windows' gesture on for good after a fight; it never triggered here, but it is gone now.
- (c) DISPROVEN later the same day (see "Trackpad takeover: the shell's own copy" at the end): the shell does not watch the key; it acts on its own copy and refreshes it only when told. The WM_SETTINGCHANGE broadcast was removed.
- (d) DISPROVEN (static evidence): the touchpad is a precision touchpad (ELAN filter drivers only pass PTP reports); ELAN's `ETDCtrl.exe` (running) watches the PrecisionTouchPad key but the only values it names there are `Status\Enabled`, `CursorSpeed` and `FeedbackIntensity` (`HasOverwrittenPTPSettings` is a one-time cursor/haptic overwrite); its own gesture keys (`HKCU\Software\Elantech\SmartPad`, `HKLM\...\Control\Elantech\SmartPad`) do not exist on this PC.
- (e) Windows and OmniControl both act on lift; OmniControl only copies the reports: nothing to change.
- (f) DISPROVEN: "Switch window (Alt+Tab)" is one atomic SendInput of Alt down, Tab down, Tab up, Alt up (`InputSender.Chord`); no Win key is sent, so it cannot open Start or search, and a gesture can never hold keys ("while held" actions are refused for inputs without a release).
- Why the existing logs cannot answer it (PROVEN): `mouse-log.txt` keeps at most `CoreTiming.HookLogLimit` (150) gesture/wheel lines per run, and they were used up by scrolling at 14:10:01, so none of the owner's later taps were logged at all. Gesture lines now go to their own rate-limited log (below).
- **Cause: SUSPECTED, not proven.** With the value at Nothing the whole run and nothing found that rewrites it, the remaining candidates are a short-lived rewrite missed by the 3 s poll, or the shell (twinui) acting on a stale copy of the setting at times. The new log tells these apart (see "How to read the log").

**What changed.**
- `Core/Engine/Trackpad/TouchpadSettingsWatcher.cs` (new): `RegNotifyChangeKeyValue` on the PrecisionTouchPad key and subkeys (names and values), re-armed before every wait on its own thread; `Changed(true)` at once on any write (the Settings app, SPI_SETTOUCHPADPARAMETERS which saves there, a driver, Windows after resume, sign-in or a device reconnect, OmniControl itself), `Changed(false)` every `Timing.TrackpadTakeoverCheck` as the backstop. Replaces the DispatcherTimer. No separate power or session hooks: re-applying when nothing changed does nothing, and any change Windows makes at resume, unlock or reconnect is a registry write that is now seen at once (a touchpad arrival also re-applies through `Refresh`).
- `GestureTakeover`: every write is read back; a value Windows did not keep counts as a failure (caution tag). `Check` re-applies every time, never gives up, at most once per `CoreTiming.TrackpadTakeoverReapplyGapMs` (1 s) per gesture so a program writing it back cannot start a tight loop (the next look catches it); its log lines are capped at 5 per gesture per minute. The re-apply inside `Update` (page open, mapping change, touchpad arrival) now logs too. `CoreTiming.TrackpadTakeoverReapplyLimit/WindowMs` removed.
- Applies to every Windows gesture in the master list alike (three/four-finger swipes, three-finger tap, tap to click, two-finger tap, pinch to zoom): one mechanism, one watcher.
- `App/Mouse/TrackpadLog.cs` (new), `%APPDATA%\OmniControl\trackpad-log.txt`: the touchpad watcher's start/stop lines and every takeover write (moved from `mouse-log.txt`), plus one line per recognised gesture (at most `Timing.TrackpadLogGestureLimit` = 30 per minute) with Windows' matching setting read back at that moment. `TrackpadGestures.Of(button)` added for that lookup.
- Mouse side checked, nothing to change: buttons, wheel and tilt are swallowed by the low-level hook (Windows never sees them unless "Also send the button's normal click"), the Gestures page's wheel areas swallow their scroll, Back/Forward over the desktop or OmniControl are swallowed; no Windows setting is written there, so nothing can be turned back on.

**How to read the log** (`trackpad-log.txt`, send it after a slip, with the time it happened):
- `ThreeFingerTap recognised; Windows' three-finger tap (ThreeFingerTapEnabled) = 0 (Nothing); OmniControl: TurnedOff` at the slip's time = the setting really was Nothing and Windows' shell still acted: a shell-side problem (next step: look at what Settings does besides the registry write, e.g. a WNF state, and do the same).
- `= 1 (Windows acts)` or `= missing (Windows acts)` = the value was on at that moment: the lines just before say who changed it (`was turned back on (1); turned off again, reads back 0`) and when.
- No `recognised` line at the slip's time = OmniControl did not see a three-finger tap (four fingers, a finger landing late): Windows' own action then ran on a gesture OmniControl did not take; the line for what it did see (if any) is there.
- `Windows' ...: TurnedOff -> Failed (...)` = a write was refused or not kept; the card shows the caution tag.

**Tests** (`GestureTakeoverTests`, fake store): rewritten externally 20 times, turned off again each time, original kept (replaces the old give-up test); a rewrite within the gap is caught at the next look; a resume or touchpad reconnect re-applies through `Update` and logs the value read back; a write Windows did not keep is a failure with its reason and no backup entry. Whole suite: 100 pass (the test build needed a fresh `--no-incremental` build: Smart App Control blocked the first `OmniControl.Core.dll`).

**Not verified.** No touch was made and no setting written: the registry watcher's live behaviour (it only reads, but it was not run here), whether the shell picks up every live change (the log will show), the tags on screen (narrowest window, both themes), the log lines' wording in a real run.

**Owner test steps.** (1) Build and launch. Mouse and Trackpad > Trackpad: "Three-finger tap" at Switch window shows no tag. (2) Tap with three fingers many times, quickly and slowly: only Alt+Tab. If search ever opens, note the time and send `%APPDATA%\OmniControl\trackpad-log.txt`. (3) Open Windows touchpad settings and set the three-finger tap to "Open search": it returns to "Nothing" at once (reopen the page to see it); the log has a "was turned back on" line. (4) In the action's cogwheel tick "Also let Windows do its own gesture": the tag "Windows also does this" appears and Windows' Settings shows Open search; untick: tag gone, Nothing again. (5) Sleep and wake the PC, lock and unlock: tap again, only Alt+Tab. (6) Repeat 2-3 with a three-finger swipe and a two-finger tap.

## Several actions per button and gesture, option A (14 Nov; built to `docs/mockups/multiple-actions-a.html`; compiled and unit-tested only, nothing seen on screen, nothing pressed)

**What.** Every card of the Mouse Buttons group (buttons, wheel, tilt) and the Trackpad group (swipes, pinch, taps) can have up to `MouseSettings.MaxActions` (20) actions. The card shows them as chips (`Button.chipx.walk`, numbered when there are two or more, a muted value such as the shortcut after the name, an x), then the dashed "+ add action" chip (`Button.blockadd.small`), then the first action's cogwheel. At Default the card shows the quiet "Default" pill alone (it picks the first action). The chips wrap onto at most two lines (`Controls/ChipFoldPanel`); what does not fit folds into the accent "+N more" chip (`Button.chipx.more`), which opens a `FlyoutPresenter.listmenu` list of every action (number, name, cogwheel, up, down, x; scrolls past `OptionListMaxHeight`).

**Storage (mouse.json), backward compatible.** The first action stays in `Bindings["layer.Button"]` exactly as before; the second and later ones are in the new `ExtraActions["layer.Button"]` (a list of the same stored texts). An old file has no `ExtraActions` and loads unchanged; an older OmniControl reading a new file still sees (and runs) the first action. Chosen over a delimiter-joined list because the stored text of a program path or shortcut is not escaped (`run:C:\a;b|c.exe` would break a joined list). A first action of Default (or Disabled) drops the others; `LayerSet.Clear` removes both.

**Running.** All actions of one firing are looked up on the layer in force when it fired; a layer action among them changes the layer for the next firing only. The first action works exactly as before (while held, on release, repeatedly, keys held, letting the input through). The others run once each, in order, after it, off the hook thread (`ActionSequence`, `Timing.MouseActionGap` = 40 ms between): on press, or on release after the first when the first runs on release; a "Repeatedly" first repeats alone. Only one sequence of later actions per input runs at a time: while one is still running (a fast wheel), a new firing runs its first action only. Later actions are one-shot: the extra-slot list (`MouseActionCatalog.ExtraChoices`) has no Default or Disabled, greys every "while held" action with "Only the first action can last while the button is held", and drops the settings only a first action has (Runs, repeat delay and speed, "Also send the button's normal click" / "Also let Windows do its own gesture"; "Times" is then always shown, `MouseOptions.OneShot`); `MouseService.RunLater` also refuses a while-held one and lets go at once of anything a mode would hold. The launch limiter (`MouseActionRunner.MayLaunch`) covers every action.

**Takeover and tags.** The first action decides: `ReplacesWindows` and "Windows also does this" read only its "Also let Windows do its own gesture", which later actions do not offer (letting the input through is decided once per firing). This differs from the brief's "any action without the keep option replaces", which would have made the first action's tick useless as soon as a second action was added.

**Mouse and keyboard.** Click a chip: that action's own picker window, opened on its settings with the Back button (`PickerModel(backToList: true)`), or on the list when the action has no settings; its x (or Delete on a focused chip) takes it away; the x on the only action = back to Default. Drag a chip onto another to move it there. The list's up/down move by one. A move or drag that would put a "while held" action in a later place is refused (its up/down is dimmed). Keyboard: Enter on the card goes to the first chip (Enter opens it), Left/Right walk the chips, "+N more", "+ add action" and the cogwheel (chips left out by the fold are skipped: `RowEditing.Parts` now ignores a part given no room). The "+N more" list is mouse first (AGENTS.md rule 5; Esc closes). Search finds a card by any of its actions' names. Unmatched stored actions (an OmniControl action not registered) are kept as they are and shown as "(an action that is no longer available)" instead of being dropped.

**Differences from the mock-up.** (1) A card at Default keeps today's "Default" pill without the dashed chip (the pill already picks the first action, so an add chip there would be a second control doing the same). (2) A single action is a neutral chip, not today's accent-tinted pill (the mock-up draws a chip; the accent tint is gone). (3) The list's reorder control is up/down arrows, not a drag handle (rows are buttons; the FX window's drag helper lives in `App/Fx`, which `Pages` must not use). (4) Chip drag has no live preview: the chip dims while dragged and moves on release. (5) The chip's value (Alt+Tab) is shown only for actions that take text.

**Not verified (nothing seen, clicked or pressed).** The fold at the narrowest window and in both themes; that the fold settles in one or two layout passes (its "+N more" words are set after layout, posted); keyboard focus look of the chips; the list's width with long names; dragging; timing of real sequences (Alt+Tab then volume, launches).

**Tests** (`MultipleActionsTests`, 7): round trip, old file (and `"ExtraActions":null`), Default/Disabled/cap, layer clear, running order and stop at a failure (fake steps), the takeover follows the first action, one-shot filtering. Whole suite: 107 pass.

**Owner test steps.** (1) Mouse and Trackpad: a mapped card shows its action as a chip with x, then "+ add action"; Default cards show the "Default" pill. (2) "+ add action" on Middle button: the picker lists no Default/Disabled and greys "Slow cursor while held" with the reason; pick "Volume up", then add "Mute": chips read "1 ...", "2 Volume up", "3 Mute". (3) Press Middle: the actions happen in that order. (4) Click chip 2: its settings window opens with Back; Back lists actions for that place. (5) Drag chip 3 onto chip 1: order 3,1,2; restart: same order. (6) Add many actions until "+N more" appears on a two-line card; the list opens, up/down/x work, it scrolls. (7) x on the only action: back to Default. (8) Three-finger tap with "Switch window" plus "Mute": Windows' own tap stays off; tick "Also let Windows do its own gesture" on the first: the tag appears. (9) Search the sidebar for "Mute": the card is found. (10) Left/Right walk the chips; Delete removes the focused chip; both themes, narrowest window.


## Recorded programs armed ahead of the sound (14 Nov; built, compiled and unit-tested; live-measured with a throwaway probe of the same Core pieces, not with the app itself)

**Owner's report.** Full control, Spotify with an EQ on Automatic (recorded on this one-cable PC): "the volume effect took 1-2 seconds before the FX got applied": the first 1-2 s of every start were heard dry.

**Causes (evidence).**
- (1) PROVEN. Full control planned a recording only once the program was heard (`PlanInput.Sounding`), and "heard" came from `MixerService.Scan` every `Timing.MixerRefresh` (2 s): up to 2 s before the run even existed. Measured with the old flow (below): dry 265, 925, 1630 and 1645 ms depending on where the play fell in the 2 s scan.
- (2) PROVEN. Then the run started its pipe and listened; the original was turned down only after the 100 ms engine tick heard it, so even with the run ready (Fast mode) the first 125-130 ms were dry, then about 85 ms of near silence before the processed copy.
- (3) DISPROVEN by reading: `EffectsEngine.Reconcile` keeps a run whose `RunSpec` is unchanged and `ProgramEngine.OnStreamsChanged` restarts the pipe only when the program's process roots change; no restart on a replan caused by another program (now a test).

**Measurements (raw).** Probe: a child process plays a 997 Hz tone (peak 0.05) into "CABLE Input" (nobody listens to it in Fast mode, the owner's current mode), the endpoint is recorded by loopback with Windows' timestamps; the replay goes through the real `AudioPipe` (process loopback, `QuietLevel` make-up and ceiling, 30 ms render) with a ring modulator as the "effect" so the processed copy is at 2003 Hz; Goertzel on 5 ms windows, onset = 3 windows above 0.005 (-46 dBFS). Times in ms after the tone's Play() call (the dry tone reaches the endpoint about 120-133 ms after that call).
```
OLD Full control (run starts on the 2 s scan that sees it playing, listen, 100 ms tick, turn down):
before-full playAfter=2300: dry onset 125, dry heard 1645 ms (max -26.0 dBFS)  | engine starts 1512 | turned down 1648
before-full playAfter=2300: dry onset 129, dry heard 1630 ms, processed onset 1844 | engine starts 1503 | turned down 1639
before-full playAfter=3000: dry onset 133, dry heard  925 ms, processed onset 1143 | engine starts  797 | turned down  934
before-full playAfter=3700: dry onset 132, dry heard  265 ms, processed onset  492 | engine starts  131 | turned down  266
OLD Fast mode (run exists at stream open, listens, turns down on the first tick that hears it):
before-fast: dry onset 131, dry heard 130 ms, processed onset 351 | turned down 140
before-fast: dry onset 120, dry heard 125 ms, processed onset 330 | turned down 128
NEW armed (session open and paused 3 s before play; turned down and recording at once):
after-armed: dry none (997 Hz max -49.3 dBFS = leakage of the processed tone), processed onset 209 | turned down -3011 | scalar during play 0.0001
after-armed: dry none (max -50.3), processed onset 196 | turned down -3020 | scalar 0.0001
after-armed: dry none (max -49.1), processed onset 200 | turned down -3012 | scalar 0.0001
NEW fresh session (the program creates its session and plays at once; armed on Windows' session-created event):
after-fresh: dry none (max -47.8), processed onset 219 | session -3 | turned down -4 | pipe up 23
after-fresh: dry none (max -52.8), processed onset 215 | session -2 | turned down -8 | pipe up 23
after-fresh: dry none (max -54.6), processed onset 218 | session -3 | turned down -10 | pipe up 24
CPU of one armed idle run (process loopback of a paused program + replay of silence through 4 biquads): 0.94 and 1.41 % of one core
   (process loopback delivers about 100 silent packets a second even while the program's stream is stopped)
A full mixer scan (devices, recording devices, sessions, process paths): median 6.5 ms, max 15.9 ms (20 runs)
```
Result: before 125 ms to 1.65 s of dry sound (owner's 1-2 s), then a gap; after, no dry sound at all and the first sound is heard processed about 75-85 ms after it would have been heard dry (the pipe's own delay; in Full control the bus adds its delay after that, as for every program). Windows kept the session's 0.0001 when the paused stream started (read back during play every time). A brand-new session is created by Windows about 120 ms before its first sound reaches the endpoint, which is why arming on the session event was in time in every run.

**What changed.**
- `RoutePlanner.PlanFullProgram`: an open but silent program (a session, no sound) that will be recorded (Recording, or Automatic with no free cable pair) gets its `Capture` run at once, armed; it takes no cable. Virtual cable, and Automatic while a pair is free, still wait for sound (a browser's always-open silent session must not take the cable). A run is kept while its program is closed (it already was through `PlanInput.Capturing`). Fast mode already planned the run at stream open. `CableBox.HasFree`; `FxSlot.AnyRuns` is the one "effects on and something enabled" rule (the planner's private `Active` is gone).
- `ProgramEngine`: arms at once: journal, original silenced (before it can sound), pipe started, make-up and ceiling set, then the replay opens (the gate stays shut while a new pipe is at gain 1). The make-or-break check moved to `RecordingCheck` (pure): the first time Windows' session meter (read before the session volume) shows the program playing, the recording must deliver passing sound within `Timing.CaptureSilenceProbe` (1.5 s), else the run fails, the original is put back and the row says why ("did not follow the program's volume" when it stayed loud, else "went silent when the original was silenced"). A pipe that restarts for a new process closes the replay until the make-up is set again.
- Status: "Effects ready for when it plays" (`SoundModes.EffectsReady`, state Waiting, so the FX window shows no delay) while armed and silent; "Effects running by recording · about N ms delay" while sound passes and for `Timing.EngineIdleAfter` (2 s) after; "Effects start when the program plays" while it is closed. `EngineRun.ShowWaiting` no longer re-sends an unchanged status.
- Detection: `Core/Engine/Audio/CoreAudio.SessionWatcher.cs` (Windows' `IAudioSessionNotification` on every active playback device, re-attached on device changes) and `App/Audio/MixerService.Watch.cs`: a scan at once on a new session (a burst becomes one scan), and every `Timing.ProgramSoundPoll` (150 ms) a quick `AudioSession.IsPlayingNow` look at the sessions of programs with effects; a change scans at once. The full scan stays at 2 s. A cable run (Virtual cable, Automatic with a free pair) now starts within about 150 ms of the program playing instead of up to 2 s (its first moments are still dry until Windows moves the stream: not measured). `IDeviceNotify` / `NotifyKey` moved from `MasterWatcher` to `CoreAudio.Interop.cs` (shared by both watchers).
- Restore paths (unchanged mechanism, now covered by tests at the planner level): effects off, last effect disabled, mode change (the spec changes, the run stops and puts the volume back, the new mode's run arms again), a failed run, the Test (stops the run first), exit (`EffectsEngine.Dispose`), a crash (`engine-journal.json`, written before the original is touched).

**Expected, not a bug:** Windows' Volume mixer shows every OPEN program that has effects (recorded) at 0, also while it is paused (before: only after it had played once). OmniControl's own slider keeps the level.

**Tests** (120 pass; 107 before): `RecordingCheckTests` (7, made-up clock: running at the first look with sound (the start-up budget: no tick, scan or probe time stands between the first sound and the processed copy), ready after the idle time and running again at once, silent recording fails after 1.5 s with its reason, a recording that stays loud fails with the volume reason, loud blocks before the silencing then quiet sound runs, silence never fails, mute and volume 0 pass full-level sound); `RoutePlannerTests`: Spotify open and paused is armed and playing keeps the same run and bus; Virtual cable never armed while silent; Recording armed even with a free pair; Helium opening, playing, Spotify pausing, Helium closing never change Spotify's run or the bus; the run ends with effects off, all effects disabled, an untestable PC, a closed program in Fast, and is kept while closed in Full; two tests updated (a silent Helium with no pair left is now armed by recording instead of waiting).

**Not verified.** The app itself was not run (the owner's running OmniControl was not stopped): the measurements used the same Core pieces in the same order in a probe, not `ProgramEngine` / `MixerService` (the app adds a UI-thread hop and a 6-16 ms scan before turning down a brand-new session; the measured headroom is about 120 ms). Not tried with Spotify, Helium or any real program: whether Spotify keeps its session while paused (if it closes it, the "fresh session" path applies, measured above with the probe); a program with several processes; the status words on screen; Fast mode on the owner's PC.

**Open items.**
- A program closed while silenced keeps 0.0001 in Windows' per-program memory; OmniControl restores it when the program next opens while OmniControl runs (its saved values, or the journal at the next start). Opened while OmniControl is NOT running, it starts near silent. This existed for recorded programs before; arming makes it apply to every open program with effects (a browser with effects is silenced all the time it is open). A fix would need Windows' undocumented per-program volume store.
- The probe left 18 entries named `...\scratchpad\probe\t*\OcOnsetProbe.exe` in `mixer.json` (all at default values, so they are not rows), and Windows remembers a volume of 100 % for each in its per-program store; harmless, remove them from the file while the app is stopped if wanted.
- A cable run that starts on sound is still dry for its first moments (the stream is moved after it started).

**Owner test steps** (after the next build; Full control, Spotify with its EQ, Automatic)
1. Open Spotify, do not play: within a moment its row says "Effects ready for when it plays"; Windows' Volume mixer shows Spotify at 0.
2. Press play: the very first note is already processed (the +24 dB at 1 kHz is there from the start, no dry start); the row says "Effects running by recording · about N ms delay".
3. Pause: about 2 s later the row says "Effects ready for when it plays"; play again: processed at once.
4. Close Spotify while playing, open it again and play: processed from the first note (its row says "Effects start when the program plays" while it is closed).
5. Switch Spotify's effects off while it is paused: Windows' mixer shows it at your volume again; on again: "Effects ready".
6. Helium (EQ on) open with no video: its row says "Effects ready"; a video's first sound is processed. Helium without effects plays through the master as before.
7. Fast mode: the same steps 1-3 (there the processed copy plays on the program's own device).
8. If a row ever says "Effects stopped: the recording ...", press Test on it and send me the result.

## Short, honest row status and the Method menu (14 Nov 2026) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 158/158 pass; nothing run, seen or heard; the running app was not stopped

Owner: the line under each program wrapped, flickered and was long; the program should say which method really runs; choosing Virtual cable with no free cable ran silently without effects.

- **One-line status on every Mixer row** (master, programs, microphones, combined): "Method · NN ms" with the method really in use: `Recording · 70 ms`, `Cable · 10 ms`, `Bus · 40 ms` (master through the cable bus; also a fan-out or microphone run through its cable shows `Cable`), `APO` (Equalizer APO, no figure). Other states: `Recording ready` (armed, silent), `Waiting for sound`, `Starts when it plays` (closed), `Starting`, `Checking`, `No free cable`, `Needs VB-CABLE`, `Needs another cable`, `Needs Full control`, `Can't record`, `Nothing plugged in`, `Needs APO`, `APO write failed`, `Recording went silent` / `Bus went silent`, `Windows would not move it`, `Not on its cable yet`, `Engine stopped`. All wording lives in `Audio/SoundModes.Status.cs` (`SoundModes.Brief`); the full explanation, the method's meaning and what to do next are the status text's tooltip (`EngineStatus.TipFor` = the FX window's long line + `SoundModes.Advice`). The status and the Test note are `NoWrap` with an ellipsis as the last resort. The FX window keeps its longer line (`EngineStatus.Text`/`LineFor`), built by the same runs.
- **Unit for the delay**: the owner said "no 'ms' wording", the brief said `Recording · 69 ms`; I followed the brief's examples. The unit is the one constant `DelayUnit` in `SoundModes.Status.cs` (set it to `""` to drop it).
- **Steady delay** (`Audio/Engine/StableDelay.cs`, tests `StatusWordsTests`): rounded to 5 ms (`Timing.DelayStepMs`), the last good figure stays while a reading is 0, and a different figure is adopted only when the same new rounded value was read 5 engine ticks in a row (`Timing.DelayStableReadings`). It is counted in readings (engine ticks), not time, so the tests need no clock. `EffectsEngine.LoadOf` (the FX window's Latency) uses the same steady figure.
- **Test result**: the row shows `Test passed · 70 ms` or `Test failed · went silent` (also `not listed`, `not playing`, `can't record`, `heard nothing`, `Testing`); the long sentence is the note's tooltip. The footer shows the same short line and the full text as its tooltip: `Services/StatusMessage.cs` puts both in one string (`Report` stays an `Action<string>` everywhere; `ShellViewModel.Report` splits it into `Status` and `StatusTip`). A failed run's footer line is `<name>: <short status>` with the long sentence in the tooltip. Any other footer message is unchanged (no second part = the same text for both).
- **Method pop-down is honest** (`SoundModes.OfferOf`, `Pages/Volume/ProgramRouteChoice`): Virtual cable is greyed with `Not available: no cable pair installed` / `the master bus uses the cable` / `every cable pair is in use` (description and tooltip) and cannot be picked; Recording is greyed only when recording is impossible on this PC (`SoundModes.CaptureMissing`); Automatic says `Currently: recording` / `Currently: cable` while a run exists. The state is read again as the pop-down opens (`ProgramRouteChoice.Refresh`). Facts: `EffectsEngine.CablesFor(key)` (pairs from the last plan, a free pair, the bus holds one; a program holding a cable counts as having one) and `EffectsEngine.MethodOf(key)`; `RoutePlan.CablePairs` / `CableFree`.
- **No silent no-effects on Virtual cable**: the planner now blocks a program set to Virtual cable with no free pair at once, silent or playing (`No free cable`, or `Needs VB-CABLE` when none is installed), and its row offers a `Use Recording` link (`Button.link small`, the row's `Fix` slot, `ProgramRouteChoice.UseRecording`) that sets the method to Recording in one click when recording is possible. Virtual cable stays strict: no automatic fallback.
- **Wiring tab and mode card**: the Cable column reads the same `EngineStatus.Brief` (it also shows `No free cable` for a program now: `SoundModes.IsCableMissing(kind)`); the constant `NeedsAnotherCable` is now "Needs another cable" (was "Needs another virtual cable"). The mode card's tags are unchanged (they state the mode's method in `ModeMethod`, a different list: what the mode does, not what a row does now).
- **Skipped / unverified**: nothing seen on screen, so the width of the status beside a row's links at the narrowest window is reasoned, not checked (longest status "Windows would not move it", 25 characters, trims with an ellipsis before it wraps). The disabled card's look is the master `Button.optioncard` disabled state, not checked. "Checking" is the short word for the first-sound check (`Effects starting (checking the recording)`). The mode-card tags (E) were left alone as above. The recording-impossible reason is shown as "Not available: needs Windows 10 version 2004 or later" (the existing `CaptureMissing` wording, lower-cased).

## Virtual cables of any maker: detection and the Wiring tab's Virtual cables group (14 Nov 2026; built to `docs/mockups/virtual-cables-group.html`) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 169/169 pass; nothing run, seen or heard; the running app was not stopped

Owner: "so that it's not exclusive to VB ... detect the virtual cable it wants" and "a cable management tab ... so the user can custom manage the cables ... any or multiple cables from multiple companies".

- **Detection, one list** (`Core/Engine/Audio/VirtualCables.cs`, `Drivers`, with its source comment): VB-CABLE ("CABLE Input / In 16ch / Output (VB-Audio Virtual Cable)"), VB-CABLE A-D ("CABLE-A Input (VB-Audio Cable A)"), VB-Audio Hi-Fi Cable, Virtual Audio Cable by Eugene Muzychenko ("Line 1 (Virtual Audio Cable)" on both sides) are passive cables; Voicemeeter (all its VAIO drivers) is listed as a mixer and Voicemod as a voice changer, never used. Unknown devices are not guessed. This PC's endpoint list was read (read only, `Get-PnpDevice -Class AudioEndpoint`): it matches the `OwnerPc` fixture (one VB-CABLE, two inputs, one output).
- **Pairing**: a usable cable is a playback side plus the recording side of the same driver that carries its sound (the existing name rule); extra inputs join their pair ("CABLE In 16ch"). A device with no other end is listed "Input only" / "Output only" and **not used**. Behaviour change: a VB-CABLE whose "CABLE Output" is disabled in Windows used to be given to the bus or a program (they record the playback side by loopback); now it is "Input only, not used", as the brief asked. Say if that should be relaxed for bus and program runs.
- **Stable id** per cable = its recording side's endpoint id (`FoundCable.Id`; a marked pair's too). Switches are stored as `MixerSettings.ForbiddenCables` (ids switched off; by default every cable is allowed), marked pairs as `MixerSettings.MarkedCables` (`CableMark`: name, playback id, recording id). Older `mixer.json` files load unchanged (both lists empty).
- **One source of allowed cables**: the probe returns every virtual device (`AudioEffectsSupport.Found`) and every usable pair; `SoundModeService` leaves out the ones switched off (`VirtualCables.Usable`) and publishes that as `Current`, so the planner (`CableBox`), the engine, the Combine window, the Method menu, the Wiring Cable column, the top bar's "Ready · N virtual cables" and the Full control card all read only ALLOWED cables. A switch applies at once (no new device look-up); a marked pair triggers a fresh look-up. The count is now of pairs (`SoundModes.AllowedPairs`), so the owner's PC says "Ready · 1 virtual cable" (it said "2" before: VB-CABLE's two inputs were counted).
- **All cables switched off** is its own fact (`StatusKind.CablesOff`, "Cables switched off", advice "Switch a cable on under Wiring > Virtual cables."), not "Needs VB-CABLE"; Install VB-CABLE shows only when no cable pair is installed at all (`SoundModes.CableInstalled`), and "Remove VB-CABLE" stays on the card while one is.
