- **Wiring tab, Virtual cables group** (under Microphones, collapsible, "N found" = the cable pairs found, allowed or not): one row per virtual device: switch (`ToggleSwitch.tool`, greyed where it can't be used), name (tooltip: maker), ends tag ("Input + Output", "Input only", "Output only", "Not plugged in"), state tag ("Free" good, "In use"), at the right "Use for: master bus" / "Use for: <row name>" (the engine's live run, `EffectsEngine.RowOnCable`) / "Use for: Automatic" (free), or "Not used: switched off / no matching output / no matching input / a mixer, not a cable / a voice changer, not a cable / not plugged in" with the row dimmed (`VolumeRow.QuietOpacity`, now one constant for both). A marked pair has an x that forgets it. Under the list the dashed "+ This device is a cable..." (`Button.blockadd.small`) opens a pop-down (`FlyoutPresenter.listmenu`): "Sound goes into" and "Comes out of" drop-downs (`ChoiceItem`, only devices not already known as cable ends), a name (filled from the playback device until typed), Add. Then the muted hint "Add more cables to run more programs on cables at once." with the link "Get more cables" (VB-Audio's page, `EngineInstaller.OpenCablePage`) and, where no cable is installed, Install VB-CABLE.
- **Search**: every cable row is a search entry under "Virtual cables" (keywords "virtual cable cables vb vb-cable hi-fi voicemeeter voicemod line manage allow switch mark"), read from the group's live rows.
- **Tests** (`tests/OmniControl.Tests/VirtualCableTests.cs`, 11): owner PC one pair (both inputs, one id, count 1); three makers (VB-CABLE, Hi-Fi, VAC Line 1) are three pairs and real devices are never taken; Voicemeeter input only is listed, not used; a VB-CABLE without its output is "Input only"; a user-marked unknown pair is used like any cable and unknown devices are not guessed; a mark of a recognised cable is not listed twice; Automatic: the bus gets the first cable by name, programs the others; a forbidden cable is never used, not even by the bus; every allowed cable in use gives "No free cable"; every cable switched off says so; an old mixer.json loads with every cable allowed.

**Differences from the mock-up.** The "Needs a restart" state tag is not shown: Windows gives no reliable sign that a just-installed cable needs a restart (the installer's own result message already says it may). The mock-up's "Free" on the bus's cable is shown as "In use" with "Use for: master bus" (what is true while the bus runs). A held pair with no run of its own (a combined device's remembered cable whose devices are unplugged) says "Use for: kept for its device".

**Skipped / unverified.** Nothing seen on screen: the row's fit at the narrowest window (switch, name up to `WiringSourceMaxWidth`, two tags, and the right-hand words, which wrap) and both themes are reasoned only; the pop-down's `ChoiceRow` cards inside a `listmenu` pop-down are unverified for looks. The second maker's real device names come from vendor pages and user reports, not from a second installed cable. Keyboard: the rows' switch and x are row parts (Left/Right, Enter); the chip, the link and Install are header stops; inside the pop-down mouse first (rule 5), Esc closes it.

**Open items.**
- A pair the user marked is not yet hidden from the "plays on" device lists, the master's real-device choice (`MasterDevice`) or the microphone lists, which still use `VirtualCables.IsCable(name)` (recognised cables only); only the Equalizer APO microphone list leaves marked pairs out. Fix by making those lists ask the found cables by id.
- The Method menu's "Not available: no cable pair installed" also shows when every cable is switched off (it counts allowed pairs).

**Owner test steps** (after the next build)
1. With only VB-CABLE installed: Volume > Wiring shows a "Virtual cables  1 found" group under Microphones with one row "CABLE", switch on, "Input + Output", "In use", "Use for: master bus" (Full control) or "Free", "Use for: Automatic" (Fast mode). The top bar says "Ready · 1 virtual cable".
2. Switch CABLE off: the row dims, "Not used: switched off"; the top bar says "Cables switched off"; in Full control the master plays without effects ("Cables switched off" on its row). Switch it on again: the bus comes back.
3. Click "+ This device is a cable...": the pop-down lists your Realtek speaker and microphones (VB-CABLE's devices are not offered). Esc closes it. Do not add a real speaker and microphone pair unless you mean it; the x on its row forgets it.

After installing a second maker's cable (for example VB-Audio Hi-Fi Cable or VB-CABLE A+B) and restarting if its installer asks: the group says "2 found", the new cable's row is "Input + Output", "Free", "Use for: Automatic"; the top bar says "Ready · 2 virtual cables"; in Full control a processed program on Automatic now gets the second cable ("Cable · NN ms" on its row, "Use for: Spotify" on the cable's row).
6. If Voicemeeter or Voicemod is installed, it is listed dimmed ("Not used: a mixer, not a cable" / "a voice changer, not a cable").

## The master's method, numbered setups, the setup table and the badge (14 Nov 2026; built to `docs/mockups/help-card-table.html` and the owner's later 8-row list) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 199/199 pass (169 before + 30 new); nothing run, seen or heard; no Equalizer APO file, registry value or audio setting written; the running app was not stopped

Owner: "add the functionality for all the options", "indicate the method the master track is running on ... just like the apps", "the user can change the settings they want per device by changing the method, including on the master track, and the list would be honest", the Help button and table on the Full control card with the three buttons centred, then: the Sound mode button on the Mixer tab too, a live setup number badge, a remembered Full control setup with a calm fall back.

**As built**
- **The master's method** (Full control): `MixerSettings.MasterMethod` ("" = Automatic, "bus", "apo"; old files load as Automatic). `SoundModes.MasterRoutes` / `MasterMethodOf` (`Audio/SoundModes.Methods.cs`, which now also holds the program routes; `ProgramRoute` became `EffectsRoute` for both). Automatic = the cable bus while a cable pair is allowed (or the master plays on several devices, or Equalizer APO can't run its effects), else Equalizer APO: so on the owner's PC Automatic is today's bus, and a PC without a cable now runs the master in Equalizer APO instead of "Needs VB-CABLE" (a change from "Automatic = today's behaviour" only where today's behaviour did nothing). Fast mode always runs the master in Equalizer APO.
- **Equalizer APO on the speakers in Full control** (setups 2-5, 7): `RoutePlanner` takes no bus cable; the master's chain goes to `ToApo` for the REAL speakers (`MasterApoDevice` from `MasterDevice.Real`; `ApoChains.RowsFor` writes its `Device: {speakers}` section, the same writer and include file as Fast mode); no bus run, so `BusDefault` gives Windows' default back to the speakers (only while OmniControl's cable is still the default) and `CableVolumeMirror` stops: the volume keys act on the speakers natively. Programs without effects play straight on the default (no `BusRoutes`); a program with effects on a cable plays its processed copy on the speakers, a recorded one replays there: each copy passes the speakers' Equalizer APO chain exactly once (the silenced original at -80 dB passes it too, inaudible). Leaving the method (bus again, or exit) takes OmniControl's master section out (`ApoChains` rewrites or removes the include; tested through a fake store). A master with no effects writes nothing and says `Direct` ("No master effects: it plays straight on the speakers"). Equalizer APO missing with effects: "Equalizer APO not applied" (`StatusKind.ApoNotApplied`), the reason in the tooltip, and the row's fix step (Choose devices, or Install Equalizer APO).
- **Equalizer APO is now read on every playback device** (`ApoState.Playback`, `PlaybackOf(id)`, Core `ApoStatus.Read(default, playback, microphones)`): this fixes the earlier open item "ApoStatus sees the cable while Full control runs" for the master's facts; Fast mode still uses `Output` (Windows' default) as before.
- **The cable that is still Windows' default is never given to a program** when the master is off the bus (`CableBox.Reserve`, the whole pair): a test caught that "CABLE In 16ch" was otherwise handed out the moment after the bus let go. It is free again after the next scan, once the speakers are the default.
- **Combined speakers on the free cable** (setup 4): `PlanCombined` already gave them a cable without a bus; tested: the fan-out plays the cable on its members, a program that picked them plays into their cable (its effects by recording, replayed into the combined device), the master stays on one speaker in Equalizer APO. With the master in Equalizer APO the master's Wiring cell is one choice and combined speakers are dimmed in its menu with "Equalizer APO runs the master on one speaker. Choose Cable bus as its method to play on several." (`MasterOutputsGate`; `MasterTargets` lists `MasterPlaysOn` only while the master is on the bus).
- **Setup 7 (Equalizer APO on a second cable for one program) is built**: a program Method "Equalizer APO on a cable" (`RouteApoCable`, id "apo-cable"); the planner gives it a whole free pair whose playback side has Equalizer APO (Device Selector tick) and a recording side, only when 2+ pairs are allowed (the owner's rule); `RunKind.ApoCable` = `CableRoute` that records the cable's RECORDING side (where Equalizer APO's output arrives; a loopback of the playback side might be taken before the APO) with no chain of its own and plays it where the program plays; the program's chain goes to Equalizer APO under that cable's `Device:` line; a failed write stops the run (`EffectsEngine.FailRun`). Greyed reasons: "needs a second cable pair", "Equalizer APO is not on a cable", "the cable with Equalizer APO is in use". Its row says `APO cable · NN ms`; the FX window treats the row as Equalizer APO (VST3 not run).
- **Method link on the master row** (`Pages/Volume/RouteChoice` + `RouteButton`, shared with the programs; `ForMaster` / `ForProgram`): "Method: Automatic v", pop-down "How the master runs" with Automatic ("Currently: cable bus" / "Currently: Equalizer APO"), Cable bus (greyed "Not available: needs VB-CABLE" / "cables switched off"), Equalizer APO (greyed with Equalizer APO's own reason on the speakers, or "the master plays on several devices"; available with no master effects: "nothing needs installing"); the fix step (Choose devices / Install Equalizer APO) sits under the cards. A pick applies at once. **Fast mode: the link is hidden** (the master always runs in Equalizer APO there and its status says "APO"). The master's status stays "Bus · NN" / "APO" / "Direct"; the cable's use ("Cable: the master bus", "Cable: free", "Cable: a program"...) is only in the status tooltip (one status line had no room).
- **Setups** (`Audio/SoundModes.Help.cs`): the 8 rows with their exact words, `WorksOf` (live: "Available" or the first missing thing; cables switched off by setup 5 count as there), `SetupNumber` (THE live number from the master's method and what the cables carry: `RoutePlan.CableUses`), `SetupOf` (what choosing a row sets), `RestoreSetup` / `SetupToRemember`. `MixerService.ApplySetup` (`MixerService.Setups.cs`) applies a row in one step: mode, the master's method (1 and 6 = Automatic, i.e. the bus while a cable is allowed; 2, 3, 4, 7 = Equalizer APO; 5 = Equalizer APO and the allowed pairs nothing of the user's holds are switched off, remembered in `MixerSettings.FreedCables`; any other row switches those back on; switching a cable by hand forgets it from that list). What the free cable then carries follows from the user's rows: a program with effects takes it when it plays (2), a combined microphone / combined speakers the user made (3 / 4: greyed "Combine microphones first" / "Combine speakers first" until one exists), a program set to "Equalizer APO on a cable" (7).
- **Setup memory**: `MixerSettings.FullSetup` (0 = none, old files load as 0). Choosing Full control restores it (first time: setup 1); if it can't run now, setup 1 and one footer line: "Setup 2 can't run now (Equalizer APO is not on this device), so Full control uses setup 1." Leaving Full control remembers the live setup (an unused free cable counts as 2 unless setup 5 kept it free) and switches setup 5's cables back on (Fast mode's combined microphones need them).
- **Badge**: the Sound mode button shows "Sound mode: Full control (3) v" with the live number in a small accent circle (`Border.badge`, new master in `Styles/Tables.axaml`, sizes `Badge*` in `App.axaml`); its tooltip names the setup ("Setup 3: master on Equalizer APO, the cable used for a combined microphone. Click for the table."); a click on the badge opens the table (the button underneath does not press; the panel's Help does the same from the keyboard).
- **Mixer tab**: the old "Full control mode" link (`ModeIndicator`, `ShowSoundModeCommand`) is removed; the same `SoundModeButton` bound to the same `SoundModeChoice` sits at the right of the tab row.
- **Help button and the card's buttons**: Full control's card now has More cables, Remove VB-CABLE, Help (or Install VB-CABLE, Help), all `Button.pill.cardaction`; the card's action column has one width (`CardActionWidth` 120 in `App.axaml`), the buttons stretch to it and the column is centred down the card on both cards (fixed in the panel's template, not per button).
- **The help card** (`SetupTableWindow`, an owned window like the FX windows, one at a time, Esc closes, 820 wide, min 560, scrolls past 640 high): only the table, built from `SetupTable` (one data source, `SoundModes.HelpCombinations`): a header row, `Border.tablegroup` bands with the notes, one `Button.tablerow` per setup with the number (`Border.badge.quiet`, filled for the live one), the four columns (shared star columns, wrapping text; the APO-limited master effects in `TextBlock.cautiontext`) and the live Works tag (`Border.tag.good` / `.caution`, wraps past `TableTagMaxWidth`). The live setup is highlighted (`.current`, "you are here" in its tooltip); clicking a row that works applies it; a row that can't is greyed and says why. It follows the engine while open.
- New master styles: `Styles/Tables.axaml` (`Button.tablerow`, `Border.tablegroup`, `Border.badge` (`.quiet`), `TextBlock.cautiontext`), included after `Bars.axaml`; documented in AGENTS.md and the Theme.axaml index. Theme.axaml stays at 699 lines.

**Differences from the mock-up**
- 8 rows (the owner's later list) instead of the mock-up's 6; the Works column shows "Available" or the live reason instead of "today" / "new setting" (as the mock-up's comment asks after the build).
- "Any, with VST3 ..." is in the normal text colour (the mock-up's green); only the limited "APO only ..." cells use the caution colour (one coloured fact per row, calm wording).
- The number circles are the master badge (outlined, filled for the live row) rather than the mock-up's grey ring for every row.
- The header row is muted small text with a divider, the group bands use the hover tint (`BrushHover`), not the mock-up's literal greys.

**Keyboard and mouse (rule 5, the owner may overrule)**: the badge is mouse only (Help on the Full control card opens the same table and is keyboard reachable in the panel); the table window is mouse first: its rows are buttons (Tab and Enter work), there are no Up/Down keys inside it.

**Skipped / unverified**
- Nothing seen on screen: the badge inside the pill, the pill's height on the tab row (it uses the pill's own 24 px, the tabs' words may be a little shorter), the action column's look with two-line labels, the table window in both themes and at its minimum width, the disabled row look (Fluent's disabled foreground plus the tag colours).
- Nothing heard or written: the master section in Equalizer APO's config for the speakers in Full control, the default going back to the speakers when the method changes, the volume keys afterwards, a program on its cable playing on the speakers through Equalizer APO once, combined speakers on the free cable.
- Setup 7 is unverified end to end: whether VB-CABLE's playback endpoints accept an APO on this PC (Device Selector lists them on many PCs) and that the cable's recording side carries Equalizer APO's output (expected: the driver receives the processed render stream). The user's own config.txt lines without a `Device:` line would also run on that cable (said in the route's tooltip).
- `BusDefault`'s Windows calls are not faked: the tests cover its pure rule (`StepFor`, `PutsBack`) and that the APO plan has no bus.
- The badge's number during the moment after the master leaves the bus can read 5 before the program's cable run starts (it is the live state).

**Open items**
- The Wiring Cable column's tooltip for a program on a cable with Equalizer APO still says "Sound goes through X to the engine".
- `RestoreSetup` checks a remembered setup against the last plan's facts, which in Fast mode name Equalizer APO on Windows' default device (the speakers in Fast), not a cable-specific state.

**Owner test steps** (after the next build)
1. Volume > Mixer: the tab row's right side shows "Sound mode: Full control (1) v" (or "Fast (8)"). Hover the number: one line naming the setup. Click the number: the "Sound setups" window with only the table; your row is highlighted; rows you can't use are greyed with the reason (for example "Needs a second cable pair").
2. Open the Sound mode panel: the Full control card's column reads More cables, Remove VB-CABLE, Help, all the same width and centred down the card. Help opens the same table.
3. **One-time step for Equalizer APO on the speakers**: Fast mode card > Choose devices > in Equalizer APO's Device Selector tick "Speaker (Realtek(R) Audio)" on the Playback tab (leave CABLE Input unticked), OK, restart if it asks.
4. Full control, master row: "Method: Automatic v" shows "Currently: cable bus". Choose Equalizer APO: within a second Windows' default playback device is Speaker again (Sound settings), the master row says "APO" (with effects) or "Direct" (none), the badge says 5 (or 2 once a program with effects plays and takes CABLE Input: its row says "Cable · NN ms"), and the volume keys change the speakers. Hover the master's status: the last line says what the cable is used for.
5. In the table click setup 1: the default goes back to CABLE Input, the master says "Bus · NN". Click setup 5: the cable's switch under Wiring > Virtual cables turns off ("Cables switched off" shows there); click setup 2: it turns on again.
6. Make a combined microphone, pick setup 3: it takes CABLE Input ("Apps record it from CABLE Output"); make combined speakers instead and pick setup 4: programs that pick them play on all their members.
7. Switch to Fast and back to Full control: the setup you had comes back; untick the speakers in Device Selector, switch Fast and back: the footer says the setup can't run now and Full control uses setup 1.
8. With only one cable: a program's Method "Equalizer APO on a cable" is greyed "Not available: needs a second cable pair".

## Trackpad takeover: the shell's own copy; the takeover switch removed; the safety net (14 Nov, later) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 215/215 pass (199 before + 16 new); no touchpad setting written, nothing tapped or pressed, the running app was not stopped

**Owner's report.** With the takeover active, every three-finger tap still opened Windows' search. `trackpad-log.txt` showed `ThreeFingerTap recognised; Windows' three-finger tap (ThreeFingerTapEnabled) = 0 (Nothing); OmniControl: TurnedOff` for every tap (16:48-16:52), and the registry read 0. Also: the page switch "Turn off Windows' own gestures while OmniControl uses them" should not exist; replacing Windows' gesture is always what a mapping does, unless that action ticks "Also let Windows do its own gesture".

**Root cause: PROVEN.** The shell (`twinui.dll` in explorer.exe) acts on its own in-memory copy of the three/four-finger settings and does not re-read the registry when the value changes.
- PROVEN live (read only, 14 Nov ~17:05): asked the shell itself through its touchpad settings service getter (below, slot 5, a pure read; no setter was called): id 15 (three-finger tap) = **1 (Open search)** while `HKCU\...\PrecisionTouchPad\ThreeFingerTapEnabled` = **0**. Also id 16 (four-finger tap) = 2, id 17 (three-finger swipes) = 1, id 18 (four-finger swipes) = 2, the shell's defaults. So the registry said Nothing and Windows still searched: the earlier guess "the shell watches the key" (`twinui` imports `RegNotifyChangeKeyValue`) is DISPROVEN.
- PROVEN (static, `dumpbin /disasm` of `C:\Windows\System32\SettingsHandlers_nt.dll`, build 26300): after writing a touchpad gesture value under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\PrecisionTouchPad` (helper at RVA 0x1F892B), the Settings app calls 0x2060F0 with the setting's id: it creates the immersive shell (`CLSID_ImmersiveShell` {C2F03A33-21F5-47FA-B4BB-156362A2F239}, `CLSCTX_LOCAL_SERVER`), asks its `IServiceProvider` for service {53660488-8855-460B-A9AB-5CFC6B5012CA} with interface {4214F6FA-EB36-4E2F-9CA2-23FDC1832DF7} (proxy registered: `OneCoreUAPCommonProxyStub.dll`) and calls vtable slot 3 with the id alone. No WM_SETTINGCHANGE, SPI or WNF call is on that path. The Settings app's own table (file offset 0x316DE0) gives the ids: 0x0F `ThreeFingerTapEnabled`, 0x10 `FourFingerTapEnabled`, 0x11 `ThreeFingerSlideEnabled`, 0x12 `FourFingerSlideEnabled`, 0x14-0x27 the per-direction custom values and key params, 0x24/0x26 `CustomThreeFingerTap`/`CustomFourFingerTap`. The Settings app reads the shell's values through slot 5 (`GetValue(id, out value)`, special case for 0x0F/0x10).
- PROVEN (static, `twinui.dll`): the service lives in the shell (both GUIDs are in `twinui.dll`, near `touchpadgestureprovider\lib\touchpadsettingsmanager.cpp`); its vtable at file offset 0x3CB738 has slot 5 = 0x80B90, a getter that returns cached fields by the same ids (0x0E-0x12 single fields, 0x14-0x27 an array) under a shared lock; slot 3 = 0x2D7900, which calls 0x5E838, the code that posts the shell's "SettingsCacheChangeMessage" (refreshes that cache).
- SUSPECTED (very likely, not tried on this PC because it changes what the shell does): calling slot 3 with the id after the registry write makes the shell take the new value at once, as the Settings app's change does. The app now verifies this itself on every write (below), so the log will prove or disprove it on the first tap.
- Tap to click, two-finger tap and pinch to zoom are not affected: they are TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS fields applied by Windows' input (`win32kbase.sys`) through SPI_SETTOUCHPADPARAMETERS (documented); the shell has no copy of them.

**What changed (the main path).**
- `Core/Engine/Trackpad/ShellTouchpadSettings.cs` (new): the immersive shell's service, slot 3 `SettingChanged(id)` (refresh) and slot 5 `GetValue(id, out value)` (what the shell acts on); slot 4 is a placeholder that is never called. COM objects released after each call; a shell that is restarting or a Windows without the service gives one sentence, never an exception.
- `WindowsGestureSetting.ShellId` (15 / 17 / 18 on the three Registry entries; a test checks every Registry entry has one and no other does).
- `ITouchpadSettingsStore`: new `Announce(setting)` (make Windows act on the stored value now; no-op for the TOUCHPAD_PARAMETERS ones) and `ReadInEffect(setting, out value)` (the shell's copy, or the stored value). `TouchpadSettingsStore`: the WM_SETTINGCHANGE broadcast is gone (Settings does not use it; `CoreTiming.SettingChangeTimeoutMs` removed); a registry write is followed by `Announce`.
- `GestureTakeover`: every write is read back twice: stored (unchanged rule) and, after `Announce`, in effect. Windows still acting on its old value = `Failed` with "Windows still acts on its old three-finger tap setting (1) and didn't take the new one.", but the original stays kept (the stored value is OmniControl's), and every later `Update` (page open, mapping change, touchpad arrival) and `Check` (any write to the key, and the 3 s backstop; at most once a second per gesture, log lines capped as before) tells the shell again; once it takes it the status goes back to `TurnedOff`. A stored "off" the shell never took (the state the owner's PC is in now, left by the previous build) is announced at the next start without writing anything; so is a stored "Nothing" the user chose but the shell never took. Giving back also announces (Windows' own action returns at once).
- Log lines now say both values: `... was missing; set to 0, reads back 0, Windows acts on 0` and per gesture `ThreeFingerTap recognised; Windows' three-finger tap (ThreeFingerTapEnabled) = 0 (Nothing), Windows acts on 0 (Nothing); OmniControl: TurnedOff`.

**The switch removed.** The Trackpad group's "Turn off Windows' own gestures while OmniControl uses them" card, `MouseSettings.TakeOverWindowsGestures` (an old file's value, true or false, is ignored: tested with `false`), `TrackpadService.IsTakeoverOn/SetTakeover` and the hotkey "Toggle turning off Windows' own touchpad gestures" (`mouse.trackpad.takeover.toggle`; a binding saved for it stays in `hotkeys.json` unused, a mapping to it reports the action missing) are gone. The takeover follows only "Remap mouse and trackpad" and each action's "Also let Windows do its own gesture" (`TrackpadService.WantedOff`, rule `MouseSettings.ReplacesWindows`). "Windows touchpad settings" is now the last card of the group (half width, alone on the last row), so the sixteen gesture cards pair from the first row. Tags: unchanged rules; the caution "Windows' own still on" now also shows when the shell kept its old value (the tooltip gives that sentence); the neutral "Windows also does this" only when the option is ticked and Windows' gesture is on. The group's tip no longer mentions a switch.

**The safety net (never the main path).** `Core/Engine/Trackpad/ShellSurfaceGuard.cs` + `ShellSurfaceProbe.cs`: when a gesture whose Windows action opens a panel (`TrackpadGesture.Opens`: three-finger tap and double tap = Search or the notification centre; three/four fingers up = Task view) is recognised and `TrackpadService.Guards` says so (remapping on, its first action on the trackpad layer in force is not Default and does not tick "Also let Windows do its own gesture", and none of its actions sends a shortcut that opens a panel itself: any Win+ shortcut or Alt+Tab, `KeyChord.OpensWindowsPanel`), the window in front is noted on the watcher thread before the action runs. Then, every `Timing.TrackpadSurfaceLook` (20 ms) for `CoreTiming.TrackpadSurfaceWatchMs` (700 ms), if a window of that panel (`ShellSurfaces.All`: SearchHost/SearchApp; ShellExperienceHost/ShellHost; explorer's `XamlExplorerHostIslandWindow`/`MultitaskingViewFrame`) comes to the front, Esc is sent (pressed and released in one SendInput) only while that window is still in front, at most `CoreTiming.TrackpadSurfaceDismissLimit` (20) a minute. Never touched: a panel that was already in front at the gesture, any other window (File Explorer, programs), anything after 700 ms, gestures at Default, gestures whose action keeps Windows' own. Each close is one log line (`Three-finger tap: Windows opened Search 180 ms later although its own gesture is off; closed it (Esc)`). Limits (honest): the panel can flash briefly before it closes; Windows' actions without a window (show desktop, switching apps or desktops, play/pause, middle click, clicks, zoom) cannot be undone this way, only prevented by the main path; a panel that opens without taking the front (rare) is not seen; on another Windows language the process and class names are the same, but on a future build they may differ (the master list is the one place to fix).

**Shared settings (caveat, unchanged).** One Windows setting covers several gestures: mapping only "Three fingers up" turns off all of Windows' three-finger swipes; mapping a one-finger tap or double tap turns off Windows' tap to click entirely (then a light tap no longer clicks anywhere; pressing the touchpad still does); a two-finger tap mapping turns off tap-to-right-click. The four-finger tap has no OmniControl gesture, so Windows' own four-finger tap is never touched.

**Not verified (no touch, no write).** That slot 3 makes the shell take the value (SUSPECTED, see above); that Esc closes each panel on build 26300 (it does for Search, the notification centre and Task view when pressed by hand on Windows 11, by design); the panels' process and class names were read from this PC only for Task view's host (`XamlExplorerHostIslandWindow`, here titled "Task Switching" for Alt+Tab) and the running processes (SearchHost, ShellExperienceHost, ShellHost), not with a panel open; the page at the narrowest window and in both themes (one card removed, one moved; same card types).

**Owner test steps.**
1. Build and launch. Mouse and Trackpad > Trackpad: no "Turn off Windows' own gestures..." card; the gesture cards pair from the first row; "Windows touchpad settings" is the last card. Shortcuts: no "Toggle turning off Windows' own touchpad gestures".
2. Open `%APPDATA%\OmniControl\trackpad-log.txt`: after start a line `Windows' three-finger tap (ThreeFingerTapEnabled) is 0 but Windows still acted on 1; told it to read the setting again, now acts on 0` = the fix works (PROVEN then). `now acts on 1` = the shell refused: the card shows the caution tag; tell the agent.
3. Tap with three fingers many times: no search, only your action. Each tap logs `... = 0 (Nothing), Windows acts on 0 (Nothing); OmniControl: TurnedOff`. If search still flashes, the line `Three-finger tap: Windows opened Search ... closed it (Esc)` shows the safety net caught it; send the log.
4. Set the card to Default: Windows' search on a three-finger tap is back at once (log: `given back: set to missing, reads back missing, Windows acts on missing`).
5. Give it the action again and tick "Also let Windows do its own gesture": search opens with your action, the neutral tag shows, and nothing is closed by the safety net. Untick: search stops.
6. Repeat with "Three fingers up" (Task view) and a three-finger swipe sideways (switch apps): Windows' own swipes stop while mapped.
7. Restart Explorer (Task Manager) while mapped: taps still do only your action (the new Explorer reads the registry at start; the 3 s look re-checks).

**How to read the new lines.** `Windows acts on N` = what the shell itself says it does now (not the registry). `... is 0 but Windows still acted on 1; told it ...` = the shell had an old copy and was told. `Windows still acts on its old ... setting` = the shell refused: caution tag on the card. `Windows opened <panel> N ms later although its own gesture is off; closed it (Esc)` = the safety net closed a panel; `it was no longer in front, left alone` = it closed by itself first; `left open, 20 were closed this minute already` = the limit stopped it.

**Open items.** Custom rules with a Trackpad trigger still do not count as "an action uses it" (unchanged); the safety net does not watch them either. The shell service is undocumented: a future Windows build may move it (the store then says so in one sentence and the takeover shows the caution tag; the safety net still works).

### Trackpad layer names are their own (14 Nov; not compiled or run: the build is blocked by the Displays work in progress, which is not mine)

- The upgrade no longer copies mouse layer names to the trackpad. `MouseSettings.TrackpadNamesIndependent` marks that a one-time repair ran: a trackpad name equal to the mouse's name of the same number is taken for the old copy and dropped (a name the owner typed on the trackpad that happens to match is lost: accepted). Mappings stay on their layer numbers.
- The trackpad always lists layers 1-3 even when unused, named "Trackpad 1/2/3" (`LayerSet.TrackpadPresets`, the one place; `DefaultNameOf` also feeds the manager window's placeholder). Clearing a name restores the default. Layers 4+ and the whole mouse family behave as before ("Layer N", layer 1 only unless used).
- Tests added in `LayerFamilyTests`: no copying, repair once, three listed by default (trackpad only), clearing restores the default. Not run.

## Displays: more software and hardware controls, warm-light schedule, presets (14 Nov) - compiled and unit-tested with fakes only; nothing seen on screen, no gamma ramp, DDC/CI write or colour effect was applied on this PC

**Software (per display, one gamma ramp).** `RampMath.Build` is the ONE computation; its order is documented there: contrast about mid-grey, gamma, black and white level (lift and limit), brightness, then per channel the colour temperature times the channel's gain, then the display's own curve (so a calibration is kept). Neutral values leave their step out, so the neutral picture is the original ramp (unit test). New sliders (all in `DisplayControls.Software`, the master list; each gives its card, up/down hotkeys `displays.sw.<key>.up|down`, gesture level `sw.<key>`, OSD element and search entry): Digital gamma (-50..+50, exponent 1 + step/100, 0 = neutral), Digital red/green/blue gain (20..100, 100 = neutral), Digital black level (0..30 % lift) and white level (70..100 % limit). Per-channel gamma was not added (three more cards for little; `RampMath` could take it).

**Software (whole desktop, one colour matrix).** Saturation, Grayscale and Invert cannot be a gamma ramp (a ramp treats each channel alone, and Windows tends to refuse falling ramps), so they are ONE matrix, `ColorMatrix.Build(saturation, invert)`, sent through the existing Magnification colour effect for ALL displays (`ColorEffect.Set`). Grayscale is a switch on saturation 0 that remembers the saturation before it; its card follows the saturation slider. Invert is a switch. Hotkeys: `displays.sw.grayscale`, `displays.sw.invert` (toggles). The brief's "single ramp function" therefore has one deviation: saturation, grayscale and invert are composed in the matrix, the rest in the ramp.

**Warm light at night.** `Models/WarmSchedule.cs` (pure, tested): from/until in half-hour steps (drop-downs, may cross midnight), eases in over the first 30 minutes and out over the last, target temperature 3000-6500 K (default 3400 K). It is a layer over each display's own temperature: the display shows the user's temperature moved towards the target, never cooler than the user chose, and goes back when the window ends. `WarmLightScheduler` looks at the clock every `Timing.WarmLightTick` (30 s) and at once on any change; the card says "Off", "Active until 07:00" or "Starts at 21:00". Action `displays.sw.warmlight` toggles it. Settings in `display-settings.json`. Reset all puts the sliders and the effect back; the schedule, if active, keeps warming (the Software header tip says what is kept). Quitting puts everything back regardless.

**Display presets.** Card "Presets..." opens `DisplayPresetsWindow` (small dialog: name box with Save current / Rename, list with Apply, Rename, Delete; names follow `PresetRules`; saving an existing name replaces it and the note says so). A preset keeps the nine sliders per display (by Windows' display name; a display it has none for gets the first display's picture), the saturation and invert. Stored in `display-settings.json` through `DisplaySettingsStore`. Action `displays.sw.preset.next` cycles the saved presets. Mouse-first window, Esc closes, Tab reaches everything; no list keyboard shortcuts (owner can overrule). UPDATE: the "Display presets" card is gone; the Software group header now has a bare preset drop-down (saved presets, led by "Current settings" while none is saved, else "Custom" when no preset equals the live picture; picking applies at once) and the round cogwheel that opens the same window. Both come from the shared `Pages/HeaderPicker` (the Mouse Buttons and Trackpad headers were moved onto it, same look). The drop-down is a search entry ("display presets manage"); the Next display preset hotkey is unchanged. Open: no other card is a preset card that should be a header drop-down (the per-display groups have no presets, none added). Not seen on screen. UPDATE 14 Nov: `DisplayPresetsWindow` is gone; the cogwheel opens the shared preset manager at the Software card (see "Unified preset manager").

**Driver honesty.** `GammaDisplay.Apply`: when Windows refuses a ramp it writes the last good ramp again (no visible change); if even that is refused the driver takes no ramps from programs (`DriverRefuses`): the display's software cards grey out with "Not supported by this display's driver" and are not tried again until Refresh/F5 (`SoftwareDisplayService.ClearRefusals`). If only the new value was refused, the old message stays ("Windows refused this setting for this display. Try a value closer to normal."). A display whose own ramp cannot be read is treated the same. No probe write is made at start-up.

**Hardware (DDC/CI).** `Core/Models/VcpFeatures.cs` is the one table of MCCS feature codes (key, name, keywords, default); the Displays master list is made from it. Added continuous controls: red/green/blue black level (0x6C/6E/70), backlight white/red/green/blue (0x6B/6D/6F/71), gamma (0x72), hue (0x90), six-axis saturation (0x59-0x5E) and hue (0x9B-0xA0); with the existing brightness, contrast, gains, saturation, sharpness and speaker volume. Choices: colour preset, input source, display mode (0xDC), speaker mute (0x8D) (each with a "Next ..." action `displays.<key>.next`), plus Screen power. Codes and names were written from memory of MCCS 2.2a (no copy of the standard was at hand): a wrong name is a fix in that one table. Sliders are a share of the monitor's own maximum, as before; a feature with no standard default (black level, gamma ...) returns on double-click to the value the monitor had when it was read. Geometry (position, size, clock, phase: analogue inputs only) and the "colour temperature request" code 0x0C were left out.

**Probe.** `MonitorProbe` reads the five core codes first (a monitor with DDC/CI off is still found after three refusals), then the capabilities string ONCE for every monitor that answered (before, only for monitors with inputs or presets: a probe can be slower by that read), then every table code the string lists, or every table code when the monitor gave no list. The string also gives the values of every choice. A code the monitor answers but did not list is not offered.

**Page.** After the probe a monitor's group lists only the cards it has ("N available"); a second group "Not available on <monitor>" (collapsed, "M not reported") holds the rest, greyed with the reason (not reported / no DDC/CI answer / a laptop's panel has only brightness / reported without a range). The laptop panel therefore shows brightness and a collapsed list. Search finds only available hardware cards. The Hardware summary line is unchanged ("1 monitor available").

**Small shared edits.** `SliderItem.ResetValue` is now an observable property (a monitor control's double-click value is known only after reading); `ButtonItem.ButtonText` is observable (the warm-light button reads Turn on / Turn off); both are backwards compatible.

**Tests** (`tests/OmniControl.Tests/DisplayPictureTests.cs`, 51 new): neutral ramp = original, each setting's direction, extremes stay rising, clamping, the colour matrix (neutral, grayscale, invert), the schedule (overnight window, ease, kelvin, state text), the service over a fake ramp (layering, schedule, refusal remembered, dispose restores), presets and settings storage (in memory; nothing is written under the user's folder), capability strings to cards, the control list's keys and OSD names unique. `dotnet test`: 250 pass. A fresh build's DLL may be blocked (0x800711C7): rebuild with `--no-incremental -p:Deterministic=false` and retry.

**Not verified (nothing could be seen or applied):** how any new card, the "Not available" group, the Presets window and the warm-light card look in both themes and at the narrowest window; whether Windows accepts the new ramps on the owner's display driver (extremes of gain, black and white level may be refused: the message then says so); whether the Magnification effect takes the invert matrix on this machine; that a real monitor's capability string parses as the fake ones do; how long the first probe takes now that the string is always read; DDC writes of the new codes (their meaning is the standard's, monitors differ); the schedule over a real night.

**Open items.** Hardware level and choice actions are now about 60 hotkey entries (every table feature has up/down): Shortcuts and the pickers are longer; a group per kind is possible if the owner finds it too long. `HalfPanel` still stacks cards full width inside a subgroup at narrow widths (see "Displays compact"). The OSD page lists the new elements by name only (a pop-up does not name its monitor).

**Owner test steps.** (1) Displays > Software: move Digital gamma, red/green/blue gain, black and white level on one display and watch the picture; double-click a slider to reset; use Reset all. If a card greys out with "Not supported by this display's driver", press Refresh/F5 to try again. (2) Grayscale and Invert switches (all displays), then the saturation slider; hotkeys under Shortcuts > Displays - Software. (3) Warm light at night: pick times a few minutes ahead, turn it on, read the state text, watch the picture warm; turn it off. (4) Presets...: Save current as "Evening", change things, Apply; Rename, Delete. (5) Displays > Hardware: each monitor's list should hold what its own menu offers; open "Not available on ..." to see the rest; change black level, gamma, sharpness, input, display mode; Refresh. (6) Quit and relaunch: the displays are back to normal, the schedule and presets remain.

## Displays: monitor presets on the Hardware header (14 Nov 2026) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 453/453 pass (433 + 20 new in `MonitorPresetTests`); nothing run, seen or written to a monitor; the running app was not stopped

**What it is.** The Hardware group's title line now has the shared `HeaderPicker` (drop-down + cogwheel) left of Refresh, the same design as the Software and Mouse headers. A monitor preset is a named set of DDC/CI values per monitor: for every monitor connected when saved, every level and choice feature in `VcpFeatures` it reports (brightness, contrast, RGB gain, black level, sharpness, volume, gamma, hue/saturation axes, input source, colour preset, display mode, mute). Never power, never reset actions, the laptop panel's brightness (Windows') is kept like any level. A level keeps its 0-100 share, a choice the monitor's raw value; an entry holds the monitor's model name and the Windows id it had.

**Apply** (picking in the drop-down, the manager's Apply, or the action): each connected monitor is paired with an entry (same name and id; else the model name when it is the only connected monitor of that name and the only unclaimed entry of it, so a monitor on another port still matches); monitors without an entry are untouched, entries of monitors that are away stay saved. Per entry only features the monitor still reports are written, levels clipped to 0-100, choices the monitor does not list skipped, values already equal skipped. The writes go through the existing `LevelStepper` (newest wanted value per key, one display call at a time), the cards show the values at once. Saving over an existing name keeps its entries of monitors that are away.

**Drop-down.** Lead entry "Current settings" (no preset saved) or "Custom"; a preset is shown when every stored value of a connected monitor equals what the cards show (at least one compared; a level also equals the share a monitor reads back after being written, so a monitor with a 0-255 range keeps matching after Refresh). When several presets match, the one last applied to a connected monitor (remembered per monitor model name in `display-settings.json`: `MonitorLastPreset`, followed by a rename, dropped by a delete) is the one named. With no monitor reporting a control (only the built-in panel, or none) the drop-down is greyed and reads "No monitor controls to save" (HeaderPicker now binds `Availability`); the cogwheel still opens the manager. The values compared are the ones OmniControl last read or set, not a fresh DDC read (a change made on the monitor's own buttons shows after Refresh).

**One piece for both groups.** `IPresetBook` is the one shape (`PicturePresetBook`, `MonitorPresetBook`); `PresetPicker` (was `DisplayPresetPicker`), `DisplayPresetsModel` and `DisplayPresetsWindow` work over it, so the manager is one window ("Monitor presets" lists each preset's monitors with a connected / not connected tag), and `PresetList<T>` holds the name rules once for both lists in `DisplaySettingsStore` (whose public picture-preset members are unchanged). Action `displays.hw.preset.next` "Next monitor preset" (Shortcuts: Displays - Hardware > Presets). Search: "Monitor presets" (monitor presets manage save apply hardware ddc). UPDATE 14 Nov: `DisplayPresetsModel` / `DisplayPresetsWindow` are gone; the books are cards of the shared preset manager through `BookPresets` (see "Unified preset manager").

**Start-up order** (the last change crashed the app by reading a field not yet made): `DisplaysPageViewModel` makes the store, then the global item, then `PicturePresetBook` and its drop-down, then `MonitorPresetBook` and its drop-down; `_hardware` is a field initialiser (empty until the displays are read), so the monitor drop-down starts greyed. A test builds the pieces in that order with no display. The page itself was not constructed in a test (it needs the monitor engine, OSD and hotkeys).

**Not verified (nothing can be seen or written here):** how the header looks at the narrowest window (count, drop-down, cogwheel, Refresh on one line); the greyed drop-down look; the manager's monitor lines; that a real monitor accepts the written values and reads back what the rounding rule expects; the writes are not paced beyond what `LevelStepper` does (one at a time).

**Open.** Two identical monitors get unique names with a suffix; if Windows changes the suffix order after a replug, entries could swap (they pair by name and id first). The manager has no keyboard list shortcuts (mouse first, as the picture presets window). A fresh DDC read before saving (instead of the cards' values) was not added: it would block the UI thread.

**Owner test steps.** (1) Displays > Hardware: set brightness and an input on a monitor, open the drop-down's cogwheel, Save current as "Work"; change values; pick "Work" in the drop-down: the cards and the monitor go back. (2) Plug in or switch off a second monitor, save a preset, remove the monitor: the manager shows it "not connected" and applying leaves it alone. (3) With only the built-in panel the drop-down is greyed. (4) Shortcuts > Displays - Hardware > Presets: bind "Next monitor preset".

## Drop-down keyboard (ComboKeys)
Every ComboBox is keyboard driven by `Pages/ComboKeys.cs` (class handler on `TopLevel`, so it also sees a pop-down's own root and runs before page/window key handlers). Cause of the old gap: keys from the open list's `ComboBoxItem` were skipped by the card routers and window Esc handlers, and `RowEditing.IsEditing` only knew the ComboBox itself, not its focused item. Left/Right on a card whose only part is a drop-down now change its value. Mouse-first pop-down design is unchanged. Not verified on screen (nothing can be pressed in this environment); owner to test the App settings 'When closing' list, the Layer drop-downs, rule editor, FX toolbar and Combine window.


## Volume page: only what works is selectable (14 Nov 2026) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 282/282 pass (263 before + 19 new in `AvailabilityTests`); nothing run, seen, clicked or heard; no Windows or audio setting changed; the running app was not stopped

**Owner's rule:** "only settings that work and are available can be selectable; the rest can be greyed out potentially with a reason". Greyed is shown, not hidden, and follows the live state. **One home for the rules and words:** `App/Audio/SoundModes.Availability.cs` (pure, tested), next to the existing Method-menu and setup-table rules; the reason is a `Gate` (reason + the mode that allows it), shown through `Pages/Volume/ControlGate`. **Live:** `EffectsEngine.PlanChanged` (new; raised when the plan's offer changes: a cable freed or taken, the master's method, Equalizer APO on a cable, uses) and `SoundModeService.Changed` re-sync every gate, menu and the setup table (`VolumePageViewModel.OnPlanChanged` / `SyncModes`), so a choice that became possible is selectable without a refresh. **Switch links:** a gate's "Switch to X" link is offered only when X itself can be chosen (`ControlGate`, one place for every gate); otherwise the reason also says "Full control is not available: needs VB-CABLE".

| # | Place | What was dishonest before | What it does now |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | "+ Combine speakers" / "+ Combine microphones" pills (Wiring group headers) | Always clickable; the window opened and Create made a device that then waited ("Needs another cable", or Fast mode for speakers) | Dimmed with the reason as tooltip (`SoundModes.CombineGate`: speakers need Full control; both need a cable that is installed, switched on and free; a microphone one apps can record from; "Needs VB-CABLE" / "Cables switched off" / "No free cable..." / the master bus holding the only pair, with "Switch to Fast" for a microphone); a "Switch to ..." link beside the pill where the other mode allows it |
| 2 | Combine devices window: kind chips and Create | Create always possible; Speakers chip dimmed only for the mode, Microphones never; the hint repeated the reason | Both kind chips dimmed with their reason (`CanChooseMicrophones`); for a NEW device the cable line says why it can't be made (with Install VB-CABLE / More cables and the Switch) and Create stays dimmed (`CombineCableLine.Blocks`); editing/saving an existing device is never blocked |
| 3 | A microphone's "Combine with..." menu | New-combined entries were selectable while no cable could be given | Those entries are dimmed with the reason, the menu's note and Switch say why; "Add to <combined microphone>" (works with no free cable) stays enabled |
| 4 | "Plays on" menu of a program: cables | Free cables were offered in Fast mode, where nothing records them | Cables are offered only in Full control (`CablesOfferedAsDevices`). Owner to confirm: a program on a free cable in Fast mode would have worked as plain routing; I followed "cables never offered" |
| 5 | "Plays on" menu: combined speakers | A combined speaker without a cable was selectable and the program then played nowhere/on the master | Dimmed with "<name> has no cable yet (Needs another cable)" (`CombinedChoiceGate`); and when such a choice is already stored (the cable was taken later) the program's row says "Needs another cable" with the sentence in its tooltip (planner) instead of silently playing on the master |
| 6 | "+ add" for the master in Fast mode | Already honest (one device, the note and Switch) | Unchanged; the Switch is now hidden when Full control is not available |
| 7 | Sound mode panel: Full control card | Selectable with no cable installed or all switched off (the mode then runs nothing) | Card words dimmed, tooltip says why, a pick does nothing (`SoundModeCard.UnavailableReason`); its status tag and buttons (Install VB-CABLE, More cables, Help) keep working. `SetSoundMode` refuses too, so the search card, the "Switch sound mode" hotkey, the setup table and every Switch link agree (one footer sentence). Fast mode stays selectable always (programs can be recorded); its Master line says "Equalizer APO is not on this device" with Choose devices, as before |
| 8 | Method menu of a program | "Virtual cable" said "no cable pair installed" even when the cables were only switched off; "Equalizer APO on a cable" said "needs a second cable pair" with no cable at all; no way to fix from the menu | Says "every cable is switched off" / "Needs VB-CABLE" / "Cables switched off" (`CableOffer.NoCable`); and the menu has one fix step under the cards: Install VB-CABLE when none is installed, Choose devices when Equalizer APO is not on a cable (`ProgramMethodFix`). Menu follows the plan live (`PlanChanged`), not only when it opens |
| 9 | Method menu of the master | Rules matched the planner (checked); a master set to Cable bus whose cable disappeared only showed the status | Row now offers "Use Equalizer APO" (`RouteChoice.UseApo`) when the bus is chosen, no cable can be given and Equalizer APO is possible on the speakers (same pattern as a program's "Use Recording") |
| 10 | Mixer rows: FX button / effects link on a row whose mode can't run them (a microphone in Fast mode without Equalizer APO) | Dimmed but its tooltip did not show (no `ShowOnDisabled`) and said nothing of the reason | Tooltip is the reason (`VolumeRow.FxTip`); combined speakers have no effects; combined microphones run in both modes, their row says what they wait for |
| 11 | A program's caution "Switch to Full control" (cannot be recorded in Fast) | Link shown even when Full control is not available | Link only when it works (`ShowsCaptureSwitch`); the tag's sentence then adds why Full control is not available |
| 12 | Mixer row's Test | Offered on a program that is open but silent; the result then said "not playing" | Dimmed with "Play sound in X first, then press Test" (`TestUnavailable`). Hidden (not dimmed) when Windows cannot record programs at all or the program is closed or has no effects: its caution tag says why (kept). Method link in Fast: hidden, confirmed (it only applies to Full control). Forget (x): only on a program that is not open, confirmed |
| 13 | Setup table rows and the Help card | Row 8 (Fast) was dimmed and unclickable when the master lacked Equalizer APO although the mode panel allows Fast; the row tooltip for a dimmed row was ok | Rows that work or Fast's own row can be clicked (`HelpWorks.CanChoose`); the other rows are dimmed, the tag and tooltip say why; rows follow the plan live. The Help card is the table |
| 14 | Wiring > Virtual cables switches | A cable in use by a run could be switched off with only a generic footer line | Tooltip says what stops while the cable is in use ("... and Spotify stops until another cable is free", "the master bus", "kept for its device"), and the footer says it after the switch (`CableSwitchTip`, `CableSwitchedOffStops`); the switch itself stays movable (the user's call) |
| 15 | Hotkeys (Shortcuts page) | "Switch sound mode" did nothing when the next mode is unavailable; "Toggle microphone FX" did nothing when the mode can't run microphone effects; the combined microphone actions did nothing with no combined microphone | `HotkeyAction.UnavailableReason` (new, set live by the Volume page): the action's words are dimmed and the reason sits under its name; pressing it says the reason in the footer instead of failing silently. The key can still be bound |
| 16 | State that changes after a choice | Programs: already offered "Use Recording"; master: only the status; combined devices: only the Cable column | Master: "Use Equalizer APO" (row 9). Combined devices: unchanged (their Wiring row says what they wait for with Install / More cables / Switch). Programs on a lost combined speaker: row 5 |

**Choices to overrule:** (a) the "+ Combine" pills are dimmed, not clickable, while a new device can't work; defining a combined device ahead of installing VB-CABLE is no longer possible (the earlier decision allowed it). (b) Full control is not selectable without an allowed cable; its card's buttons still work. (c) Cables are not offered as "plays on" devices in Fast mode.

**Skipped / not done:** the mouse button and gesture pickers do not grey an OmniControl action that does nothing now (they are built when the master list changes, so a greyed state would go stale; the action says the reason in the footer when run); a Method menu card for "Automatic" stays selectable even when neither a cable nor recording can run (the row status says what is missing); the master's "Cable bus" card does not warn that it takes the only cable from a combined device or program (the planner gives the bus the first cable); the master playing on a combined speaker with no cable still shows the generic "No speakers are plugged in" status; "Add to" a combined microphone and the program/master Fast single-device menus were left as they were because they work.

**Unverified:** nothing was seen on screen: the dimmed look of the Sound mode card words and of a Shortcuts action (`StackPanel.unavailable` added to the master `Grid.unavailable` style), the "+ Combine" pill with its Switch link at the narrowest window, the Create button dimmed with the cable line, the new fix steps under the Method cards, and every tooltip. The new sync runs on every plan change (it re-syncs all rows); not measured. Tests are pure rules only (`AvailabilityTests`: modes, combine gate, combined choice, Method wording, setup table rows, `ControlGate`, cable switch words, Test, the planner's lost combined speaker); the page's wiring is reasoned from code and a clean build. Tests builds were refused twice by Smart App Control (0x800711C7) and passed after a rebuild with `-p:Deterministic=false --no-incremental`.

## Volume page: Easy mode, phase 1 (14 Nov 2026; built to `docs/mockups/easy-mode.html`) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 303/303 pass (282 before + 21 new in `EasyModeTests`); nothing run, seen, clicked or heard; no Windows or audio setting changed; the running app was not stopped

**Hard rule kept:** Advanced is the existing page. The only edits to its files are hosting: `VolumePage.axaml` got a title row (the page's own title plus the switch; the shell's title is skipped through `ShowsOwnHeader`), `CardsHost` got `IsVisible="{Binding IsAdvanced}"` and sits beside the new `EasyHost`; `VolumePageViewModel.cs` calls `CreateEasy` at the end of its constructor and disposes `Easy`; `FeedMeters` returns while Easy is shown (nothing is drawn then). Everything else is new files (`Pages/Volume/Easy/*`, `VolumePageViewModel.Easy.cs`, `Audio/EasyEq.cs`, `Services/FirstRun.cs`).

**As built**
- **The switch:** "Easy | Advanced" are two `Button.pill`, the open one `.custom` (no new master look). The choice is `AppSettings.VolumeView` (saved at once, so it is remembered across tabs and runs, and the page is built in that view: no flash). **First run:** `FirstRun.Decide` runs when the settings are first read: if no `*.json` file existed in `%APPDATA%\OmniControl` (`AppDataStore.HasSavedFiles`) the view is Easy and the welcome is pending; otherwise Advanced and `EasyWelcomeDone = true` (an existing user who tries Easy is not welcomed). The decision is saved immediately. `AppSettings.Current` became lazy so making an `AppSettings` (tests) touches no file.
- **Easy page** (`EasyPageView`): "Everything" card (the master's own `VolumeRow`: thick slider, typeable value, double-click resets to 100, Mute, "Sound" drawer); the apps card (the Mixer's own listed programs, so open ones first and the "Show closed" setting is honoured, then the microphones (recording devices, not combined ones); each row: icon, name, state word ("playing" / "open" / "not open" / "microphone"), level, Mute, "Sound"; the Mixer's own "Add program..." pop-down at the right of the card's title); the footer (tag "Set up" / "Not set up yet", what is set up, "Set up again", "Something not working? Fix it"); a confirmation line under the title that fades after `Timing.EasyConfirmation` (the fade time is `EasyFadeTime` in `App.axaml`).
- **Sound drawer** (`EasySound`): chips Normal / Bass boost / Vocal / Night / Loud / My own, the FX window's response thumbnail (`Fx/ResponseThumb`, its own maths: `ChainResponse`), five sliders (+-12 dB, whole dB, double-click 0, typeable, wheel and keys from the shared handlers). **One definition** (`EasyEq`): Bass = low shelf 100 Hz, Low mid = bell 300 Hz, Mid = bell 1 kHz, High mid = bell 3 kHz, Treble = high shelf 8 kHz. The chips are `Eq.PresetShapes` sampled at those five frequencies and rounded to a dB ("Loud" = "Loudness"). It edits the row's **first Parametric equaliser** in the same chain the FX window edits (made first in the chain, with the five bands, when there is none), saves through `MixerService.SaveFx` and switches the row's effects on for a non-flat sound; a flat sound with nothing else in the chain switches them off again. Advanced shows the same bands. If that equaliser was shaped in the FX window the sliders are greyed (see "Easy Sound drawer follows the real effect"); if the effects are off the drawer says so.
- **Honesty:** the drawer greys itself and says why in one line (with the same fix button Advanced offers: Choose devices / Install Equalizer APO) when the row's own state says the sound cannot be applied: `EffectsGate` (mode cannot run microphone effects), `CaptureGate` (a program that cannot be recorded in Fast mode), or the row's `Fix`; under it the row's short engine status (tooltip: its long line). "More controls..." (and the curve) open the row's FX window.
- **Welcome and Sound screen:** the six cards come from `EasyGoals.All` (the one list: id, title, fact, picture parts; add a goal there and it appears). Card 1 opens a **real Sound screen** (the drawer on the master, live, "Back" to the cards, "Done" to the Easy page with "Your sound is on: Bass boost."); "Just the volume" and "Skip" go to the Easy page; the other three go to the Easy page with the footer note "Guided setup for this goal is coming soon." "Set up again" reopens the cards; "on" is shown only on card 1 (master sound not flat and its effects on); the others are not tracked yet.
- **Fix it:** looks at the master, programs and microphones in that order; the first row with a setup step missing has that same step started (its `Fix`), and a calm sentence says which; else the first row that cannot apply its sound is named with its reason; else "Nothing OmniControl can check is wrong...".
- **Hotkeys:** "Switch Volume view (Easy / Advanced)" (`volume.view.toggle`) and "Next sound preset for everything" (`volume.easy.sound.next`), on the Shortcuts page like every action.

**Differences from the spec / skipped**
- **Built afterwards, in phase 2 (next section; this paragraph is how phase 1 ended):** the guided setup of cards 2 to 5 (their questions, checklists, "Choose my speakers", "Use another way", install steps), the "Goal / Sound / Setup / Done" progress strip, and card 1's "Setting it up" checklist and "All set" screens. Card 1's Sound screen applies **live** (the global rule "values change live"), not "nothing saved until the end"; "Back" leaves what was chosen in place.
- The picture parts use the master `Border.tag` (green = Equalizer APO, accent = OmniControl's engine); **the cable is a plain tag, not blue** (there is no blue tag in the masters).
- "Fix it" may start an installer or Device Selector through the existing step: it asks as that step does. It does no checks of its own beyond reading each row's state.
- The microphone list shows recording devices only (combined microphones, cables and wiring stay in Advanced). Meters are not drawn in Easy. The "Show closed" switch itself is only in Advanced (Easy honours it).
- **Keyboard:** buttons carry `Tag="grouptool"`, so Up/Down walk them (a second `GroupedCards.Attach` on `EasyHost`); sliders are reached with Tab or the mouse, then Left/Right/Up/Down change them (a slider is not a stop of the Up/Down walk). The switch buttons are reached with Tab (they sit outside both hosts so Advanced's own Up/Down walk is unchanged) or the hotkey above.
- **Layering exception, open item:** `Pages/Volume/Easy` draws `Fx/ResponseThumb` and uses `Fx/FxAnalysisViewModel` (a Pages to Fx reference, as the FX window is a feature module). Moving the response drawing to a neutral place is a later refactor.

**Owner test steps (nothing here was seen on screen)**
1. On this PC the first-run decision lands on Advanced (settings exist), so the page opens as before. Press "Easy": the Easy page opens (no welcome: `EasyWelcomeDone` is true). Press "Advanced": the old page is back. Leave the app and reopen: it opens in the view you left.
2. To see the first launch: close the app, rename the folder `%APPDATA%\OmniControl` (keep it as a backup), start the app: it opens in Easy on the six cards. Skip, and the cards must not come back until "Set up again". Put the folder back afterwards.
3. In Easy: move the Everything slider and Mute, then press Advanced: the Master row shows the same level and mute. Press "Sound": choose Bass boost; the five sliders, the curve and the chip follow; the master's FX window shows a Parametric equaliser with five bands. Double-click a slider: 0 dB. Choose Normal: with nothing else in the chain the master's effects switch off.
4. Same on a program row and the microphone row ("Sound"); a closed program with "Show closed" off (in Advanced) is not listed. Add a program with "Add program...".
5. With Equalizer APO missing, or the speakers not ticked in its Device Selector: the master's drawer is greyed with one line and its fix button.
6. The footer: "Set up again" shows the cards (card 1 with a check mark and "on" once a sound is set); each card now opens its guided setup (phase 2, below).


## Volume page: Easy mode, phase 2 - the guided setups (14 Nov 2026; built to `docs/mockups/easy-mode.html` sections 3 and 4) - compiled (Release throwaway build, 0 warnings, 0 errors, folder deleted) and `dotnet test` 339/339 pass (302 + 37 new in `EasyFlowTests`); nothing run, seen, clicked or heard; no Windows or audio setting changed; the running app was not stopped; no installer, Device Selector or mode switch was ever called (tests use fakes)

**Hard rule kept:** no Advanced file was edited. All new code is under `Pages/Volume/Easy/Flow/` and `Audio/EasyApply.cs`; the only existing files touched are Easy's own (`EasyViewModel`, `EasyView`, `EasyWelcomeView`, `EasyPageView`, `EasyGoals`, `GoalCard`, `EasySound`, `Audio/EasyEq.cs`) and `VolumePageViewModel.Easy.cs` (a partial of the page, which can reach the page's private cable rules and setup steps without changing them).

**As built**
- **One flow engine, one definition per goal** (`EasyFlows.All`): screens (questions, Setup, Done), a checklist rule, the "All set" words, optional extra lines. `EasyFlowViewModel` runs any of them: progress strip "Goal · questions · Setup · Done" that ticks, Back / Next (the first Back returns to the six cards, nothing applied), and the checklist **re-evaluated on every change** (the page's `Refresh`, which already listens to the mixer, the modes, the engine's status and plan), so a line ticks by itself, for example "Your speakers are selected" once Device Selector has been closed and `SoundModeService` looked again. Next on Setup is enabled only when every non-optional line is Done.
- **Checks are pure** (`EasyChecks`: `(EasyAnswers, EasyFacts)` -> lines with state Done / Todo / Blocked, subtitle, optional fix). `EasyEnvironment` makes the facts from the existing services and rules only (`SoundModes.ApoMissingOn` / `MicApoMissing` / `DeviceSelectorFixes` / `ModeUnavailable` / `NoCableKind` / `MasterOfferOf`, `SoundModeService.ProgramCaptureCaution`, `EffectsEngine.MasterMethod`, the page's own `FreeCableFor` / `BusHeldCable` / `BusCableName`). A fix runs only from its button; it is looked at again first (done or blocked meanwhile = not run), asks its `EasyConfirm` (the existing message window) when it changes a mode or a method, and one calm sentence says how it went. Installer, Device Selector and VB-CABLE steps are the very methods Advanced's buttons run (`InstallEqualizerApoAsync`, `ChooseDevicesCommand`, `RunSetupAsync`), so their messages and failure window are the same.
- **Card 1:** Sound screen (the phase 1 drawer on the master, now never greyed so it works before the setup is done; it applies live as before), then Setup: "Equalizer APO is installed" (fix: its installer), "Your speakers are selected in Equalizer APO" (fix: Device Selector), "Apply your sound" (fix: writes the chosen sound back and switches the master's effects on; blocked until the lines above are done; Normal needs nothing). When the master runs on the cable bus (Full control) the Equalizer APO lines are replaced by one done line, "the bus does it". "Use another way" (optional line) appears in Fast mode only while Equalizer APO is not ready and Full control can be chosen (an allowed cable exists); its button asks first and says what changes. All set: "Everything you play now sounds like <preset>..." with "Set up something else" / "Go to Volume".
- **Card 2:** "Which apps?" (ticked list of the Mixer's own programs, playing ones marked, the Mixer's "Add program" pop-down beside it, at least one), Setup with one small picture per ticked app (the goal's picture parts with the app's name), "Effects work for these apps" (Fast: `ProgramCaptureCaution`; Full: process loopback or an allowed cable) and "Give each app its own sound" (flat Parametric equaliser added or the app's own kept, switched on, route set to Automatic). "Go to Volume" opens each ticked app's Sound drawer once.
- **Card 3:** microphone (recording devices, no cables), look chips from `EasyEq.MicPresets` (Normal, Clear voice, Warm, Reduce rumble: five-slider gains, wording "an equaliser cleanup", no noise-removal claim), Setup: in Full control a first line "Microphone effects run in Fast mode" (asks, then switches), "Equalizer APO is installed", "Your microphone is selected in Device Selector" (the same coverage check for the capture device; fix opens Device Selector), "Apply" (puts the look on the microphone's chain through the same input key as Advanced).
- **Card 4:** two or more microphones ticked and a name (default free "Combined microphone"), Setup: "A virtual cable is available" (free cable named; else VB-CABLE install, "More cables", or "Free the cable" = master method Equalizer APO, only when `MasterOfferOf` allows it, after a question) and "Create the combined microphone" (`AddCombined`). All set names the real cable output ("pick CABLE Output as the microphone").
- **Card 5:** two or more real speakers (with one, the screen says so and Next stays dimmed), name (default "Both speakers", numbered when taken), Setup: "Full control is on" (switch after a question, or why it cannot), the cable line, "Create the combined speaker". All set: apps have it in their Plays on list; an extra line "Everything plays on both speakers" with "Set my master to play on both" (asks first; only when the master is on the bus, otherwise a sentence says to pick it in an app's Plays on list).
- **"Set up again" marks (✓ on):** card 1 master sound not flat and effects on; card 2 any program with its own equaliser on (flat counts, since the setup makes it flat); card 3 any microphone with a non-flat equaliser on; card 4 any combined microphone; card 5 any combined speaker.
- The phase 1 "coming soon" footer note and the separate Sound screen are gone (the Sound screen is the first question of card 1).

**Differences from the spec / skipped**
- Card 1's Sound screen still applies live (the global rule), not "nothing saved until the end". Going Back from it leaves the chosen sound in place.
- "Use another way" switches to Full control and, if the master's method was set to Equalizer APO, back to Automatic so the cable bus is what runs (the confirm text says Full control, not the method).
- Card 2 "+ Add another app" is the Mixer's own "Add program" button (its words), and a newly added app is not ticked for you.
- Card 3 offers no noise suppression (no effect exists for it). In Full control it asks to go back to Fast mode rather than routing the microphone through a cable.
- Card 2's "Effects work" line reads the mode checks only (it does not run the recording Test); the Test needs a playing app and stays in Advanced.
- No new hotkeys: a guided setup is a screen, not a repeatable action.
- A failed step: installer failures keep Advanced's own failure window; other failures show one sentence under the checklist.
- The progress strip is small text with a tick, not a custom control.

**Open items**
- The cable checks reuse the page's private `FreeCableFor` / `BusHeldCable` / `BusCableName` through delegates; moving those rules into a service would let `EasyEnvironment` ask them directly.
- `Pages/Volume/Easy` still draws `Fx/ResponseThumb` (phase 1 open item).
- A microphone's "Normal" look switches its chain off only when nothing else in it is on (the same rule as the Sound drawer).

**Unverified (nothing was seen on screen):** every new screen's layout at the narrowest window and in both themes (strip wrapping, checklist lines with a long subtitle and a fix button, the ticked list at `OptionListMaxHeight`, look chips, name box), whether lines tick quickly enough after Device Selector closes (it depends on `SoundModeService`'s re-look and Windows' registry), the confirm windows' wording, and that the Mixer's Add program pop-down behaves inside the flow. The XAML has no namespace prefix inside a binding (the grep found none), but bindings are resolved only at run time: a bad one would show when the flow opens. Keyboard: buttons carry `Tag="grouptool"`; the ticked list uses `OptionList`'s own keys.

**Owner test steps (the real installers, Device Selector and mode switches run only when YOU press their buttons)**
1. Easy, "Set up again", card 1: pick Bass boost, Next. With everything ready the lines are already ticked and Next goes to "All set". With Equalizer APO's Device Selector missing your speakers: "Your speakers are not selected yet" with "Choose my speakers"; close Device Selector and watch the line tick (a restart may be needed).
2. Same card on a PC without Equalizer APO: "Install it", and the optional "Use another way" (asks before switching to Full control).
3. Card 2: tick an app, Next: its picture and two lines; press "Give each app its own sound"; "Go to Volume": its row shows the Sound drawer open.
4. Card 3: choose the microphone and a look; the checklist; Apply; then the microphone's Sound button shows the look.
5. Cards 4 and 5 with VB-CABLE installed: names, the cable line, Create; All set; the Wiring tab in Advanced shows the new device. Card 5 with the master on the bus: "Set my master to play on both".
6. Back from every screen changes nothing; "Set up again" shows "✓ on" on the cards that apply.
